**Scope (intent statement)**: a scheduler that **populates the blockchain with miners over time** and **defines the rate of automated transactions**, so the simulated network reproduces the historical behavior of the real Bitcoin network as far as the data allows. This is the systematization of what today is hardcoded: 4 fixed miner bots (`bot_1..4`), the referral-auction drip for non-miners (`NonMinerIntroIntervalMs` = one every ~2 in-game days), and per-block coin-recirculation probability (`BotSendProbabilityPerBlock = 0.5`). Instead of constants, these should follow historical curves.

**Companion docs**: `AIHelperFiles/step13-btc-market-data-and-dev-alt-timeline-plan.md` §1 (the price-dataset acquisition — the template this round follows) · `AIHelperFiles/scheduled-bot-transactions-plan.md` (current bot tx behavior) · `AIHelperFiles/btc-pools-hardware-plan.md` + `Documentation/ProjectDesignManual.md` Ch. 26 (difficulty regulator — the consumer of "network power") · Ch. 27 (hardware credits/pools) · CLAUDE.md "Open Design Questions" (the Network Fee Market Simulation research note — §2.1's fee metrics are its Option A input, a free synergy of this step).

## 0.5 Decisions locked (rounds 1–2 — 2026-07-07)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-14.1** | **Hybrid fidelity (OQ-14.1-c)**: a visible bot-miner cast that grows over time (population replay) + an invisible aggregate mass carrying the rest of the network's power. The population scale is a **new, dedicated fractal factor** — it may be (and is, see §3.1) even more reduced than /100, but **once decided it is frozen** and applied consistently. Anchoring principle (user): fix the scale at the historical maximum and derive every other point relative to that maximum — realized as the log-decade model in §3.1 (P-14.A). |
| **D-14.2** | **Transaction realization is split by role, real sends only, NO synthetic filler.** Miners: their natural BTC sell-flow — re-pace the existing miner→non-miner donation system to the historical curve (miners selling BTC is the natural outflow). Non-miners: a NEW exchange scheduler moving BTC among active non-miner holders, paced from the dataset as far as real balances allow. Under-shooting the historical rate when balances can't sustain it is accepted; fabricating economy is not. |
| **D-14.3** | Cutoff **2025-12-31** with the same freeze semantics as the price dataset (D-13.5); extending the series is **deferred until after Basic Mode v0.1**. |
| **D-14.4** | Hashrate → power conversion is **normalized, era-agnostic**: "total power at player start = the bootstrap's ~6 participants," relative growth from there (log-domain compression, §3.1). The per-era hardware table (CPU/GPU/FPGA/ASIC) is recorded as a **pending proposal for after Basic Mode v0.1** (§9.1). |
| **D-14.5** | **Pool history is out of scope** — the casino pool keeps its current always-available behavior; a future player-created pool will also ignore pool history. Other/competing pools may later follow the historical rhythm on the fractal base — future plan (§9.2). |
| **D-14.6** | **Coin Metrics (Option A of §2) locked as the primary source; fee columns fetched NOW** (zero marginal cost). **Fees join `price_usd` in the fractal exemption** (user decree): fee values are never /100-scaled — they replay at face value, which is what makes the future fee-replay trivial. **The Network Fee Market question (CLAUDE.md / PRIVATE_ROADMAP §5) is direction-locked: Option A (historical fee replay) is the chosen mechanism; Option B (reactive fee market from our own simulated congestion) is retained as a FUTURE validation experiment** — if a reactive market later reproduces a curve similar to the replayed one, that confirms the population/volume simulation of this very plan was built right. (Supersedes the roadmap's pre-decision working hypothesis, which leaned B-first.) |
| **D-14.7** | **Time-shiftable from day one**: every lookup keys off the game clock's local date (which any `TimelineConfig` offset already shifts — the `BtcMarketDataService` precedent); every calendar anchor the scheduler needs routes through `TimelineConfig.Shift()`; scheduler targets are pure `date → target` functions so a future alt-timeline bootstrap can fast-build population history by calling the same functions (ProjectDesignManual Ch. 35 interplay). |
| **D-14.8** | *(round 2)* **The player-share anchor — replaces the free `GrowthFactor` knob**: total network power is sized so that **a player wielding the era-standard resources always holds one visible-cast member's share** — at the historical maximum, `1/28 ≈ 3.6%` of the base block distribution, in every era. Maximal, intelligent use of resources beats the baseline somewhat, never by much; careless resource use falls below it easily. "Pools become mandatory late-game" confirmed as the intended pressure — pool adaptation/competition and any mining-power rebalancing are **deferred, to be designed ON TOP of this step** (§9.6). Realization: §3.1's derived `TotalNetworkUnits` (era-standard power × cast size); the ~28-at-max cast (`CastPerDecade = 2`) is locked by this same arithmetic. |
| **D-14.9** | *(round 2)* Invisible-mass blocks are attributed to **rotating ghost miner names** (small curated pseudonym pool, one-off addresses) — assistant recommendation accepted. |
| **D-14.10** | *(round 2)* **The historical early-era quietness is accepted** — no gameplay floor for automated txs; the founders' scripted events are the early flavor. |
| **D-14.11** | *(round 2)* Invisible-mass BTC is **frozen forever in v1** (retired-Satoshi precedent); recirculation reconsidered only if ND.4 calibration shows the visible economy BTC-starved. |
| **D-14.12** | *(round 2)* Visible cast is **spawn-only in v1**; churn/retirement (the GPU-extinction realism) deferred (§9.5). |

## 1. What data the scheduler actually needs (metric → design purpose)

The sim is a **1:100 fractal replica** (D-13.10 precedent: raw real-world data in the asset, `/100` scaling at the service boundary, prices exempt). Whatever source we pick must supply, per **day**, from **2009-01-03** onward:

| # | Metric (daily) | What it drives in-game | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | **Transaction count** (`TxCnt`) | The automated-transaction rate: how many bot/filler txs per day the network should produce (÷100) | The single most important series. Real blocks carry ~2,000–3,000 txs today; our cap is 24/block — the /100 rule already fits (step13 plan §1.6) |
| M2 | **Hashrate** (or difficulty) | Total network mining power over time → how many "participant power units" the bot population must sum to (the difficulty regulator's `anchor = InitialDifficulty × power` already consumes exactly this shape via `SimulationService.SetActiveMiningPower`) | Hashrate must be translated into *participants' worth of power* (like `FoundersMiningService`'s `P = 1.0` unit) using era-typical hardware — that conversion is round-1 design work, but the raw series is the input |
| M3 | **Miner / pool attribution** | How many *distinct* miners exist and how concentrated they are → bot-miner population count, pool era onset (our casino pool ≈ Slush pool, born Dec 2010) | No API gives "number of miners" directly; per-block attribution (coinbase tags) is the measurable proxy, only meaningful from ~2011 (pool era). The 2009–2010 era needs research-based estimates (§2.5) |
| M4 | **Active addresses** (`AdrActCnt`) | Non-miner user population → pace of the referral-auction drip / holder bots | Optional but cheap if the source bundles it |
| M5 | **Block count per day** | Cross-check series (our block pace is fixed by the difficulty regulator; useful for validation only) | Free from any of the sources |
| M6 | **Fees (mean/median per tx, native units)** | NOT this plan's deliverable — but fetching it in the same pipeline feeds the flagged "Network Fee Market Simulation" research (its Option A = historical fee replay) for free | Decide in round 1 whether to include the column now (§4 OQ-14.6) |

---

## 2. Options for obtaining the historical data

All four candidates below were **empirically verified alive on 2026-07-07** (actual API responses inspected, not assumed) — the same discipline as the Step-13 round, where CoinGecko turned out to be paywalled and bitcoincharts dead (§2.6).

### 2.1 Option A — Coin Metrics Community API ⭐ (recommended primary)

- **What**: free, no-key REST API with curated daily metrics for BTC from genesis. One endpoint covers M1, M2, M4, M5, M6 in a single request family:
  `https://community-api.coinmetrics.io/v4/timeseries/asset-metrics?assets=btc&metrics=TxCnt,HashRate,DiffMean,AdrActCnt,BlkCnt,FeeMeanNtv,FeeMedNtv&frequency=1d&start_time=2009-01-03`
- **Verified (2026-07-07)**: returns valid JSON from `2009-01-03T00:00:00Z`; early days show `HashRate: null → 0.00000094…` and `TxCnt: 0`, exactly the expected newborn-network shape.
- **Pros**: everything daily in one place, genesis-complete, clean nulls (no fake zeros — matches our "blank ≠ 0" parsing rule), JSON/CSV output, documented metric methodology (it's the de-facto academic standard), trivially reproducible pipeline (one script, no scraping).
- **Cons / caveats**: response is **paged** (`page_size` max 10,000; ~6,400 days fits in 1–2 pages, follow `next_page_url`); community tier is rate-limited (fine for a one-shot build); **no miner attribution** (M3 must come from elsewhere); community metrics list should be re-checked at implementation time (paid-tier metrics occasionally rotate).

### 2.2 Option B — blockchain.com Charts API (cross-check / fallback)

- **What**: the veteran free charts API: `https://api.blockchain.info/charts/{chart}?timespan=all&sampled=false&format=json` with charts `n-transactions`, `hash-rate`, `difficulty`, `n-unique-addresses`, `transaction-fees`, `miners-revenue`, `avg-block-size`.
- **Verified (2026-07-07)**: `n-transactions?timespan=4weeks` returns valid `{x: unixTs, y: value}` series (status "ok").
- **Pros**: no key, dead-simple schema, series reach back to 2009; independent methodology → ideal **cross-check** against Option A (the step13 pipeline's "assert the join" habit, applied across providers).
- **Cons / caveats**: `timespan=all&sampled=false` must be confirmed to really return every day, unsampled (the default response is downsampled — flag for implementation); methodology is opaque (no docs on how e.g. hash-rate is derived); no miner attribution; the API has historically changed shape without notice.

### 2.3 Option C — mempool.space mining APIs (pool-era miner attribution)

- **What**: free REST APIs from the mempool.space open-source project: `https://mempool.space/api/v1/mining/hashrate/all` (network hashrate + difficulty history) and the `mining/pools/…` family (pool dominance / blocks-per-pool over time windows).
- **Verified (2026-07-07)**: `hashrate/all` returns `{timestamp, avgHashrate}` pairs starting **1231006505 = 2009-01-03** (genesis day) — full-history, genesis-complete.
- **Pros**: the only *API* candidate with **pool attribution** (M3) built in — pool share history is exactly "how many distinct big miners and how concentrated" for the pool era (2011+); hashrate series doubles as an Option-A cross-check; open-source (methodology inspectable).
- **Cons / caveats**: pool endpoints are organized around time windows (24h/3d/1w/…/all) rather than "per historical day" — extracting a *daily pool-count time series* may require the per-pool blocks endpoints or accepting coarser granularity (verify at implementation); pre-pool era (2009–2010) attribution is structurally impossible here (nobody tagged coinbases yet).

### 2.4 Option D — Blockchair daily block dumps (per-block granularity, heavyweight)

- **What**: free daily TSV dumps, one file per calendar day, every block that day with per-block fields **including `guessed_miner`** and `transaction_count`: `https://gz.blockchair.com/bitcoin/blocks/blockchair_bitcoin_blocks_YYYYMMDD.tsv.gz`.
- **Verified (2026-07-07)**: directory listing is live and free, files from **20090103** onward (467 bytes early, growing later).
- **Pros**: the *richest* option — per-block miner attribution means we can compute "distinct miners per day/week" ourselves (M3) for the whole chain, plus exact per-block tx counts; TSV is trivially aggregated to our daily CSV; this is the same "download the primary dump, aggregate locally" shape as the Mt. Gox leg of the price pipeline.
- **Cons / caveats**: ~6,400 file downloads (one per day) — a long, throttle-sensitive crawl (Blockchair rate-limits bulk access aggressively and asks bulk users for sponsorship; a polite, cached, resumable script is mandatory — same `%TEMP%` cache pattern as the Binance kline fetcher); `guessed_miner` is only as good as coinbase-tag heuristics (≈"Unknown" for most of 2009–2010, decent from 2011).

### 2.5 The 2009–2010 miner-count gap — research sources, not APIs (any option needs this)

No API can say how many *people* mined in 2009 — coinbases weren't tagged. If round 1 decides the scheduler needs an early-era miner-count curve (not just hashrate), it will be a small **hand-curated table** (the "13 halt days" precedent — deliberately curated, documented, never interpolated silently), built from published research: Sergio Demián Lerner's Patoshi work (attributes ~22k early blocks to one miner — we already model that as Satoshi's regulated ~10% share), the known early-miner lore (Hal's block 78, etc. — already embodied by our founders), and hashrate÷(era-typical hardware) estimates (CPU ~2–10 MH/s in 2009, GPU ~100–600 MH/s from mid-2010). Note the sim **already covers 2009 structurally**: the historical bootstrap + founders + 4 bots ARE the early network; the scheduler's real work starts where growth takes off (2010+), which is exactly where data quality improves.

### 2.6 Dead / rejected sources (carried over from Step 13 — do not re-litigate)

- **bitcoincharts.com** — dead (verified 2026-07-04; DNS resolves, nothing listens). Its Mt. Gox dump was recovered via the Wayback Machine for the price dataset; it has nothing for network metrics anyway.
- **CoinGecko** — free API returns error `10012` beyond the last 365 days (verified 2026-07-05); paid tiers still lack the network metrics we need. Rejected.

### 2.7 Recommendation — ✅ CONFIRMED as D-14.6 (round 1)

**Primary: Option A (Coin Metrics)** for the daily M1/M2/M4/M5/M6 backbone — one reproducible script, genesis-complete, clean nulls, **fee columns included**. **Option B (blockchain.com)** as the independent cross-check asserted during the build step. **M3 (miner attribution) turned out NOT to be needed**: D-14.1's hybrid drives the visible cast from the hashrate curve (§3.1), so Options C/D stay documented as fallbacks only — no pool/attribution fetch in this step (consistent with D-14.5 pool-history-out-of-scope). Early era per §2.5 only if round-2 calibration proves the hashrate-derived cast curve inadequate for 2009–2010 (not expected — the bootstrap already covers that era).

Proposed asset shape (mirroring `Data/HistoricalPrices/`): **`Data/HistoricalNetwork/btc_network_daily_2009_2025.csv`**, schema ≈ `date,tx_count,hashrate,difficulty,active_addresses,block_count,fee_mean_btc,source` — raw real-world values (CSV stays raw; `/100` and any hashrate→power-units conversion happen at the service boundary, D-13.10 precedent), `InvariantCulture`, blank = null never 0, construction scripts kept out of the repo (`C:\Users\PERSONAL\Desktop\Proof of Fun\Project out of repo data\`) with the same "assert the join / assert continuity, refuse to write otherwise" discipline as `Merge-BtcDailyHistory.ps1`.

## 3. The design

### 3.0 The engine insight everything rests on: shares matter, magnitude doesn't

Raw hashrate replay is neither executable nor meaningful in our engine. Real hashrate grows **~12 orders of magnitude** between player start (Mar 2009) and 2025 — bots physically perform nonce attempts (1 bet = 1 attempt), so nobody can "be" 10¹² participants; and the difficulty regulator (Ch. 26) **holds block pace at ~58,500 in-game s regardless of absolute power** (the LWMA feedback trims whatever the feed-forward anchor says). In-engine, absolute network power only manifests as (i) the difficulty number itself and (ii) **each miner's SHARE of blocks**. So the scheduler replays the network's **shape** — who exists, and what fraction of blocks each stratum wins — never its raw magnitude. This is also why D-14.4's "normalize to the bootstrap's ~6 participants" is the only coherent reading, and why the log-domain compression below is a legitimate *second* fractal reduction (explicitly allowed by D-14.1: a scale "even more reduced than /100," frozen once chosen).

### 3.1 P-14.A — the log-decade growth model (population + power, one curve)

Both hybrid layers are driven by one era-agnostic quantity derived from the dataset's hashrate series:

```
decades(date) = log10( H(date) / H(playerStartDate) )        // 0 at player start, ≈12 by 2025
```

- **Visible cast (population layer)** — real bot miners with real wallets:
  `targetVisibleMiners(date) = BaseCast + round(CastPerDecade × decades(date))`, `BaseCast = 4` (today's `bot_1..4`).
  New miners are introduced the way the referral auction already introduces non-miners (block-relative drip, `NetworkRoot` node registration + `BotWalletRegistry` identity), whenever the live count is below target; **spawn-only in v1** (D-14.12). **Choosing `CastPerDecade` IS choosing the user's "scale anchored at the historical maximum"**: max cast = `BaseCast + CastPerDecade × decades(2025) ≈ 4 + 12×k`. **`CastPerDecade = 2` ⇒ ~28 visible miners by 2025 — LOCKED by D-14.8's own arithmetic** (the 1/28 anchor presumes it; Block Explorer stays a readable cast, not a crowd).
- **Invisible mass (power layer)** — ONE aggregate pseudo-miner ("rest of the network"), built exactly like the founders (`FoundersMiningService` pattern: a pure power number fed to the difficulty regulator + nonce attempts drained in lockstep with player betting — a **concurrent miner, never a clock mover**). Its size is **derived from the D-14.8 player-share anchor, not free-knobbed** (the earlier draft's `GrowthFactor` is gone):

  ```
  EraStandardPower(date)  = MaxHardwareCredits ^ ( decades(date) / decades(datasetEnd) )
                            // 1 credit at player start → 100 (the Ch. 27 hardware cap) at the historical max
  TotalNetworkUnits(date) = EraStandardPower(date) × targetVisibleMiners(date)
  InvisibleUnits(date)    = TotalNetworkUnits − Σ(actual live powers: player + founders + visible cast + casino pool)
  ```

  Anchors check out at both ends: player start ⇒ `1 × ~6 ≈ 6` units (D-14.4 ✓); historical max ⇒ `100 × 28 = 2,800` units, so a player wielding the full 100-credit cap holds exactly `1/28 ≈ 3.6%` (D-14.8 ✓). Mid-era the same holds relatively: a player at the *era-standard* power holds one cast share; a player ahead of era hardware beats the baseline boundedly; under-used credits fall below it fast — exactly the intended pressure gradient.
  **Honest interplay note (P5)**: the "not much more than baseline" ceiling is enforced from the player's side by *era-gated hardware availability*, which is P5's job, not this scheduler's — until P5 ships, a player who hoards the full 100 credits in an early era can exceed the era baseline substantially. Accepted interim behavior; the network side (this plan) stays correct either way, and D-14.8 explicitly defers rebalancing to the later pool/power design (§9.6).
- Its mined blocks are handled as external blocks (founder precedent); coinbases go to **rotating ghost miner names** — a small curated pseudonym pool with one-off addresses, BTC frozen forever (D-14.9 + D-14.11). DEV surfacing: `user://logs/network_population_trace.csv` (one row per day-change/spawn/knob change — `founders_trace.csv` precedent); `CastPerDecade` stays dev-inspectable but is design-locked.

### 3.2 P-14.B — transaction pacing by FULLNESS PARITY (the /100 × /100 trap)

D-13.10's `/100` **must not** be applied to daily tx counts directly: our blocks-per-day is *already* /100 of real (2,100-block halvings ⇒ ~1.477 blocks/in-game-day vs. real ~144), so /100 on the daily count would overflow chain capacity by exactly 100×. The correct fractal target collapses both factors into one clean rule:

```
targetTxPerBlock(date) = ( realDailyTx(date) / realDailyBlocks(date) ) / 100     // real txs-per-block, /100
                          clamped to [0, 23]                                     // block cap minus coinbase
```

Sanity checks: 2009–2010 (real ~1–2 txs/block) ⇒ ~0 automated txs — historically faithful near-empty blocks; 2017+ (real ~2,000–4,000/block) ⇒ 20–40 ⇒ **clamps at 23 = the real "full blocks" era reproduced by the cap itself**. The early-era quietness (which *reduces* today's bot liveliness) is **accepted — D-14.10**: no gameplay floor; the founders' scripted events carry the early flavor.

**Realization (D-14.2, real sends only):** each mined block, the scheduler computes the target and tops up **automated** traffic toward it, never competing with organic traffic — player/casino/founder/swap txs count toward fullness first, automated senders fill only remaining capacity:
1. **Miner sell-flow** (existing system, re-paced): the miner→non-miner donation engine (`scheduled-bot-transactions`, warmup + 10–40% sends) replaces its flat `BotSendProbabilityPerBlock = 0.5` with a probability derived from `targetTxPerBlock`.
2. **Non-miner exchange scheduler** (new): fills the remainder by scheduling real UTXO sends **between active non-miner holders** (recipient/amount rules reuse the existing Min/MaxSendFraction shape; fees attach post-activation as everywhere). If balances can't sustain the target, it under-shoots — accepted (no filler); recirculation makes later targets more sustainable.

### 3.3 Dataset + services + scheduler (the concrete pieces)

- **Asset**: `Data/HistoricalNetwork/btc_network_daily_2009_2025.csv` — schema `date,tx_count,hashrate,difficulty,active_addresses,block_count,fee_mean_btc,fee_median_btc,source` (`source = coinmetrics`), raw real-world values, `InvariantCulture`, blank = null never 0, continuous-dates assertion. Built by one out-of-repo PS script (Coin Metrics, paged) + a second script cross-checking tx_count/hashrate against blockchain.com within tolerance (§2.2) — the "assert or refuse to write" discipline of `Merge-BtcDailyHistory.ps1`.
- **Loader**: new autoload **`BtcNetworkDataService`** (#17), mirroring `BtcMarketDataService` exactly (load once in `_Ready`, day-indexed array, O(1) lookups, day-change event; read-only over a static asset ⇒ no persistence, no checkpoint coverage, **no reset-list entry needed** — but re-ask CLAUDE.md Pattern 2's three questions at implementation for anything else this step persists). Raw fields DEV-only; gameplay consumes derived accessors: `GetDecades(date)`, `GetTargetVisibleMiners(date)`, `GetTotalNetworkUnits(date)`, `GetTargetTxPerBlock(date)` — all scaling at the service boundary (D-13.10 precedent), all pure `date → value` (D-14.7).
- **Scheduler**: **`NetworkPopulationScheduler`**, a pure static controller (`FoundersMiningService`/`HistoricalEventScheduler` pattern — no Godot/chain state of its own; chain-derived idempotent decisions), driven by `SimulationService`: per-frame → drain invisible-mass attempts; per-block → tx top-up scheduling; on day-change → population target check/spawn. Nothing persisted (block = the only commit; spawned bots' *identities* land in `BotWalletRegistry`, which is deliberately reset-spared like all identity files).
- **Fees**: columns ship in this dataset (D-14.6); making `NetworkFeePolicy` replay them is **its own future step** — this plan only guarantees the data is on disk and fractal-exempt when that step comes.

This sidesteps the autoload-ordering problem entirely (the bootstrap runs from `CalendarTimeService._Ready()`, autoload #3 — long before `BtcNetworkDataService`, autoload #17, would normally initialize) with **zero project.godot reordering** and no risk to already-shipped live-play code paths. The one real code change this required: `BtcNetworkDataService._Ready()`'s load logic was extracted into a public, idempotent `EnsureLoaded()` so a throwaway instance can trigger it explicitly.

The real EB.2 schedule itself is date-derived and entirely unaffected. Not worth an extra confirmation block to close.

Separately, the ghost pool's fixed round-robin kept all 12 pseudonyms permanently TIED in blocks mined — a perfectly flat leaderboard that reads synthetic.
  - [x] **Sell-flow split into two independent rotation cycles** (developer decision, 2026-07-10): **casino-bots** — new `NetworkRoot.TryCasinoBotDonation` rotates ONLY among `bot_1..4` (random start offset, first eligible sends, one send max per hit) on its own `CasinoBotSellFlowMeanBlocks = 100` cadence (geometric draw per live block — "~1 tx per 100 blocks", the rate the developer confirmed), **independent of the historical fullness-parity budget** — the bot bid stream becomes era-stable (~1.5 bids per 100-day auction window, D-EB.9) and this IS the "auction-bid exemption from the budget" knob ND.3 flagged under D-14.10, made real. **Cast miners** — `TryCastSellFlow` (replaces `TryMinerSellFlow`) keeps the historical budget but rotates the cast with a random start offset (`TryNonMinerExchanges`' fairness pattern). Shared per-sender gates (warmup / ≥ 1 BTC / 10–40% fraction / fee / no-self) extracted into `TrySellFlowSend`. Auction qualification is UNTOUCHED — eligibility lives entirely in `ComputeAuctionLedger` (D-EB.7); only WHO gets to send changed.
  - [x] **Parity-accounting exemption + pre-floor fix**: casino-bot-cycle txs are exempted from the budget's organic-pending count via an in-memory `_casinoBotCycleTxIds` set (deliberately not persisted — a restart reverts the mempool itself, so the set correctly restarts empty; without this exemption the ~0.01/block early-era demand would be wholly consumed by the bot cycle and the cast would starve all over again). And organic pending txs now cancel accrued demand **before** flooring (`owed = max(0, target + carry − pending)`): the old post-floor `wholeOwed − pending` both erased up to a whole block's accumulated carry on a collision AND never counted organic traffic at all on sub-1-owed blocks (a systematic overshoot in busy eras).
  - [x] **Ghost attribution randomized**: `AdvanceGhostRotation` now draws the next pseudonym at random (plus a random initial index — a constant 0 start overrepresented `unknown_miner` by one block per session/bootstrap). The invisible mass's block TOTAL is untouched (power/attempt math unchanged); only its attribution spreads organically — some names ahead, some behind, no more 12-way tie. Applies to live play and the EB.1 entry-year bootstrap alike (both call the same method); already-mined blocks keep their attribution, only new ghost blocks diverge.

Then commit.
- **ND.4b — competitive fast-cycle referral bidding — spec (✅ SPEC COMPLETE, 2026-07-10 — design only, ZERO open questions remaining, NOT built)**: reworks the referral auction from ND.4a's slow, revenue-neutral casino-bot donation cycle into a fast, escalating ASCENDING-AUCTION bid war, per the developer's direct spec. Scope is narrow and deliberate: it changes `AuctionWindowMs`, `TryCasinoBotDonation`'s cadence/amount model, and `ComputeAuctionLedger`'s leader semantics — it does **not** touch `TryCastSellFlow` or `TryNonMinerExchanges`, which keep the unmodified historical fullness-parity budget from ND.3/ND.4a. This phase's OWN deliverable — a fully locked, implementation-ready spec — is complete; every decision below is final. Actual implementation is tracked separately as **ND.4c**, next.
  - [x] **Window shortened** (D-ND4b.1): `AuctionWindowMs` 100 → **20 in-game days**.
  - [x] **Casino-bot donation cadence made deterministic** (D-ND4b.2): `TryCasinoBotDonation`'s current geometric ~1-per-100-block draw (ND.4a) is replaced by a per-block donation-count draw (D-ND4b.3 below) targeting an average of **~1 `bot_1..4` → recruitable-non-miner donation per live block** — a 100×+ frequency jump, deliberate and specific to this minigame. "Recruitable" = introduced (`IntroUnixMs` reached) AND `Status != Resolved` (a resolved auction's winner is permanent, per the existing invariant — never re-opened, confirmed unchanged by D-ND4b.12 below).
  - [x] **Donation count per block varies, ~1 on average** (D-ND4b.3 — developer-confirmed, 2026-07-10): a flat "always exactly 1" reads monotone/synthetic, so each live block first draws how many casino-bot donation ATTEMPTS to make this block from a weighted distribution over `{0, 1, 2}` — 1 the common case, 0 and 2 both real but minority outcomes, simulating natural/organic behavior (proposed weights, tunable: **15% / 70% / 15%**), capped at 2, never more. Each attempted slot independently runs the target/sender selection below; **a bot may donate at most once per block** (slots exhaust the not-yet-used-this-block bot list), so a 2-donation block always involves two DIFFERENT bots.
  - [x] **Target priority = soonest-to-expire, gated by affordability** (D-ND4b.4 — developer-confirmed): for each donation slot, rank recruitable non-miners — (1) already-in-auction ones first, sorted by ascending `WindowCloseUnixMs` (closest to its 20-day expiry gets first shot — keeps live races alive instead of every slot drifting to whichever target is easiest), then (2) not-yet-in-auction ones (no countdown running yet — lower priority; ordering among these is the assistant's own filler, unconfirmed, but low-stakes). Walk this list in order; for the first target where at least one not-yet-used-this-block bot can afford that target's current minimum (leading bid's raise floor, or the fixed first-donation floor) + the fixed fee, pick the sender uniformly at random among the affordable bots (ND.4a's fairness pattern, kept) and donate — **always respecting affordability first**: a bot never gets forced into a donation it can't cover; if the top-priority target isn't affordable by any remaining bot, the slot falls through to the next-priority target that IS affordable. If NO target in the whole list is affordable by any remaining bot, that slot simply doesn't fire — a legitimate, additional source of the occasional zero-donation block alongside D-ND4b.3's own weighting.
  - [x] **First donation to a not-yet-competing non-miner — fixed floor, not a balance fraction** (D-ND4b.5): a recruitable non-miner's very first donation is pinned to a **0.1 BTC principal floor** + a **fixed 0.1 BTC fee** (`NetworkFeePolicy.MinFee` — NOT the existing `MinBotFeeBtc..MaxBotFeeBtc` 0.1–1.0 random range the ordinary sell-flow uses, which would be a nonsensical fee-to-principal ratio on a 0.1 BTC bid). The principal floor then gets the D-ND4b.11 random tail added on top (below) before broadcast. This donation both funds the non-miner and activates its 20-day countdown, exactly as today's "first qualifying bid" rule — just with a fixed tiny principal instead of ND.4a's 10–40%-of-balance draw.
  - [x] **Escalating minimum-raise formula for later donations** (D-ND4b.6 — developer-confirmed 2026-07-10, "sí, vamos con tus recomendaciones"): a donation to an ALREADY-competing non-miner must raise the current leading bid's principal by at least `raiseMin`; any amount ≥ `raiseMin` is accepted (no upper cap — a bidder may always overpay, as the player's jump straight to 10 BTC in the spec demonstrates). Locked formula (reproduces both of the developer's worked examples exactly):
    - `MinBidBtc = 0.1` (the same floor as D-ND4b.5's first donation)
    - `raiseMin(leadingBid) = max(MinBidBtc, 0.10 × leadingBid)`
    - `raiseMax(leadingBid) = max(2 × MinBidBtc, 0.20 × leadingBid)`
    - Check @ leadingBid = 0.2 BTC: raiseMin/raiseMax = 0.1 / 0.2 ⇒ next-bid band **[0.3, 0.4]** — matches "bot_3 donated 0.2, next raise is 0.1–0.2, bot_4 sends 0.4" exactly.
    - Check @ leadingBid = 10 BTC: raiseMin/raiseMax = 1 / 2 ⇒ next-bid band **[11, 12]** — matches "player jumps to 10, next bot sends 11 or 12" exactly.
    - A casino-bot making a RAISE donation (as opposed to a first donation) picks its target principal as a **coin flip between `leadingBid + raiseMin` and `leadingBid + raiseMax`** (50/50, per the spec — a two-point discrete choice, distinct from the continuous uniform draws used elsewhere in the sell-flow), then adds the D-ND4b.11 random tail on top.
    - **Resolved 2026-07-10** (developer confirmed): the spec's "next donor cannot donate less than 0.4 BTC" line was the developer's own shorthand slip — the real enforced floor after a 0.4 BTC leading bid is `leadingBid + raiseMin` = 0.4 + 0.1 = **0.5 BTC**, not 0.4. `raiseMin` is always a strictly-greater-than-leader floor; no exact-tie bid can ever become leader through this path (see D-ND4b.11/D-ND4b.13 for the separate, much rarer coincidental-tie case).
  - [x] **New leading-bid semantics — LAST qualifying raise wins, not cumulative total** (D-ND4b.7 — a behavior change to `ComputeAuctionLedger`): today's `TopDonor` sums a bidder's donations across the WHOLE ledger and crowns the highest cumulative total the leader. ND.4b replaces this, for the fast-cycle regime, with a real ascending-auction rule: the most recent donation that met-or-exceeded the required minimum raise IS the sole leading bid. A qualifying-bidder donation that does **not** meet the current minimum raise still gets recorded (counts toward `TotalReceived`/`DonorCount` for display, per the existing "all funding counts toward totals" rule) but does not become leader and does not reset the countdown — same treatment a non-qualifying (cast) donation already gets today.
  - [x] **Countdown resets on every accepted raise** (D-ND4b.8): each donation that clears the minimum raise becomes the new leader AND resets the non-miner's window to a fresh 20 days from that donation's own timestamp. This effectively repurposes `FirstBidUnixMs` into "timestamp of the CURRENT leading bid" rather than literally the first-ever bid — likely needs a rename (e.g. `LeadingBidUnixMs`) so the field name doesn't lie about its own semantics; `WindowCloseUnixMs = LeadingBidUnixMs + AuctionWindowMs`, recomputed on every accepted raise. A non-miner can stay in-auction indefinitely as long as qualifying raises keep landing inside each rolling 20-day window; it only resolves once 20 days pass with no new one.
  - [x] **Player wallet UX — non-blocking minimum-bid warning** (D-ND4b.9): wherever the player can send BTC to a known recruitable non-miner address (BTCWallet's send panel, primarily), surface the live "minimum to compete" figure (`leadingBid + raiseMin`, or the fixed 0.1 BTC floor if the non-miner has no leading bid yet) and, if the entered amount is below it, show a clearly worded but **non-blocking** warning that the send will still execute but will **not** count as a competitive bid (won't move the leader, won't reset the countdown) — it still lands as ordinary funding, the same treatment any other sub-threshold or non-qualifying donation already gets.
  - [x] **BlockExplorer Enroll Mode — new displayed field** (D-ND4b.10, corrected 2026-07-11): alongside each recruitable non-miner's existing info, add the current leading bid's **LIVE, current SC value** — computed from `BtcMarketDataService`'s price as of NOW, recomputed fresh on every refresh (the original wording here, "day-of-donation SC value... a point-in-time valuation frozen to when the bid landed, not continuously re-priced against today's rate," was a writing mistake the developer caught late and is corrected throughout this plan/CLAUDE.md/ProjectDesignManual/GLOSSARY — nothing in this system displays a value frozen at a historical day outside of settlement, D-ND5.6) — displayed next to its BTC amount, e.g. "Leading bid: 0.40 BTC (≈ 2.14 SC)". The displayed BTC figure is the PRINCIPAL only (net received, fee excluded), per the developer's own worked example (a 0.15465460 BTC principal shown alongside a 0.25465460 BTC total-with-fee debit). No new persistence — recomputed on the fly inside `ComputeAuctionLedger`/its BlockExplorer consumer, consistent with the ledger's existing fully chain-derived, nothing-persisted design (Pattern 2's three questions still resolve to "no" — this is derived, not stored).
  - [x] **Random 8-decimal tail on every casino-bot donation, to kill artificial round numbers** (D-ND4b.11 — developer-specified, 2026-07-10): every casino-bot donation amount (first-donation floor OR raise coin-flip target) gets a random fractional tail ADDED ON TOP before broadcast — e.g. a 0.1 BTC first-donation floor actually sends something like `0.15465460` BTC principal (fee stays the flat `0.1` from D-ND4b.5/NetworkFeePolicy.MinFee, unaffected by the tail — confirmed by the developer's own example, `0.25465460 − 0.15465460 = 0.1` exactly). The tail is **strictly additive** (never subtracted), so the amount broadcast is always ≥ the computed minimum — it only ever makes a bid MORE competitive, never invalidates it. `Money.Normalize` (8-decimal) as usual. Exact tail magnitude/distribution is an implementation-time tuning choice (the developer's own example implies a tail roughly on the same order as the base amount — proposed: `Random.Shared.NextDouble() × baseAmount`, i.e. up to +100%, tunable) — the only HARD requirements are: (a) additive on top of the required minimum, (b) applies to principal only, never fee, (c) makes an exact-amount collision between two independent donations astronomically unlikely.
    - **Tie-break rule** (developer-specified, resolves the "two donations to the same target land in the same block" case D-ND4b.4's per-slot design can legitimately produce): if two valid donations to the SAME non-miner both land in the same block, **both transactions execute for real** (funds move, neither is rejected) — but only the HIGHER of the two amounts counts as the new leading bid / resets the countdown; the lower one is recorded toward `TotalReceived`/`DonorCount` like any non-leading donation (D-ND4b.7). In the vanishingly rare case the two amounts are EXACTLY equal (the D-ND4b.11 tail is specifically designed to make this near-impossible, but not provably impossible), the tie breaks toward whichever transaction has the EARLIER "send moment" (D-ND4b.13).
  - [x] **Tie-break mechanism for exact-amount collisions — transaction order within the block** (D-ND4b.13 — developer-confirmed 2026-07-10, resolves the earlier OQ-ND4b.1; no new schema field needed): "earlier send moment" for two txs sharing the same `block.Timestamp` (the engine has no sub-block timestamp field) is defined as the ORDER the two donation transactions were appended to the block's transaction list (i.e., their index in `block.Transactions`). Since both the per-slot target/sender selection (D-ND4b.4) AND the per-block count draw (D-ND4b.3) are already randomized, this ordering varies block-to-block on its own — satisfying "the send moment must vary block to block to look organic" with zero new persisted state.
  - [x] **Permanent wins stay permanent — no retroactive reinterpretation** (D-ND4b.12 — developer-confirmed, resolves the earlier OQ-ND4b.2): a `Resolved` non-miner's winner from ND.4a's cumulative-sum regime is NEVER reopened or recomputed under ND.4b's rules — the existing "a win is permanent" invariant holds exactly as before. Only non-`Resolved` (`InAuction`) non-miners adopt the new mechanics going forward. An `InAuction` non-miner's PRE-ND.4b donation history still needs one-time re-evaluation under the new last-bid-wins rule the first time `ComputeAuctionLedger` runs post-upgrade (replaying an old cumulative-leader history through the new rule can legitimately pick a different leader/timestamp than what was shown pre-upgrade) — call this out clearly in-code and in the implementation commit, not a silent reinterpretation.
  - [x] Spec-writing is this phase's entire scope, and it is done: zero open questions remain (D-ND4b.1–13 all locked, developer-confirmed 2026-07-10). No code has been written — that work is ND.4c, below.
- **ND.4c — implement the ND.4b referral-bidding rework** ✅ BUILT + PLAYTESTED (2026-07-10) — turns the locked ND.4b spec (D-ND4b.1–13) into code; developer commit done:
  - [x] `NetworkRoot.cs` constants: `AuctionWindowMs` 100d → 20d (D-ND4b.1); `CasinoBotDonationWeightZeroPercent`/`CasinoBotDonationWeightOnePercent` = 15/70 (the remaining 15% is implicit — D-ND4b.3) over `{0,1,2}`; `MinBidBtc = 0.1` (D-ND4b.5); a new `_marketData` autoload reference (`BtcMarketDataService`, populated in `_Ready()`) for the D-ND4b.10 SC valuation. `CasinoBotSellFlowMeanBlocks` (ND.4a's geometric draw) retired.
  - [x] `TryCasinoBotDonation` rewrite: `DrawCasinoBotDonationCount()` (weighted 0/1/2, D-ND4b.3) → priority-ranked target list built ONCE per block (active auctions soonest-to-expire first, then not-yet-competing ones, D-ND4b.4) → `TryCasinoBotDonateOnce` per slot (affordability-filtered bot pool, fixed first-donation floor OR coin-flip raise target via new `RaiseMin`/`RaiseMax` helpers, D-ND4b.5/6) → additive random tail headroom-capped so a send can never fail for lack of funds (D-ND4b.11) → one-bot-per-block cap (`usedBotIds`). Fee pinned to `NetworkFeePolicy.MinFee` for this cycle only — `TryCastSellFlow`'s existing `MinBotFeeBtc..MaxBotFeeBtc` range is untouched. The old `TrySellFlowSend`-based single-slot geometric draw is gone; `TryCastSellFlow`/`TryNonMinerExchanges` (the historical-budget cycles) are unmodified.
  - [x] `ComputeAuctionLedger` rework: `TopDonor` (cumulative-sum leader) deleted; replaced by a per-non-miner ascending-auction ratchet — qualifying bids grouped by shared block timestamp (bids in the SAME block race the SAME starting leader, never each other in sequence — the D-ND4b.11 same-block tie-break, resolved by each group's own iteration order per D-ND4b.13's append-order `seq` counter, no new schema field), each group's floor = leader's principal + `RaiseMin(leader)` (or `MinBidBtc` pre-leader), strictly-higher bids ratchet the leader forward. A gap of more than `AuctionWindowMs` between the leader's bid and the next candidate group freezes resolution there permanently (D-ND4b.12 — `Resolved` auctions never revisited by a later bid, however large). `FirstBidUnixMs` renamed to `LeadingBidUnixMs` throughout (`NonMinerDonationSummary`, `BlockExplorer.cs`).
  - [x] `NonMinerDonationSummary`: new `LeadingDonorScValue` (decimal?) — the leading bid's BTC principal × `BtcMarketDataService.GetEffectivePriceUsd` at the bid's own game-calendar date (D-ND4b.10); null before Market Birth or with no leader yet.
  - [x] BlockExplorer Enroll Mode (`RefreshEnrollMode`): leading-bid line now renders `"leading bid <addr> (X.XXXXXXXX BTC ≈ Y.YYYYYYYY SC)"` when a same-day price exists, BTC-only otherwise; `FirstBidUnixMs` → `LeadingBidUnixMs` reference updated.
  - [x] BTCWallet send panel: new `_auctionBidWarningLabel` (non-blocking, amber) + `RefreshAuctionBidWarning()` wired to `_toDropdown.ItemSelected`/`_manualAddressInput.TextChanged`/`_amountInput.TextChanged`/`EnterSendMode`, backed by a new public `NetworkRoot.GetMinimumCompetitiveBidBtc(address)` (returns the exact same floor the casino-bot cycle itself enforces — UI and backend agree on the threshold by construction, not by duplicated formulas). Clears on a successful send.
  - [x] `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors (three passes: core auction logic, BlockExplorer, BTCWallet).
  - [x] Developer playtest (2026-07-10, long run to March 2014): everything worked as expected. Commit done.
- **ND.4 — calibration playtest + docs pass** ✅ **COMPLETE (2026-07-10)**:
  - [x] Calibration playtest (developer, long run 21 Mar 2010 → Mar 2014 via the EB.1 entry-year tool + live play): D-14.8 share anchors, block pace, the N=40 non-miner pool, and the ND.4b/ND.4c 20-day fast-auction cadence all confirmed working as expected in play. No BTC-starvation or other D-14.11 revisit trigger observed.
  - [x] **§6.2/§6.4 decoupling proposal written into ProjectDesignManual** as a documented-but-unbuilt option — `Documentation/ProjectDesignManual.md` new **Chapter 36** (§36.6 specifically): the non-miner-pool-past-40 scaling investigation, the `O(registered nodes × chain length)` cost finding, and the full "decouple non-miner wallet identity from full `NodeAgent` broadcast registration" proposal (reading balance/UTXO state off the player's canonical chain via the existing `GetAddressBalanceDetails`/`CollectUsedAddressSet` pattern), written out in the detail D-EB.8 promised — NOT implemented.
  - [x] **CLAUDE.md**: new `### BtcNetworkDataService` (autoload #17) and `### NetworkPopulationScheduler` subsections (Key Architecture chapter, with an explicit note that the scheduler itself is a plain static class, not a registered autoload); the "Referral auction" canonical-decisions row rewritten for the ND.4b/ND.4c ascending-auction model (20-day rolling window, bid-ladder formula, permanence caveat); two new "Implemented" bullets (the population scheduler + the auction rework).
  - [x] **ProjectDesignManual**: new **Chapter 36 — Historical Network Population Scheduler** (the two-layer hybrid model: era-standard power/scale anchor, cast spawn-drip, ghost rotation, the fullness-parity tx layer, the EB.1 entry-year bootstrap, and §36.6's decoupling proposal); **Chapter 22 §22.7** added (the ND.4b/ND.4c ascending-auction rework: bid ladder, rolling window, permanence, the casino-bot cadence, player-facing UI); §22.6's now-superseded window/leader-model lines cross-referenced forward to §22.7 rather than silently left stale; Chapter 22's header status line updated to point at §22.7 as the current bidding rules.
  - [x] **GLOSSARY**: new entries — *Cast Miner*, *Era-Standard Power*, *Ghost Miner*, *Leading Bid*, *Non-Miner*, *Qualifying Bidder*, *Recruitable*, *Referral Auction*.
  - [x] This plan → **COMPLETE**. Step 14 (the historical network population scheduler + the referral-auction rework it enabled) is done.
  - [x] **ND.4d addendum (2026-07-10, same day, post-completion)**: playtesting a real player donation surfaced a design refinement — the player's own minimum raise over the leading bid should NOT use the casino-bots' 10%/20% `RaiseMin`/`RaiseMax` formula. Implemented as an asymmetric floor in `NetworkRoot.ComputeAuctionLedger`'s ratchet walk and `GetMinimumCompetitiveBidBtc`: the **player's** required raise is a flat **1 satoshi** above the leader; the **casino-bots'** required raise is unchanged (still the D-ND4b.6 formula). Deliberate one-sided exception, not a balance bug — a cheap player raise is easy for the very next casino-bot bid to overtake, teaching the risk empirically rather than via a blocking warning. `dotnet build` 0/0. Documented in `Documentation/ProjectDesignManual.md` new **§22.8**; CLAUDE.md's Referral auction canonical-decision row and GLOSSARY's *Leading Bid* entry updated to match. Playtested + committed 2026-07-10.

## 4. Open questions

### 4.1 Round 1 — RESOLVED (2026-07-07)

All seven round-1 OQs were answered by the user and locked as **D-14.1 … D-14.7** in §0.5. Notable answer detail preserved verbatim in spirit: OQ-14.1 → hybrid, with the population scale anchored at the historical maximum and every other point derived relative to it (realized as P-14.A's log-decade curve — the assistant's proposed refinement of the same principle, since linear max-normalization of a 10¹²-range series would zero out everything before ~2023); OQ-14.2 → miners' flow = their natural sell-side donations, non-miners get a dedicated exchange scheduler, no synthetic txs; OQ-14.6 → fees fetched now, fee replay (Option A) locked as the mechanism with the reactive market (Option B) kept as a future *validation* experiment.

### 4.2 Round 2 — RESOLVED (2026-07-07)

All five round-2 OQs answered and locked as **D-14.8 … D-14.12** (§0.5). The pivotal answer, OQ-14.8, did more than calibrate: it **replaced the free `GrowthFactor` knob with a principled anchor** — "the player at era-standard resources always holds one visible-cast member's share (`1/28 ≈ 3.6%` at the historical max), can beat it somewhat with maximal smart play, and falls below it easily with careless play" — from which §3.1's `TotalNetworkUnits` derivation follows with no free parameter left. (Arithmetic note for the record: `1/28 = 0.0357 ≈ 3.6%`, i.e. the fraction 0.0357 — not 0.035%.) OQ-14.9–14.12 → assistant recommendations accepted verbatim: ghost-name pool with frozen BTC, historical quiet accepted, frozen invisible-mass BTC in v1, spawn-only cast in v1.

## 5. Extra subphases (2026-07-09, user request before ND.4): EB — DEV entry-year bootstraps & the historical referral timeline

Two additions requested before the ND.4 verification round: (1) an efficient way to generate test worlds landing the player in ANY year of the dataset, and (2) the locked design for WHEN and HOW FAST the casino referral ecosystem (donor miners + donation-receiving non-miners) populates. They interlock: EB.1's fast-build needs EB.2's schedule to reproduce non-miner history, so **EB.2 lands first**.

### 5.0 Why the DevAltTimeline doesn't fit here

The Step-13 simulacrum MOVED genesis and the whole crew (+484 days) — a throwaway world with anachronistic dates. This is the opposite need: **genesis and the founders stay at their canonical times**, and the world **fast-builds its own real history** — founder arcs playing out, cast miners spawning per the ND.2 curve, halvings crossing — up to a landing on **March 21 of any year 2009–2025**, where the "new crew" (player + `bot_1..4` + casino pool) enters fresh, exactly like today's Mar-2009 landing. This is the §9.1 "entry-point bootstrap" concept, built now as a **DEV testing scaffold** (not yet the player-facing feature): worlds are **canon-compatible** (every data-driven gate — market birth, fee activation, decades, halvings — reads true dates and just works, because the timeline IS canonical). D-14.7's pure `date → target` functions are what make this cheap.

### 5.1 EB.1 — the DEV entry-year bootstrap generator

- **Selector**: `TimelineConfig.DevEntryYear` (const int; **`0` on `main` forever** = canonical Mar-2009 start; valid 2010–2025 on a DEV branch). `EntryDayLocal = DevEntryYear == 0 ? PlayerStartDayLocal : Mar 21 of DevEntryYear`. The world stamp gains a suffix (`CANON-2009-01-03+ENTRY-2015`), so the EXISTING guard auto-wipes on any change — year↔year and on↔off, both directions, zero new machinery (ProjectDesignManual §35.1). Orthogonal to `DevAltTimeline` (never both non-default).
- **The fast-build** (extends `HistoricalBootstrapService` past its canonical ~113-block segment): scripted-cadence blocks (~58,500 in-game s ± jitter ⇒ ~1.477/day) from Mar 2009 to `EntryDayLocal`. Per block at date `d`: cast members spawn per the ND.2 curve (same registry + one-per-block drip); the miner is drawn by **weighted sample over the same power model** — Satoshi per his pace-to-11,000 regulator (retiring ≈ Apr 2011), Hal fading to Aug 2009, powered cast at era-standard, ghosts for the invisible remainder; scripted founder events (the April-2009 Hearn round-trip) inject at their canonical dates; non-miners spawn + auction windows open per EB.2's schedule; halvings land by height (~3.9y each — entry 2025 crosses all 4).
- **Difficulty stays feedback-only (power = 0) during the build** — the honest-but-cheap option: truly solving late-era blocks at power-scaled difficulty (585 × ~3,300 ≈ 1.9M attempts/block) is computationally impossible for an 8,600-block build, while low-difficulty PoW keeps `ChainIsValid` fully legitimate (~5M hashes ≈ seconds) and the LIVE regulator re-converges within a few post-landing blocks (the feed-forward anchor is absolute, eased at α = 0.7). The low pre-landing difficulty column is an accepted DEV anachronism (D-13.0 spirit).
- **Tx history — options** (OQ-EB.1): **A (recommended v1)**: coinbase-only build + a **seed-funding pass** just before landing (a few miner→non-miner donations so holders land funded and the exchange scheduler can breathe); chain stays light, the live ND.3 budget takes over from landing. **B**: run the real ND.3 budget for only the last N in-game months before landing. **C (rejected for now)**: full historical tx replay — O(blocks × UTXO-replay) build cost and a ~100k-tx chain that every runtime full-chain scan pays for afterwards.
- **Landing semantics are all inherited**: player start instant = last built block's timestamp (§24.9, chain-derived — automatic); pre-genesis restarts reset to the entry instant; Satoshi lands retired (entry ≥ 2011) with ~11,000 BTC frozen; the swap desk lands open (entry ≥ Jul 2010) with live prices.
- **Build sizes** (from the dataset, ~1.477 blocks/day): 2010 → ~539 blocks · 2011 → ~1,078 · 2013 → ~2,158 · 2015 → ~3,236 · 2017 → ~4,316 · 2020 → ~5,935 · 2023 → ~7,552 · 2025 → ~8,632 (4 halvings).
- **Registry note**: switching entry years wipes the world but not identities (the registry is reset-spared by design); a previous build's cast simply gets reused in spawn order — ND.2's powered-first-N rule already tolerates any surplus. For a fully pristine cast list, deleting `bot_wallet_registry.json` manually remains available (identity files are deliberately outside the guard).

### 5.2 EB.2 — the historical referral timeline (the user's question 2, answered from the data)

**When does the referral process start?** The dataset answers unambiguously: through ALL of 2009, active addresses ≈ blocks per day (50 ≈ 50 — every active address is a miner's coinbase; even the famous first exchange rate of Oct 2009 created no measurable holder population). The first **sustained ≥2× address/block decoupling is 2010-07-16** — Mt. Gox's launch, within data noise (≥3× by 07-20). Non-miner holders became a real population exactly when the first exchange opened. **Proposal (P-EB.1): the referral process — non-miner introduction and their auctions — begins at Market Birth**, reusing the existing canonical data-driven gate (`BtcMarketDataService.FirstDataDateLocal`, 2010-07-18): no new date constant, same philosophy as the trading unlock. Consistency bonus: ND.3's fullness budget already makes 2009-era donations ≈ 0, so nothing meaningful is lost by the ~16-month idle referral era — it's the historically honest quiet.

**How many non-miners, at what rhythm?** The address curve (M4) is the population proxy, in the same log-compressed shape as everything else: `nonMinerTarget(t) = 1 + round(3 × log10(maxAdrActSinceBirth(t) / AdrActAtBirth))`, running-max so it never shrinks (the spawn-only D-14.12 shape). *(Implementation note, 2026-07-09: the running-max starts AT birth — anchor = the birth day's 860 — because including pre-birth history lets the July-2010 slashdot spike inflate the anchor and strand 2 bots; see the EB.2 build log.)* At **3 non-miners per address-decade the EXISTING 10-bot pool is fully consumed**: empirically 1 at birth (2010-07-18), 6 by the June-2011 bubble, ~1/year after, **all 10 by 2016-01-20**, flat after. **No new non-miner spawning machinery is required**; an `AddNonMinerBot` mirror becomes worthwhile only if the dataset is ever extended past the dataset's own peak (§9.3). The once-only auction mechanic is untouched — only the INTRODUCTION SCHEDULE changes: **`NonMinerIntroIntervalMs` (one per ~2 in-game days from live-mining start) retires** in favor of the curve, in canon live play and in EB.1 builds alike (one shared pure function).

> **⚠️ Factual correction (2026-07-09, found during the §6 EB.3 investigation)**: this section originally said the address curve's running-max "peaks in Dec 2017." **Verified against the actual CSV — that was never checked and was wrong.** The dataset's true all-time active-address maximum is **2021-04-15 (1,366,494)**, and the full birth→peak span is **3.201 address-decades**, not "~2.9." This doesn't change the shipped N=10 intro schedule (those 10 dates, 2010-07-18 → 2016-01-20, were independently derived from the real running-max curve and are unaffected by what the eventual peak date is), but every "2017 peak" / "2.9-decade" reference in this plan and its sibling docs (CLAUDE.md, ProjectDesignManual §22.6, PRIVATE_ROADMAP) was corrected to match. See §6 for the fuller investigation this correction is part of.

**And the donor miners?** No separate schedule needed — **the ND.2 cast curve IS the donor-entry rhythm**: 4 base bots at player start + ~2 cast miners per hashrate-decade, each becoming a donor after its first-mine warmup (ND.3). The numbers converge pleasingly with the recipient side: **~10 visible donors exist at Market Birth — exactly when the first recipient appears** — growing to ~23 through the 2013 era and ~33 by 2025, always ahead of the 1→10 recipient curve, which is how the real economy ordered itself (miners first, holders after).

**Amendments (2026-07-09, canonical — D-EB.5/D-EB.6/D-EB.7):** the auction **window grows from 7 in-game days to 6 in-game MONTHS** (provisional, 6–12 range, dev-tunable — with only 10 non-miners ever, each auction is too precious for a blink-and-miss week), and the **window countdown starts at the bot's FIRST QUALIFYING BID** rather than its introduction — a bot nobody has bid on stays recruitable indefinitely, and every auction that resolves has a real winner. **Bids come only from casino players (D-EB.7)** — defined as nodes whose mining REQUIRES betting at the casino: the player **and the classic casino-miner-bots `bot_1..4`**, who already run real betting sessions to mine and so already have a casino relationship. The much larger, historically-growing cast of Step-14 miners (up to **29 additional beyond the classic 4, 33 total at the 2025 max**) mine via drained attempts, never bet, and so never qualify — their sell-flow, and every non-miner↔non-miner exchange, stays economy that funds wallets without touching auctions. **Bootstrap guarantee, made explicit**: in EB.1 entry-year builds (2011–2025) the non-miners ARE introduced at their historical dates during the fast-build — they are world citizens like the cast, NOT part of the landing crew — and the D-EB.1 seed-funding pass funds their wallets just before landing **without constituting bids** (in this initial development, `bot_1..4` aren't yet part of the fast-build's own miner roster — see EB.1 §5.1), so the player arrives to funded holders and **pristine, not-yet-started auctions**.

### 5.3 Round EB — RESOLVED (2026-07-09, all assistant recommendations accepted). READY TO IMPLEMENT.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-EB.1** | Fast-build tx history = **option A**: coinbase-only build + a **seed-funding pass** just before landing (a few miner→non-miner donations so holders land funded); the live ND.3 budget takes over from the landing block. B (thin window) unbuilt; C (full replay) rejected. |
| **D-EB.2** | ~~An auction window that expires with ZERO bids leaves the non-miner still recruitable~~ — **SUBSUMED by D-EB.6 (2026-07-09)**, kept for the record: under the first-donation countdown an unbid window never *starts*, so the "expired with zero bids" state cannot exist and the re-open question disappears. |
| **D-EB.3** | ~~Non-miner pool stays capped at the existing 10~~ — **REOPENED at round 3 (§6, 2026-07-09)**: the user is requesting a scale-up (10 → up to 220). See §6 for the investigation and the pending recommendation. `AddNonMinerBot`-equivalent machinery is being scoped there, not deferred to §9.3 anymore. |
| **D-EB.4** | **The Market-Birth referral gate (P-EB.1) applies to the canonical LIVE game**, not just fast-builds: the 1-per-~2-days `NonMinerIntroIntervalMs` intro from live-mining start is retired everywhere, replaced by the shared pure schedule function (birth-gated address curve). |
| **D-EB.5** | *(2026-07-09, canonical change)* **Auction window = 6 in-game MONTHS** (~180 in-game days ≈ ~266 blocks), superseding the 7-day starter value: with the pool capped at 10 forever (D-EB.3) each auction is a rare, precious event, and a week (~1.7 real hours of continuous autobet at 100X) is blink-and-miss. **PROVISIONAL**: dev-tunable, expected range **6–12 months**, to be confirmed by the developer's own flow testing — and deliberately low-stakes to get exactly right, because D-EB.6 means the clock only runs once someone has actually bid. Implements at EB.2 (`NetworkRoot.AuctionWindowMs`). |
| **D-EB.6** | *(2026-07-09, canonical change; wording corrected same day per D-EB.7)* **The window countdown starts at the bot's FIRST QUALIFYING DONATION — i.e. its first real BID (D-EB.7)** — not at its introduction, and regardless of whether that bid happens in a bootstrapped world or live. Consequences: (a) a never-bid-on non-miner never starts its clock ⇒ **recruitable indefinitely** (subsumes D-EB.2); (b) every window that expires necessarily has ≥ 1 qualifying bid ⇒ **every resolved auction has a winner** (§22.1's old "closed with no winner" state disappears); (c) the bot transfers that fund non-miners (sell-flow, exchanges, seed pass) neither start nor move auctions — see D-EB.7. Implements at EB.2 (`ComputeAuctionLedger`). |
| **D-EB.7** | *(2026-07-09, user correction, wording finalized same day)* **Only casino players can BID in referral auctions — a referral is a casino relationship, and an entity that doesn't play at the casino can't hold one.** **Refined test for "casino player": mining that REQUIRES betting at the casino (bet-driven, hardware-credit-locked) — exactly like the player.** Today that means **the player AND the classic casino-miner-bots `bot_1..4`** (`BotWalletRegistry.MinerBots`) — they already run real betting sessions (`StartBots`/`BuildBotConfigs`, hardware-credit-locked speed) to mine, so their existing sell-flow donations (ND.3's `TryMinerSellFlow`) qualify as bids **without any new machinery**. The **Step-14 CAST miners** (`BotWalletRegistry.CastMiners`, up to **29 additional beyond the classic 4, 33 total at the 2025 maximum** — recomputed precisely from the dataset, superseding an earlier "~27" recollection) do **NOT** qualify: they mine via **drained attempts** (founder-style, concurrent with the player's time advancement), never place a bet, and so never form a casino relationship — their sell-flow donations, and all non-miner↔non-miner exchanges, stay **economy only**. The auction ledger distinguishes qualifying bidders (player + `bot_1..4` addresses) from mere senders. EB.1's seed-funding pass (drawn from cast + miner-bot sell-flow) still funds non-miner wallets **without constituting bids** in the initial development. **Recommendation (assistant), not yet built**: keep the casino-miner-bot roster fixed at the classic 4 — promoting any of the growing cast to full casino-player status (hardware credits, bankroll, betting sessions) is a substantial, separate feature (a "casino membership growth" system), not a natural extension of this plan; defer it, don't build it now. |

## 6. Round 3 (2026-07-09): non-miner pool scale-up & confirming the era-standard reference

Documentation-only round, per the developer's request — no code changes in this section. Two questions: (1) is the "1/33 average, adaptive, unaffected by player/founders" mental model already what's built? (2) can the non-miner pool grow from 10 toward 220, with a correspondingly shorter (~1 in-game month) auction window?

### 6.1 The era-standard reference — already built exactly as described, no change needed

**Confirmed**: `BtcNetworkDataService.GetEraStandardPower(date)` (ND.1) already IS the "1/33 average" reference, and it already has exactly the property requested — a pure function of the CALENDAR DATE only:

```
EraStandardPower(date) = EraMaxHardwareCredits ^ (decades(date) / decadesAtDatasetEnd)
TotalNetworkUnits(date) = EraStandardPower(date) × targetVisibleMiners(date)
```

`decades(date)` reads the dataset's hashrate curve; `targetVisibleMiners(date)` is the ND.2 cast-size formula. **Neither input depends on the player's, founders', or any bot's actual live power** — so `EraStandardPower(date)` is precisely "what one of the N historic miners is worth today," and `TotalNetworkUnits(date) / targetVisibleMiners(date) = EraStandardPower(date)` by construction — the average, at every date, is exactly this value. It already answers "am I above or below the historical average" as a pure lookup, with no player/founder feedback loop. It's already surfaced in DEV telemetry too (`network_population_trace.csv`'s `castPowerEach` column IS this quantity, logged every block).

**One clarification worth stating explicitly**, since it's adjacent but different: the *invisible-mass ("ghost") layer's* power DOES respond to the player's/founders'/cast's actual live power (`InvisibleUnits = max(0, TotalNetworkUnits − actual others)`) — that's intentional and separate: it's what keeps the *total* network power (and hence the difficulty regulator's pace) tracking the historical shape regardless of how the real participants split it. It never touches `EraStandardPower(date)` itself, which stays the untouched reference point. Nothing to build here — this section is purely a confirmation.

### 6.2 Non-miner pool scale-up (10 → N) — investigation before a number is chosen

**The schedule math needs no new data — it's already fully parametric.** `BtcNetworkDataService`'s `NonMinerPoolSize` / `NonMinersPerAddressDecade` / `BaseNonMinersAtBirth` constants already drive the whole intro schedule from the EXISTING active-address curve (M4). "Translating the data to 220" is not a data-acquisition problem — it's retuning two constants and letting the same formula re-derive N intro dates from the same CSV. No new dataset, no new pipeline.

**Numeric simulation against the real CSV** (birth-anchored running-max, `perDecade = (N−1) / peakDecades`, `peakDecades = 3.201` — see the correction below):

| N | perDecade | last slot deploys | count by 2011-06-19 (bubble) | count by 2013-11-29 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10 (shipped) | 3.0 | 2016-01-20 | 6 | 8 |
| 20 | 5.94 | 2017-11-29 | 10 | 15 |
| 40 | 12.18 | 2017-12-13 | 20 | 30 |
| 100 | 30.93 | 2021-01-06 | 49 | 75 |
| 220 | 68.41 | 2021-01-06 | 106 | 164 |

**Finding — the log curve front-loads growth, it doesn't spread it evenly.** At N=220, **106 of the 220 non-miners (48%) deploy before the June-2011 bubble**, many on the exact SAME calendar day (the first five dates at N=220 are `2010-07-18, 2010-07-19, 2010-07-19, 2010-07-20, 2010-07-25` — multiple same-day intros, a direct consequence of `round()` jumping several integers in one day once the density constant is high). This isn't wrong, but it's a real *design* consideration independent of performance: a pool that "feels" like 10 rare, gradually-arriving individuals at N=10 becomes dozens of same-day arrivals in the first year at N=220 — worth deciding deliberately, not discovering by accident (§6.5, OQ-EB.7).

**⚠️ Factual correction found during this investigation**: earlier EB.2 documentation (this plan, and nowhere else — verified clean in CLAUDE.md/ProjectDesignManual/PRIVATE_ROADMAP) stated the address curve "peaks in Dec 2017" and spans "~2.9 address-decades." **That was never actually checked against the CSV and was wrong.** Verified now: the dataset's true all-time active-address maximum is **2021-04-15 (1,366,494)**, and the birth→peak span is **3.201 address-decades**. This does NOT change the already-shipped N=10 schedule (those 10 dates were derived independently from the running-max curve itself, not from knowing the peak date in advance) — only the prose describing it was corrected (§5.2 above).

**The real technical risk — registered node count multiplies an existing, chain-length-growing cost.** Traced through the actual code (`NetworkSimulator.cs`, `BlockchainService.cs`), not assumed:
- Every `NodeAgent` registered via `SharedNetwork.RegisterNode` gets **every** mined block (`BroadcastBlock`) and **every** broadcast transaction (`BroadcastTransaction`) pushed into its own local `Blockchain` object — this is a real per-node cost, not just bookkeeping, because:
- `AddTransactionToPendingTransactions` (called on every node, for every broadcast tx) needs `GetUtxoSet()`, which is cached **per node, per `_chainVersion`** — cheap when the cache is warm, but a **full chain replay** (`O(chain length)`) the first time it's called after that node accepts a new block. Since a new block invalidates every node's cache simultaneously, and the very next transaction broadcast after a block forces every node to rebuild, **the cost of "the first tx after a mined block" scales as `registered node count × chain length`**.
- This is not a new problem introduced by this request — it already exists today at ~40-ish registered nodes (player + 4 casino-miner-bots + up to 33 cast + 10 non-miners + casino + 2 founders + occasional session-transient ghosts), and it already grows every block as the chain lengthens over a long playthrough (up to 71,400 blocks at the canonical 2141 supply-exhaustion horizon). **Scaling non-miners alone from 10 → 220 would push total registered nodes from ~40-55 to ~250-265 — roughly a 5-6× multiplier on a cost that is already the single most expensive thing in the per-block pipeline.**
- **I have not benchmarked actual wall-clock timings** — the "never launch the game" rule means this is architectural reasoning from the code, not a measured number, and I want to be explicit about that distinction rather than pretend precision I don't have.

**The clean long-term fix, if N needs to go well past ~50: decouple "non-miner wallet identity" from "full broadcast-registered `NodeAgent`."** Non-miners are passive holders — recall the pattern `GetAddressBalanceDetails`/`CollectUsedAddressSet` already use (reading balance/UTXO membership directly off the *player's* canonical chain by address, with no need for the queried address to own a registered `NodeAgent` at all). A non-miner could hold just keys (for signing when the exchange scheduler picks it as a sender) without ever being `SharedNetwork.RegisterNode`'d — removing it from the `BroadcastBlock`/`BroadcastTransaction` loops entirely, and building its sends by reading the player's canonical UTXO set instead of its own replicated `.Blockchain`. This decouples "how many non-miners exist" from "how many nodes pay the broadcast cost." **This is a real, scoped refactor, not a small tweak** (`BuildAndBroadcastUtxoSpend` currently reads `sender.Blockchain.GetSpendableUtxos` everywhere) — proposed as its own deferred subphase (§9.7), not bundled into this round.

### 6.3 Round 3 — RESOLVED (2026-07-09). IMPLEMENTED.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| **D-EB.8** | **Non-miner pool size: 10 → 40** (OQ-EB.5, assistant recommendation accepted). `BtcNetworkDataService.NonMinerPoolSize = 40`, `NonMinersPerAddressDecade = 12.183693` (= `(40−1)/3.201`, the corrected peak-decades span — §6.2) — deploys the full 40 by **2017-12-13** (20 by the June-2011 bubble, 30 by the 2013 bubble). `BotWalletRegistry.NonMinerBotCount` raised to match (40) — **the two constants must stay in lockstep**, one sizes the schedule, the other sizes how many real wallet records exist to fill it. **§6.2's decoupling refactor (§9.7) is confirmed as a real option for going further (toward 100–220) later, NOT built now** — the developer wants a **detailed theoretical write-up of that proposal added to ProjectDesignManual**, scheduled for **ND.4** (this plan's last phase), as a documented-but-unbuilt option to weigh then. *(Delivered at ND.4, 2026-07-10: `Documentation/ProjectDesignManual.md` Ch. 36 §36.6.)* |
| **D-EB.9** | **Auction window: 180 days (6 months) → 100 in-game days** (OQ-EB.6, confirmed value, not tied to any specific N). `NetworkRoot.AuctionWindowMs = 100L × 86,400,000L`. |
| **D-EB.10** | **Front-loaded/same-day intro clustering at N=40 is ACCEPTED** (OQ-EB.7) — historically motivated (the real address count did jump fast right after Mt. Gox opened); no minimum-spacing rule added. |

**Implemented (2026-07-09)**: `BtcNetworkDataService.cs` (pool size + density constant, with the corrected 3.201-decade provenance in the comment), `BotWalletRegistry.cs` (`NonMinerBotCount` 10→40, with an explicit comment on the lockstep requirement and the no-retroactive-growth caveat for existing registries), `NetworkRoot.cs` (`AuctionWindowMs` → 100 days). `dotnet build`: 0 warnings / 0 errors. Schedule re-verified offline against the live CSV with the EXACT hardcoded constants — reproduces the predicted 2017-12-13 full deployment precisely.

**⚠️ Migration note for the developer's next launch**: an EXISTING `bot_wallet_registry.json` (from before this change) has only 10 non-miner records — it does **not** grow retroactively (there's no `AddNonMinerBot` equivalent, deliberately, per D-EB.3's original "no new machinery needed" reasoning, now revisited at fixed N=40 instead of continuous growth). Delete `bot_wallet_registry.json` (or the whole `user://` folder, as usual) before playtesting the new pool size, or the schedule will target 40 intro slots while only 10 have a real bot behind them.

### 6.4 Deferred to ND.4: the §6.2 decoupling proposal as a documented option

Per D-EB.8, this plan's **last phase (ND.4)** must add a **detailed theoretical proposal** to `Documentation/ProjectDesignManual.md` covering the §6.2 decoupling refactor (non-miner wallet identity separated from full broadcast-registered `NodeAgent`, reading balance/UTXO state off the player's canonical chain instead of a per-node replica) — written as a fully-specified, NOT-YET-BUILT option for later scaling the non-miner pool past 40 (toward 100–220) without the `O(registered nodes × chain length)` cost this round's investigation found. This is a documentation deliverable, tracked in ND.4's checklist (§3.4) alongside the rest of that phase's docs pass.
