## 7. ND.5 — Recruitable Bidding Details: SC Cashback Settlement for Auction Donors (✅ IMPLEMENTED, 2026-07-10 — ND.5a…e all built; ✅ calibration playtest per §7.5 PASSED as part of the combined rounds, 2026-07-14/15 — §11)

A new feature requested by the developer once all of ND.0–ND.4d had shipped and been playtested: a per-non-miner detail scene showing its recent bid history, culminating in a real SC-for-BTC settlement when its auction resolves — donors get paid back in SC (at the auction's CLOSING valuation, not their own donation-day valuation — corrected by the developer mid-spec, see D-ND5.6) for their tracked bids, and the resulting new referral forwards the equivalent BTC to the casino. Every item below is now a locked decision (**D-ND5.N**); the four items originally flagged as open questions were resolved by the developer on 2026-07-10, one of them (OQ-ND5.2) correcting an earlier misreading of the spec — see D-ND5.4's rewrite. Nothing in this section is built yet; this is the spec for whoever implements it.

### 7.1 Naming correction (required, not optional)

**D-ND5.1**: The developer's working name, "ReclutableBiddingDetails," mixes Spanish ("Reclutable") into a code-facing identifier — CLAUDE.md's Language Policy is explicit that "all project files, source code, UI text, code-facing names, and documentation inside the repository must be in English," Spanish being reserved for chat/planning only. This plan uses **`AuctioningCompanyDetails`** (scene + controller name) throughout — the literal English equivalent, same meaning, same acronym-free readability. Flagging this rather than silently translating, since it's the one place this spec deviates from the developer's own wording.

### 7.2 What the scene shows, and the two valuation moments (do not conflate them)

**D-ND5.2 — access gate**: a new button appears in BlockExplorer's Enroll Mode, on the same line as a non-miner's existing leading-bid info, but ONLY for non-miners that have received at least one bid (`Status == InAuction` AND `LeadingBidUnixMs != 0` — i.e., exactly the population that already shows a "leading bid" line today, per §22.7/22.8). A non-miner with no bids yet ("awaiting first bid — no countdown") gets no button — there is nothing to show.

**D-ND5.3 — the tracked pool is a GLOBAL top-10-by-value ranking, not a per-donor list**: the scene shows up to **10 qualifying donations** this non-miner has ever received while in-auction — a single shared pool competed for by EVERY qualifying donor together (player and `bot_1..4` all rank against each other in the SAME pool; there is no per-donor reservation of slots). Each row shows: the BTC amount, the donor's address, and that donation's **LIVE, current SC value** (`BtcMarketDataService`'s price as of NOW, recomputed fresh on every refresh — the same live-valuation convention §22.7/D-ND4b.10 uses for the leading-bid display). **Corrected 2026-07-11**: the original text here said "own day-of-donation SC value... a point-in-time valuation" — that was a writing mistake the developer caught late during playtesting; NOTHING in this system displays or computes a value frozen at a historical day, in ANY use, EXCEPT settlement itself (§7.3, D-ND5.6), which deliberately revalues at the CLOSING date instead. **This per-row value is purely informational, live, and never re-priced as anything else — it is NOT the value used at settlement.**

**D-ND5.4 — eviction is VALUE-RANKED, not chronological FIFO (corrected 2026-07-10 — supersedes an earlier draft of this section that read the spec as FIFO)**: every qualifying donation (win-or-lose, per resolved OQ-ND5.1 below) competes for one of the 10 slots purely by **BTC principal amount**, regardless of who sent it or when. Concretely: if fewer than 10 donations are currently tracked, a new one simply joins the pool; once 10 are tracked, a new donation joins ONLY if its BTC amount is strictly greater than the current SMALLEST tracked amount, which it then evicts (a tie does not evict — first-in stays). **The BTC from an evicted (or never-tracked) donation becomes the non-miner's own property, permanently** — excluded from both the eventual SC refund and the eventual BTC-to-casino sweep (§7.3) forever, even after the non-miner becomes a referral. The developer's original phrase "las primeras van desapareciendo" described the common CASE (in an ascending auction, older bids are usually also the smallest, so they usually ARE the ones evicted) but not the actual RULE, which is value, not order — confirmed explicitly: "sus donaciones aparecen comparadas con donaciones de otros casino-miners o player y solo quedan las 10 mas valiosas sin importar de quien sean."

- **OQ-ND5.1 — RESOLVED (2026-07-10)**: the tracked pool includes EVERY qualifying donation, both ones that became leader at the time and ones that failed to clear the raise threshold — confirmed, no distinction between "winning" and "losing" bids for tracking purposes.
- **OQ-ND5.2 — RESOLVED (2026-07-10), the correction above**: donations from the SAME donor compete on equal footing against every other donor's donations for the shared 10 slots (D-ND5.3/5.4) — there is no per-donor slot reservation. **At settlement**, all of a given donor's donations that ended up inside the final tracked pool (D-ND5.7, unchanged by this correction) are summed and paid together — "se cuentan todas las donaciones que hizo CADA donador dentro de la lista de 10 últimas general y recibe la paga solo por las donaciones que ESE donador hizo y que esté dentro de las 10 últimas general."

### 7.3 Settlement: what happens the instant an auction resolves

**D-ND5.5 — trigger**: unchanged from §22.7 — the SAME 20-day rolling window, the SAME ratchet-determined leader, the SAME `Resolved` transition. This section adds NEW side effects to that transition; it does not change when or whether a non-miner resolves.

**D-ND5.6 — settlement valuation (CORRECTED mid-spec by the developer)**: every donation still inside the tracked pool (≤10, per §7.2, now value-ranked per D-ND5.4) at the moment of resolution is revalued using the **CLOSING date's** BTC/SC price — the resolution block's own game-calendar date — applied UNIFORMLY to all of them, regardless of each donation's own original date. This is a deliberate departure from §7.2's per-row LIVE display (which always shows the CURRENT/now value, purely informational, corrected 2026-07-11 — see §7.2's own correction note) — **the two valuation moments serve different purposes and must not be conflated in implementation**: the list view answers "what is this bid worth right now"; the settlement answers "what is this bid worth today, the day the auction actually closes."

**D-ND5.7 — per-donor SC payout**: for each UNIQUE donor with one or more donations still in the tracked pool, sum their tracked BTC principal and pay them `sum × closingDatePrice` in SC, funded by the casino (funding mechanism resolved at D-ND5.4b below). A donor with several tracked donations gets one aggregated payout, not several — the closing price is the same for all of them anyway, so aggregating first is equivalent to paying per-donation and summing, just simpler.

**D-ND5.8 — BTC sweep to casino, after all SC payouts complete**: once every tracked donor has been paid, the non-miner (now confirmed as the new permanent referral of the leading bidder, exactly as §22.7 already does) sends the TOTAL BTC of the (≤10) tracked pool to the casino in one transfer. Per D-ND5.4, any BTC from donations evicted (or never tracked) stays with the non-miner forever — the sweep only ever touches what's still in the tracked pool at the exact moment of resolution.

- **OQ-ND5.3 — RESOLVED (2026-07-10, "vamos con tus recomendaciones")**: the network fee on the non-miner's BTC-to-casino sweep is deducted FROM the swept total (casino receives `windowTotal − fee`), the same convention every other send in this engine already follows (`BuildAndBroadcastUtxoSpend`'s `amount + fee ≤ spendable` gate) — the casino's received BTC ends up a hair short of the SC total it just paid out, and **this is accepted, not a bug to fix**: "no importa que casino pierda un poco en el cambio." **Documentation requirement (developer's explicit instruction)**: ProjectDesignManual must state this shortfall plainly as a known, accepted asymmetry, flagged as a candidate to revisit later if it ever matters (e.g. if a rank/commission system needs the books to balance exactly).

**D-ND5.10 (architectural, not really optional — the concrete mechanism this needs)**: `ComputeAuctionLedger` today is a PURE, side-effect-free function, called freely and repeatedly from UI refreshes (Enroll Mode, the BTCWallet warning, the casino-bot cycle's own per-block snapshot). Settlement (paying SC, sending BTC) is a REAL state-changing event that must fire **exactly once** per non-miner's resolution, not once per UI refresh. The correct hook, matching the engine's existing "a block is the only commit" architecture: a new `NetworkRoot.TrySettleResolvedAuctions(block)` step, called once per live block from `HandleMinedBlock` (the same place `ScheduleBotTransactionsAfterBlock`/`TryDistributePendingCasinoRewards` already run) — it diffs the CURRENT block's `ComputeAuctionLedger` against the PREVIOUS block's, finds any non-miner whose status just flipped `InAuction → Resolved` this block, and runs the settlement (§7.3) for exactly those newly-resolved non-miners, exactly once. No new persisted state is needed for the diff itself — the previous block's ledger can be recomputed on demand from the previous block's timestamp, consistent with "nothing between blocks persists."

- **OQ-ND5.4 — RESOLVED (2026-07-10)**: the SC-crediting mechanism is confirmed for the player, and the SAME mechanism is confirmed to apply identically to `bot_1..4` ("igual que se propone para Player, así mismo debe funcionar para pagar a los casino-miner-bots también") — with one refinement over the original proposal: funding comes from **`CasinoScBalanceService.MainBalance` ONLY, never Bankroll**. **D-ND5.4b (the funding mechanism)**: before paying any tracked donor, if `MainBalance` can't cover the TOTAL settlement (the sum of every unique donor's payout for this resolution), the casino draws an **on-demand auto-loan** into Main — reusing the exact same on-demand bankruptcy-flavor pattern `CasinoScBalanceService` already has for Bankroll recharges (`AutoLoanAmount` chunks, `LoanCount++`/`TotalLoaned +=` per chunk, looping if a single chunk isn't enough), just retargeted to a new trigger condition (an auction settlement shortfall, not a Bankroll-dose exhaustion). Once Main can cover the total, each unique donor is paid: **player** via `PrincipalBalanceService` + a new `CasinoClientLedgerService` entry (`Kind = "auction_payout"`, visible in `ClientsTransactions`/`ScTransactions`); **`bot_1..4`** via `NodeFinancialState.PrincipalBalance` (`GetOrCreateNodeFinancialState`/`SetNodeFinancialState`, the existing bot SC-balance primitive) — the identical funding source and loan-fallback rule for both recipient types. `CasinoScBalanceService.MainBalance` is debited by the total paid out either way; `CumulativeProfitSinceLoan` (`TotalSc − TotalLoaned`) reflects both the loan and the payout automatically, through the existing formula — no new metric needed.

### 7.4 The `AuctioningCompanyDetails` scene itself

**D-ND5.9 — scene lifecycle**: the scene is entered from the Enroll Mode button (§7.2), targeting one specific non-miner (passed via a small static hand-off field on the scene's own controller — e.g. `AuctioningCompanyDetails.PendingNonMinerAddress`, set by BlockExplorer immediately before `SceneManager.Go`, read in `_Ready()`; no existing cross-scene parameter-passing convention was found elsewhere in the codebase to reuse, so this is a new, minimal, self-contained pattern, not an extension of `SceneManager`). While the non-miner is still `InAuction`, the scene shows the live tracked-donation list (§7.2), auto-refreshing like BlockExplorer's own Enroll Mode. **If the non-miner resolves WHILE the scene is open** (detected by the scene's own refresh noticing the status flip, driven by the SAME `TrySettleResolvedAuctions` side effect that already ran in the background — the scene never triggers settlement itself, it only ever displays the result), the view switches to a **settlement summary**: each paid donor + their SC amount, and the BTC total swept to casino. The scene's only exit is a Back button; once left, it is **permanently unreachable** for that non-miner — Enroll Mode's own button only ever appears for `InAuction` non-miners with a leader (D-ND5.2), so a `Resolved` entry never offers the button again. "The scene gets deleted" (the developer's phrasing) is therefore not a special mechanic to build — it is the natural, already-existing consequence of the button's own visibility gate; no explicit scene-destruction code is needed beyond Godot's normal scene-unload-on-navigate-away behavior.

### 7.5 Phase checklist (proposed)

- [x] **ND.5a — settlement architecture**: `NetworkRoot.TrySettleResolvedAuctions(block)` (D-ND5.10) wired into `HandleMinedBlock`; the tracked-pool derivation (D-ND5.3/5.4 — the value-ranked top-10, NOT chronological FIFO) added to `ComputeAuctionLedger`'s output (`NonMinerDonationSummary.TrackedDonations`, maintained by `ComputeTrackedDonationPool` as a running min-eviction pool as each qualifying donation arrives); the previous-block-vs-current-block resolution-diff logic (recomputes `ComputeAuctionLedger` against `chain[^2].Timestamp`, no new persisted state).
- [x] **ND.5b — settlement execution**: per-donor SC payout (D-ND5.6/5.7) + the on-demand Main-only auto-loan funding fallback (`CasinoScBalanceService.PayFromMainWithAutoLoan`, D-ND5.4b) + the BTC-to-casino sweep (D-ND5.8, fee deducted from the total per D-ND5.4's OQ-ND5.3 resolution), all firing from `SettleResolvedAuction`/`TrySettleResolvedAuctions`. New `CasinoClientLedgerService.RegisterAuctionPayout` (`Kind = "auction_payout"`). `CasinoScBalanceService.MainBalance` debited by the total paid out. `user://logs/auction_settlement_trace.csv` telemetry added.
- [x] **ND.5c — `AuctioningCompanyDetails` scene**: the live tracked-donation list (§7.2) + the settlement-summary view (§7.4, D-ND5.9, backed by the new pure `NetworkRoot.GetAuctionSettlementSummary`), the static hand-off parameter pattern (`AuctioningCompanyDetails.PendingNonMinerAddress`), auto-refresh mirroring BlockExplorer's Enroll Mode.
- [x] **ND.5d — Enroll Mode wiring**: the new per-non-miner "Details →" button (D-ND5.2, gated on `LeadingBidUnixMs != 0`) — Enroll Mode's single RichTextLabel blob was refactored into a scrollable per-row `VBoxContainer` to host it; `SceneManager` entry (new `SceneId.AuctioningCompanyDetails`), navigation back to BlockExplorer.
- [x] **ND.5e — docs pass**: CLAUDE.md (extended the Referral auction canonical decision row + a new Implemented bullet + Navigation Map/File Organization entries), ProjectDesignManual (new §22.9 — includes OQ-ND5.3's resolution's documentation requirement, the fee-shortfall note flagged for possible future revisit), GLOSSARY (new terms *Tracked Donation Pool*, *Auction Settlement*), this plan → COMPLETE.
- [ ] `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors (✅ verified after each subphase) + a calibration playtest (mirroring ND.4a/ND.4's format: watch a non-miner accumulate tracked donations — including at least one eviction of a smaller earlier donation by a larger later one, to prove the ranking is value-based not chronological — resolve it, verify the SC payouts land on the right accounts at the closing-date rate, verify the auto-loan fires correctly if Main can't cover the total, verify the BTC sweep amount, verify the scene's live-vs-summary switch) — **left for the developer**, per the 21 Mar 2010 `TimelineConfig.DevEntryYear` bootstrap already set up for this round of testing.

## 8. ND.6 — Casino-Miner Bidding Refinement: the Saturation Ladder (✅ SPEC LOCKED 2026-07-12 — OQ-ND6.1…8 all resolved; canonical decision set D-ND6.1…10 in §8.5; ✅ IMPLEMENTED — ND.6a…c built, then refined during the calibration rounds: ND.6d two-mode ladder 2026-07-14, ND.6e urgency window 2026-07-15; combined playtest PASSED per §11. Known residual: bots-only bid flow still stalls without player participation — that is subphase ND.8d, §12)

### 8.0 Motivating incident, and what this supersedes

Two problems surfaced in the developer's latest playtest of the ND.4b/ND.4c auction cycle (with the first-cut "self-competition avoidance rules 1–3" of 2026-07-11, currently unstaged in `FilterAndPrioritizeTargetsForBot`):

1. **The game stalls once all bots participate.** Under rules 1–3 as built, a bot is hard-excluded from any pool where it holds a top-5 tracked slot. With only 4 bots and 10 tracked slots per pool, the system converges on a **deterministic absorbing state**: every bot ends up holding a top-5 slot in every open auction, every bot's eligible-target list goes empty, and no casino-bot bid can EVER fire again. This is not a tuning problem — it is structural. A hard rank-filter with no probabilistic escape has exactly one long-run destination: silence.
2. **A player leading bid always wins.** Downstream of (1): once the bots are frozen, the player's bid rides its 20-day window uncontested, every time. There is currently no mechanism that ever challenges the player's lead in the saturated state.

The developer's new ruleset below **supersedes rules 1–3 wholesale** (the top-5 hard filter, the new-vs-retake two-group priority, and the no-fallback rule are all replaced or absorbed by it). The one piece that survives unchanged is the slot pipeline's shape: bot chosen FIRST, then that bot searches its own rules-filtered target list.

**Stall diagnosis confirmed (OQ-ND6.8, 2026-07-12)**: "el juego se queda estancado" = the AUCTION DYNAMIC freezing (item 1 above — zero bot bids in the saturated state), NOT an application hang. The developer found no technical bugs in the playtest — nothing separate to hunt before this rework.

### 8.1 The developer's ruleset, restated (P-ND6.1…6 — historical restatement of 2026-07-11; every ambiguity flagged below was resolved on 2026-07-12 — **§8.5's D-ND6.1…10 is the canonical spec** wherever the two differ)

- **P-ND6.1 — selection fairness is unconditional**: each donation slot picks its bot uniformly at random (1/4 among not-yet-used-this-block bots), and a bot keeps its full selection probability even when its own rules will end up producing NO donation. A chosen bot that declines (rule-blocked, or a failed ladder roll) consumes the slot — **no other bot substitutes** — with the single exception of P-ND6.6's affordability cascade. (This matches the unstaged code's existing shape; it becomes an explicit locked rule here.)
- **P-ND6.2 — unparticipated pools have first priority**: if the chosen bot has NO participation in some currently-recruitable (InAuction) non-miner, that non-miner is its first donation priority (spread wide before ever re-bidding). [Definition of "participation" → OQ-ND6.1; ordering among several unparticipated pools → presumably the existing soonest-to-expire-then-awaiting-first-bid order of `priorityTargets`.]
- **P-ND6.3 — the saturation ladder (the core new mechanic)**: when the chosen bot already has a participation in EVERY recruitable non-miner, it considers ONE re-bid, in a pool where its position has slipped below the top of the tracked ranking, with a probability that grows as its position sinks:

  | Bot's position in that pool | Re-bid probability | Declines |
  |---|---|---|
  | below the top 3 | **5%** | 95% |
  | below the top 4 | **8%** | 92% |
  | below the top 5 | **13%** | 87% |
  | below the top 6 | **21%** | 79% |
  | below the top 7 | **34%** | 66% |
  | below the top 8 | **55%** | 45% |
  | below the top 9 | **89%** | 11% |

  On a failed roll the bot simply does not donate this slot, and **never** does another bot substitute for a failed roll. A bot holding a top-3 position in a pool never re-bids there at all — top-3 is the "satisfied" state. [Nested-tier vs exact-rank interpretation → OQ-ND6.2 — this table is deliberately transcribed as the developer wrote it, cumulative-sounding "below the top k" conditions included.]
- **P-ND6.4 — the ladder percentages are consecutive Fibonacci numbers** (5, 8, 13, 21, 34, 55, 89 — noted here so no future reader "corrects" them into a smooth curve): each tier is ≈ φ (1.618×) the previous, giving a naturally accelerating urgency as a bot sinks in the ranking. The next Fibonacci number (144) would exceed 100% — the ladder terminates exactly where the 10-slot pool does. Whether intentional numerology or intuition, it is a well-shaped escalation curve and should be kept as a named constant table, not derived from a formula.
- **P-ND6.5 — the double-participation constraint**: when the chosen bot already has **2 participations in every recruitable pool**, it only bids in a pool where (a) it does NOT hold one of the top-3 positions, and (b) it does NOT hold the 10th (smallest) tracked slot — because its own new bid, entering a full pool, would evict its own smallest donation, forfeiting a settlement refund it has already secured as the auction currently stands (D-ND5.4's eviction-is-forfeiture rule). [The (b) reading is the assistant's reconstruction of a garbled sentence → OQ-ND6.3; whether the ladder probabilities still apply on top → OQ-ND6.4.]
- **P-ND6.6 — half-balance cap + affordability cascade (the ONLY substitution path)**: if the required donation for the priority target exceeds **half the chosen bot's total BTC balance**, that target is skipped and the bot tries its next-priority donations in order; if NOTHING on its list is affordable under the half-balance rule, then — only in this case — the slot may pass to another casino-miner (which re-runs its OWN full pipeline: its own participation state, its own ladder roll, its own half-balance cap), cascading through all four; only if no bot can bid does the slot yield nothing. This replaces the current plain `spendable ≥ required + fee` check with a stricter cap, and re-scopes substitution: **affordability failure cascades; rule-based or roll-based refusal never does.** [Which "total balance" → OQ-ND6.5; interaction with the D-ND4b.11 additive tail → OQ-ND6.6.]

### 8.2 Assistant observations (gameplay + long-range sustainability)

**8.2.1 — Why this fixes the stall, structurally.** The ladder replaces a hard filter (rank ≥ some cutoff ⇒ permanently ineligible) with a probabilistic one (deeper rank ⇒ more likely to re-engage). Probabilistic filters have no absorbing silent state: as long as at least one bot ranks below top-3 somewhere and can afford the raise, a challenge eventually fires. The two mechanisms that END an auction under this regime are both organic:
- **top-3 satisfaction**: with 5 qualifying bidders (player + 4 bots) and only 3 "satisfied" seats, at most 3 participants can go quiet at once — but as escalation proceeds, the largest bidders occupy the top-3 and stop, shrinking the challenger set;
- **the half-balance cap**: raises grow geometrically (×1.1–1.2 per bot raise), bot balances grow only by mining income, so every bot eventually prices out. The auction's true terminator becomes **economic** (bot wealth) rather than purely **temporal** (window expiry) — a much more interesting endgame than "whoever bid last before everyone froze."

**8.2.2 — Quantifying the anti-"player always wins" pressure.** Approximate figures, useful as a calibration baseline (block pace `TargetBlockSeconds = 58,500` in-game s ⇒ a 20-day window ≈ **29.5 blocks**; `E[donation slots/block] = 1.0` from the 15/70/15 draw; a given bot is chosen for a slot with probability ≈ 0.25/block):

  | Bot's exact rank in the contested pool | Expected challenges per 20-day window (one bot) | P(that bot stays silent a full window) |
  |---|---|---|
  | 4 | ≈ 0.37 | ≈ 69% |
  | 5 | ≈ 0.59 | ≈ 55% |
  | 6 | ≈ 0.96 | ≈ 38% |
  | 7 | ≈ 1.55 | ≈ 21% |
  | 8 | ≈ 2.51 | ≈ 8% |
  | 9 | ≈ 4.06 | ≈ 1.7% |
  | 10 | ≈ 6.57 | ≈ 0.1% |

  Worst case for competition — all four bots saturated at the shallowest ladder tier (5%) — still yields a combined ≈ 1.5 challenges/window, i.e. the player's "bid once and wait" strategy survives a full window uncontested only ≈ 23% of the time (vs **100% today**). In any mixed-rank state a challenge is near-certain. This is meaningful pressure without being oppressive: at ranks 4–6, individual bots challenge roughly once per one-to-three windows — the player mostly wins the auctions they actively defend, and loses the ones they abandon. That is exactly the "interesting equilibrium" shape.

**8.2.3 — The ladder + rolling window = potentially very long auctions.** Every accepted raise resets the 20-day window (D-ND4b.8). A deep-ranked rich bot at 89% effectively always re-bids when chosen (~6.6 challenges/window), so a contested pool among wealthy bots can stay open for many consecutive windows — potentially game-years — until half-balance caps bite. This may be desirable (marquee auctions become long-running background dramas) but should be watched in the calibration playtest: if it overshoots, the gentlest brakes are (a) shaving the top ladder tiers (89 → 55, 55 → 34), or (b) Option B below (urgency-weighting), which concentrates the same challenges near expiry instead of spreading them.

**8.2.4 — Three competitive eras emerge, for free.** (i) **Early** (few pools): bots saturate participation fast → ladder regime dominates → high per-pool pressure. (ii) **Mid** (intros arriving steadily until all 40 land, empirically 2017-12-13): every new non-miner intro diverts saturated bots back to P-ND6.2's spread-first priority — new intros act as **pressure-release valves** on contested pools. (iii) **Late** (intros exhausted, pools resolving): pure ladder regime over a shrinking recruitable set, against declining bot wealth (see 8.2.5). The auction game's texture changes across the timeline without any era-specific code.

**8.2.5 — The long-range sustainability problem this ruleset does NOT solve: structural bot BTC drain.** Every resolved auction sweeps the tracked pool's BTC to the casino (D-ND5.8); tracked donors are refunded **in SC**, not BTC (D-ND5.7). Bots hold SC in `NodeFinancialState` but have **no SC→BTC path** — so each auction cycle is a one-way pump: bot BTC → casino treasury, bot SC ↑. Meanwhile `bot_1..4`'s mining share (their only BTC income) shrinks steadily as the Step-14 cast + invisible mass grow through the decades curve. Compounding: the player CAN buy BTC at the swap desk. Left alone, the late game re-derives "player always wins" through economics instead of rules — bots too poor to clear half-balance caps on any meaningful raise. **Recommended structural fix (deferrable, flagged now): let `bot_1..4` use the swap desk** — they are casino clients with growing SC balances, and `CasinoCoinSwapService.GetSwapFeePercentFor(clientId)` was explicitly built as the per-client hook. Settlement SC → swap desk → BTC → new bids closes the loop elegantly AND gives the swap desk organic non-player volume. Alternatives: casino "sponsorship" redistribution of swept BTC; or accepting the late-game player dominance as intended difficulty easing. This does not block ND.6 — but it should be a named deferred item (proposed: §9.8) rather than an unexamined assumption.

**8.2.6 — Self-eviction deserves to be a general guard, not only P-ND6.5's special case.** The forfeiture mechanic (your own new bid evicting your own smallest tracked donation from a FULL pool) applies whenever a bot holds the current smallest slot of a full pool — regardless of how many participations it has elsewhere. Two coherent stances: (a) **generalize the guard** (never bid into a full pool where your own donation is the smallest — conservative, protects bots from wasting BTC); or (b) **allow it knowingly** — evicting your own 0.1 BTC floor bid to place a leading 5 BTC bid is often net-positive (lose a small refund, gain the lead + a large refunded principal). Stance (b) is strategically richer and arguably what a competent bidder would do; stance (a) is simpler and matches the developer's protective intent in P-ND6.5. Worth an explicit decision (OQ-ND6.3 covers the reading; this covers the scope).

**8.2.7 — Eviction naturally recycles interest — if "participation" is defined as CURRENTLY TRACKED.** If a bot's donations get fully evicted from a pool, the tracked-slot definition of participation (OQ-ND6.1's option a) makes that pool "unparticipated" again → P-ND6.2 first priority → the bot re-engages as if new. This is organic and self-balancing (the pools that most outgrew a bot's early bids are exactly the hot ones), and it falls out of the definition for free. The "ever donated" definition (option b) instead parks evicted bots in the ladder's deepest tier. Both defensible; (a) recommended for its emergent re-engagement.

**8.2.8 — Interplay with the player's 1-satoshi raise privilege (ND.4d).** A ladder challenge computes its raise off the LEADING bid. When the player min-raises by 1 satoshi, the next bot challenge still jumps 10–20% over essentially the pre-player amount — so cheap player raises do not slow bot escalation, they just insert the player at the top between bot jumps. Consequence: under the ladder, the player's cheapest viable strategy shifts from "bid once, wait" to "min-raise after each bot challenge" — costing one satoshi per exchange while bots escalate each other's floor. That is a genuinely fun risk-management loop (each exchange resets the window, and the player must stay attentive), but note it means an attentive player still wins almost every auction they care about, spending almost nothing — the ladder defeats ABSENT players, not ACTIVE ones. If that asymmetry is too soft, the knob is NOT the player floor (ND.4d is deliberate) but auction fatigue: see Option D below.

### 8.3 Balance options (equilibrium-shaping knobs, combinable)

- **Option A — ship the ruleset as specified** (exact-rank Fibonacci ladder, half-balance cap, affordability-only cascade), all numbers as named tunable constants (`ReBidProbabilityPercentByRank` table, `MaxBidBalanceFraction = 0.5m`). Recommended as the v1 — the 8.2.2 numbers suggest it already lands in a good band, and the calibration playtest should measure before more mechanics are added.
- **Option B — urgency-weighted ladder**: multiply each rolled probability by a time factor that grows as the window nears expiry (e.g. `0.5 + 0.5 × elapsed/window`). Same expected per-window pressure, but challenges cluster near the deadline — organic "auction sniping" drama, and fewer mid-window resets means shorter total auction lifetimes (directly addresses 8.2.3). Cheap to add later without re-tuning the table.
- **Option C — wealth-aware aggression**: scale the rolled probability by affordability headroom (e.g. ×`min(1, spendable / (4 × requiredAmount))`), so rich bots push hard and poor bots conserve. Smooths the 8.2.5 decline (poor bots fade gradually instead of hitting the half-balance wall) but partially masks the structural drain rather than fixing it.
- **Option D — post-exchange escalation memory (anti-min-raise fatigue)**: if the same pool has seen ≥ N leader changes within its current window, bump every bot's ladder tier up one step there (competition begets competition). Directly counters 8.2.8's "attentive player wins for satoshis" softness by making actively-contested pools progressively hotter. Slightly more state to derive (leader-change count is already chain-derivable from the ledger walk — no persistence needed).
- **Option E — per-bot challenge cooldown**: a bot that placed any bid in the last N blocks halves its ladder probabilities. Prevents high-tier burst sequences (a rank-10 bot firing on 3 consecutive chosen slots) and spaces challenges out to read as deliberation rather than spam. Cosmetic-organic, low cost.

Composition **LOCKED (D-ND6.10, 2026-07-12)**: **A now; B and D as the first two pre-approved calibration levers if the playtest shows (respectively) auctions running too long or the min-raise strategy feeling too safe.** C and E only if specific pathologies appear.

### 8.4 Open questions — ✅ ALL RESOLVED (developer, 2026-07-12; folded into §8.5's locked set)

- **OQ-ND6.1 — participation definition**: **RESOLVED → (a)** — holds ≥1 CURRENTLY TRACKED donation in that pool's top-10 (chain-derived; eviction recycles interest per 8.2.7). Recruitable set = all currently `InAuction` (awaiting-first-bid included), `Resolved` excluded. → D-ND6.2.
- **OQ-ND6.2 — nested tiers vs exact rank; roll mechanics; pool targeting**: **RESOLVED** — nested/exact are two names for the same mapping; either works, but the word **"rank" is banned for this feature** (reserved for the future casino ranking system) — use **"tier"** with the same meaning. Roll mechanics: **NOT one roll per slot position** — exactly **ONE roll per donation slot**, made on the tier with the **LOWEST re-bid probability** among the bot's own tracked slots in the ONE selected pool (e.g. a bot holding 4th-tier and 7th-tier slots in `non-miner-3`'s pool rolls the 4th tier's 5%, never the 7th's 21%). Pool targeting among several qualifying pools: **soonest-to-expire among qualifying** — but selection starts FROM THE DONOR BOT (see OQ-ND6.4's resolution: fewest-own-participations preference first, expiry as the tie-break). Never re-rolled pool-by-pool. → D-ND6.3/6.4/6.5/6.6.
- **OQ-ND6.3 — P-ND6.5(b) reading**: **RESOLVED → the self-eviction guard** (a bot never bids into a full pool where its own donation holds the smallest tracked slot — the bid would evict its own donation and forfeit its secured refund). → D-ND6.7.
- **OQ-ND6.4 — ladder inside the double-participation state; mixed states**: **RESOLVED** — the ladder probability IS still rolled there, under the already-defined exclusions: **tiers 1st/2nd/3rd (satisfied) and 10th (self-eviction guard) excluded**. Mixed states are governed **from the bot, not from the pool**: a chosen bot prefers donating where it holds FEWER positions than elsewhere (so all bots naturally spread themselves in), then within the selected pool it rolls once on its lowest-probability tier. → D-ND6.5/6.6/6.7.
- **OQ-ND6.5 — "half the total BTC balance"**: **RESOLVED → half of the SPENDABLE balance** (mature/confirmed — what `GetAddressSpendableBalance` returns). → D-ND6.8.
- **OQ-ND6.6 — cap bounds the additive tail too**: **RESOLVED → yes**: `required + tail + fee ≤ spendable × MaxBidBalanceFraction`. → D-ND6.8.
- **OQ-ND6.7 — substitution bookkeeping**: **RESOLVED** — the original chosen bot that cascades away is **NOT** marked used-this-block (it did nothing); the substitute that actually donates marks itself used as normal. → D-ND6.9.
- **OQ-ND6.8 — stall diagnosis**: **RESOLVED → confirmed as the auction-dynamic freeze** (8.0's item 1); no technical bugs found — nothing separate to hunt. → noted in §8.0.

### 8.5 Locked specification (D-ND6.1…10 — 2026-07-12, canonical)

- **D-ND6.1 — selection fairness is unconditional; declines consume the slot.** Each donation slot picks its bot uniformly at random (1/4 among not-yet-used-this-block bots); a bot keeps its full selection probability even when its own rules end up producing NO donation. A chosen bot that declines (rule-excluded target list, or a failed ladder roll) consumes the slot — no substitution — with the single exception of D-ND6.9's affordability cascade. (P-ND6.1 confirmed.)
- **D-ND6.2 — participation = currently tracked.** A bot "participates" in a pool iff it holds ≥1 CURRENTLY TRACKED donation in that pool's top-10 (`NonMinerDonationSummary.TrackedDonations`). Full eviction of a bot's donations makes the pool "unparticipated" again — the 8.2.7 re-engagement recycling is intended behavior. Recruitable set = all currently `InAuction` non-miners (awaiting-first-bid included), `Resolved` excluded.
- **D-ND6.3 — terminology: "tier", never "rank".** A tracked slot's position in a pool's value ranking is its **tier** (tier 1 = largest donation … tier 10 = smallest slot of a full pool). The word "rank" is reserved for the future (yet-undesigned) casino ranking system and must not appear in this feature's code, constants, telemetry, or docs. (§8.2's earlier analysis predates this decision and keeps "rank" as historical record only.)
- **D-ND6.4 — the saturation ladder, exact-tier mapping.** `tier 4 → 5%`, `tier 5 → 8%`, `tier 6 → 13%`, `tier 7 → 21%`, `tier 8 → 34%`, `tier 9 → 55%` — consecutive Fibonacci percentages kept as a literal named constant table (`ReBidProbabilityPercentByTier`), never derived from a formula (P-ND6.4 carried over). Tiers 1–3 have no ladder entry (satisfied state, D-ND6.7). **Amended at ND.6c (developer, 2026-07-12): the sequence's next value — `tier 10 → 89%` — is REMOVED from the table**, since D-ND6.7's self-eviction guard makes a tier-10 best slot unreachable before any roll (it was initially shipped guard-shadowed at ND.6a; the developer chose deletion over dead weight — restore a tier-10 entry only if that guard is ever relaxed). The nested-vs-exact distinction is moot: with D-ND6.5's single-roll rule both phrasings denote this same mapping. **Amended again at ND.6e (developer, 2026-07-15 — Option B exercised): the NORMAL table itself is unchanged, but while a NORMAL pool's rolling window is inside its FINAL 7 in-game days every tier rolls the URGENCY table instead — one Fibonacci level up per tier: `tier 4 → 8%`, `5 → 13%`, `6 → 21%`, `7 → 34%`, `8 → 55%`, `9 → 89%`** (`UrgentReBidProbabilityPercentByTier`; see the ND.6e subphase entry for the calibration finding; ND.6d's EARLY-RUSH table is untouched and ignores urgency).
- **D-ND6.5 — ONE roll per slot, on the bot's best tier in the ONE selected pool.** After the chosen bot selects its single target pool (D-ND6.6): if the pool is unparticipated, the donation is deterministic (no roll — first-time bids need no ladder, as today). If participated, the bot makes exactly ONE ladder roll using, among ITS OWN tracked slots in that pool, the tier with the LOWEST re-bid probability (= its shallowest/best slot — holding tiers 4 and 7 rolls 5%, never 21%). A failed roll = no donation this slot; never re-rolled against another slot or another pool, never substituted.
- **D-ND6.6 — bot-centric target preference order.** Evaluated from the donor bot: order the qualifying pools by **ascending count of the bot's own tracked slots there** (0-participation pools first — P-ND6.2's spread-wide priority is the count-0 case; a bot naturally prefers pools where it holds fewer positions), ties broken **soonest-to-expire** (consistent with D-ND4b.4; awaiting-first-bid pools — always count-0 — keep their existing order within that group). The half-balance affordability walk (D-ND6.8) skips unaffordable targets in this same order; the first qualifying, affordable pool is THE target for D-ND6.5's single roll.
- **D-ND6.7 — pool-level exclusions: satisfied top-3 + the self-eviction guard, in every participation state.** A pool is never a re-bid target while the bot holds there (a) any top-3 tracked slot (**satisfied**), or (b) the smallest slot of a FULL pool, i.e. tier 10 (**self-eviction guard** — its own new bid would evict its own smallest donation, forfeiting a settlement refund already secured as the auction stands, D-ND5.4's eviction-is-forfeiture). Both exclusions apply whether the bot holds 1, 2, or more slots in that pool — the ladder is the universal probability layer on top (each pool evaluated purely from the bot's own slot set there). **Deliberate consequence**: a bot whose BEST slot is tier 10 by definition holds the 10th slot, so exclusion (b) removes that pool before any roll — which is why the `tier 10 → 89%` ladder row was removed entirely at ND.6c (see D-ND6.4's amendment).
- **D-ND6.8 — half-spendable cap, bounding the FULL send.** "Half the total BTC balance" = half the SPENDABLE balance (mature + confirmed — `GetAddressSpendableBalance`), as `MaxBidBalanceFraction = 0.5m`. The cap bounds the entire outgoing amount: **`required + additive tail (D-ND4b.11) + network fee ≤ spendable × MaxBidBalanceFraction`** — the tail is clamped to fit under the cap, and if `required + fee` alone already exceeds it, the target is skipped (the D-ND6.6 affordability walk). Replaces the plain `spendable ≥ required + fee` check.
- **D-ND6.9 — affordability-only cascade + bookkeeping.** Only when NOTHING on the chosen bot's qualifying list is affordable under D-ND6.8 does the slot pass to another casino-miner (which re-runs its OWN full pipeline — own participation state, own preference order, own roll, own cap), cascading through all four; if no bot can bid, the slot yields nothing. Rule-based or roll-based refusal NEVER cascades (D-ND6.1). Bookkeeping: the bot that cascades away is **not** marked used-this-block; the bot that actually donates marks itself used as normal.
- **D-ND6.10 — composition locked: Option A ships now.** Options B (urgency-weighted ladder) and D (post-exchange escalation memory) are the two pre-approved calibration levers if the playtest shows, respectively, auctions running too long (8.2.3) or the player's min-raise strategy feeling too safe (8.2.8). Options C and E only if their specific pathologies appear. All numbers ship as named tunable constants. **Option B was exercised at ND.6e (2026-07-15)** — implemented as a discrete final-7-days URGENCY table (one Fibonacci level up per tier), not a continuous expiry-scaled curve.

### 8.6 Implementation notes (updated to the locked spec)

- All changes land in the existing slot pipeline: `TryCasinoBotDonateOnce` + a rewritten `FilterAndPrioritizeTargetsForBot` (the unstaged rules 1–3 replaced by the D-ND6 pipeline: qualifying-pool derivation with D-ND6.7's exclusions → D-ND6.6's preference order + D-ND6.8's affordability walk → D-ND6.5's single roll → D-ND6.9's cascade). No new persisted state — every input (tiers, participation, leader, expiry) is already chain-derived from `NonMinerDonationSummary.TrackedDonations`/`ComputeAuctionLedger`, and the RNG is `Random.Shared` like the rest of the cycle. Pattern 2's three questions all still answer "no".
- Constants (all `private const`/`static readonly` beside the existing ND.4b block, tier-named per D-ND6.3): `ReBidProbabilityPercentByTier` (the Fibonacci table as literal values, 89 included though guard-shadowed), `MaxBidBalanceFraction = 0.5m`, `SatisfiedTopTierCount = 3`.
- **Telemetry is mandatory for this one**: probabilistic rules cannot be calibrated from gameplay feel alone. One row per donation SLOT (not just per successful bid) — chosen bot; its outcome state (`unparticipated-target` / `ladder-rolled` / `all-satisfied` / `guard-excluded` / `half-balance-blocked` / `cascaded-to-X` / `no-target`); the selected pool; the bot's own slot tiers there; the rolled tier + its probability + the roll result; required/tail/fee vs the cap; cascade hops. A new `user://logs/casino_bot_bid_trace.csv` mirroring `auction_settlement_trace.csv`'s pattern.
- Calibration playtest additions (on top of §7.5's pending checklist): verify the saturated state now produces challenges at roughly 8.2.2's predicted cadence (read "rank" there as "tier"); verify a player leading bid gets contested within 1–2 windows; verify auctions still RESOLVE (8.2.3's long-auction risk — Option B is the pre-approved lever if not); verify the min-raise loop's feel (8.2.8 — Option D is the lever); watch one bot hit its half-balance cap and confirm the cascade fires exactly once and only for affordability; confirm in the trace that no `rolledTier` row ever reads 10 (the guard must exclude those pools before any roll — the table no longer even has a tier-10 entry, per D-ND6.4's amendment) and that a fully-evicted bot re-engages a pool as unparticipated (D-ND6.2 recycling).

### 8.7 Implementation subphases (ND.6a…c)

- [x] **ND.6a — pipeline rewrite** (`Scripts/BlockchainPort/Simulation/NetworkRoot.cs`, built 2026-07-12): `FilterAndPrioritizeTargetsForBot` (the unstaged self-competition rules 1–3) deleted; `TryCasinoBotDonateOnce` rewritten as the D-ND6.1/6.9 slot driver (fair pick → per-bot pipeline → affordability-only cascade via a `visitedThisSlot` set, decliner never marked used) + the new `TryBuildCasinoBotBid` per-bot pipeline (returns a `CasinoBotSlotOutcome`: `Donated` / `NoQualifyingTarget` / `NothingAffordable` / `RollDeclined` / `BroadcastFailed` — only `NothingAffordable` cascades): per-pool own-tier derivation from `TrackedDonations` (stable value sort, ties keep arrival order), D-ND6.7 exclusions (satisfied top-3 — subsumes old rule 1, since the leader is by construction tier 1 — + the self-eviction guard on the smallest slot of a full pool), D-ND6.6 order (own-slot count ascending via stable `OrderBy`, ties keep the D-ND4b.4 soonest-to-expire-then-awaiting order), D-ND6.8 half-spendable cap (`bidBudgetCap = spendable × MaxBidBalanceFraction`; gate on `required + fee`, RaiseMax principal clamped under it, D-ND4b.11 tail headroom measured against it — full-send bound per OQ-ND6.6), D-ND6.5 single roll on the bot's best (lowest-probability) tier, unparticipated targets deterministic. New constants beside the ND.4b block: `ReBidProbabilityPercentByTier` (literal Fibonacci dictionary — shipped with the guard-shadowed 89 included, then removed at ND.6c per D-ND6.4's amendment), `MaxBidBalanceFraction = 0.5m`, `SatisfiedTopTierCount = 3`, plus `MaxTrackedDonations = 10` hoisted from `ComputeTrackedDonationPool`. "Tier" naming throughout; pre-existing "rank" comment wordings in the auction code swept to "order" (D-ND6.3). `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors.
- [x] **ND.6b — slot telemetry** (built 2026-07-12): `user://logs/casino_bot_bid_trace.csv`, mirroring `auction_settlement_trace.csv`'s writer pattern — one row per BOT VISIT within a donation slot (= one row per slot normally; a D-ND6.9 cascade adds one row per substitute, same `slot` index, ascending `hop`), written from `TryCasinoBotDonateOnce` right where each visit resolves. Columns: `blockTimestampMs,blockIndex,slot,hop,botNodeId,outcome,targetNodeId,ownTiersInTarget,rolledTier,rolledProbabilityPercent,requiredBtc,amountBtc,feeBtc,spendableBtc,bidBudgetCapBtc` — outcome ∈ `donated` / `no-qualifying-target` / `nothing-affordable` / `roll-declined` / `broadcast-failed`; `ownTiersInTarget` pipe-joined (`"4|7"`); `rolledTier = 0` = no roll (unparticipated/deterministic). Detail carried by a new `CasinoBotBidTrace` record filled progressively inside `TryBuildCasinoBotBid`. Both this trace AND `auction_settlement_trace.csv` (found missing since ND.5) added to the world-reset trace delete list. `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors.
- [x] **ND.6c — docs pass** (2026-07-12): CLAUDE.md (Referral-auction canonical row extended with the saturation ladder + a new Implemented bullet), `Documentation/ProjectDesignManual.md` (new §22.10 — the stall, the tier terminology, the ladder table, the 4-step pipeline, the affordability-only cascade, the telemetry, the B/D calibration levers, the §9.8 deferred note), `Documentation/GLOSSARY.md` (*Saturation Ladder*, *Tier* — with the explicit "not the casino ranking system" disambiguation; ND.5-era "value-ranked" wordings swept to "value-ordered" per D-ND6.3), this plan's status line → ND.6 IMPLEMENTED. **Includes the D-ND6.4 amendment (developer decision at this subphase): the guard-shadowed `tier 10 → 89%` entry is REMOVED from `ReBidProbabilityPercentByTier`** (dead weight — the self-eviction guard makes it unreachable; restore `[10] = 89` only if that guard is ever relaxed), with the code comment, §8.5's D-ND6.4/6.7, and §8.6's playtest checklist all updated to match.
- [x] **ND.6d — early probability rush** (`NetworkRoot.cs` + `AuctioningCompanyDetails.cs`, built 2026-07-14): the ~1-in-game-year 2011 calibration playtest confirmed the stall recurred via a NEW door — the player's asymmetric +1-satoshi retakes (§ND.4d) kept pushing every contested bot's best slot **up** to tier 4/5, where the NORMAL 5%/8% roll left them declining ~95% of the time. `casino_bot_bid_trace.csv` (blocks 1050+) was pure `roll-declined` at tier 4 with spendable ~1000 BTC (affordability never the constraint) and **zero** donations, and the player won every referral uncontested. Fix: a SECOND probability table `EarlyRushReBidProbabilityPercentByTier` (`{4:34, 5:55, 6:89}`, developer-specified) applied while a pool holds `< EarlyRushSlotThreshold (7)` occupied tracked slots, reverting to the NORMAL `ReBidProbabilityPercentByTier` at ≥7. Mode is a pure function of the target pool's live `TrackedDonations.Count` — threaded through the `qualifying` tuple to the D-ND6.5 roll via a new `ReBidProbabilityPercentFor(tier, occupiedSlots)` helper; no persisted state, self-correcting as slots fill (a pool in early-rush can hold at most 6 slots, so the roll only ever lands on tier 4/5/6 — no tier 7+ early entry needed). `AuctioningCompanyDetails` now prints each slot's live re-bid chance (`[re-bid NN%]` / `satisfied`) + the pool's current mode in the section title, via the shared public `NetworkRoot.ReBidProbabilityLabel`. Also swept the retired Step-10 `0.10000000` fee placeholder (a cosmetic leftover the same playtest flagged) → `0.00000000` in all four BTC send panels, and made BTCWallet re-default its fee field via `ApplyFeeState()` after a send instead of clearing to empty (which had revealed the stale grey placeholder). `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors.
- [x] **ND.6e — the urgency ladder, Option B exercised** (`NetworkRoot.cs` + `AuctioningCompanyDetails.cs`, built 2026-07-15): the continuing 2011 playtest showed the scarcity recurring through the ND.6d fix's OWN blind side — the early rush keeps YOUNG pools (<7 slots) contested, but once pools mature to NORMAL mode the calm 5%/8%/13% shallow tiers throttled re-bids again, and with 3 pools player-led the bots went near-silent on them. The `casino_bot_bid_trace.csv` tail (blocks ~1005–1119, 120 visits) read: 79 `roll-declined` (66 of them NORMAL tier 4/5/6 at 5%/8%/13%), only 7 rolled re-bids into participated pools (vs 13 deterministic first-time entries), zero `nothing-affordable` (spendables 674–1448 BTC vs required 2–4 BTC on most pools — affordability again never the constraint). Fix (developer-directed, D-ND6.10's Option B rather than a flat table raise): a THIRD table `UrgentReBidProbabilityPercentByTier` (`{4:8, 5:13, 6:21, 7:34, 8:55, 9:89}` — each NORMAL tier's next Fibonacci percentage) rolled instead of NORMAL while the pool's rolling window is inside its **final 7 in-game days** (`AuctionUrgencyWindowMs = 7 days`; shared public predicate `IsAuctionInUrgencyWindow(windowCloseUnixMs, nowMs)`), threading `block.Timestamp` into `TryBuildCasinoBotBid` and the urgency flag through `ReBidProbabilityPercentFor(tier, occupiedSlots, urgent)` / `ReBidProbabilityLabel`. Challenges cluster into an organic late-window "sniping" phase; an accepted raise resets the 20-day window and self-extinguishes the urgency; EARLY-RUSH ignores urgency (steeper at every tier it has); unparticipated first-time bids stay deterministic (no leader ⇒ no window ⇒ never urgent). `AuctioningCompanyDetails` shows the mode (`normal bidding — FINAL-WEEK URGENCY (≤7d left)`) and urgency-aware per-slot `[re-bid NN%]` labels (its local `rank` loop variable renamed `tier` — a pre-existing D-ND6.3 vocabulary violation). Telemetry needs no new column — an urgent roll is visible in `casino_bot_bid_trace.csv` as the shifted `rolledProbabilityPercent` value. D-ND6.4/D-ND6.10 amended in place; CLAUDE.md / ProjectDesignManual §22.10 / GLOSSARY updated. `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors.
- [ ] `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors after each subphase + the §8.6 calibration playtest — **left for the developer** (same `TimelineConfig.DevEntryYear` bootstrap arrangement as ND.5's pending checklist; §9.8's bot SC→BTC replenishment stays the named deferred follow-up). ND.6d's playtest verification (young pools now contested, mode label correct, fee placeholder gone) and ND.6e's (a mature player-led pool re-contested inside its final week; the pool panel's FINAL-WEEK URGENCY title + shifted labels correct; the pool calming back down after an accepted raise) are pending.

## 9. Future / deferred (documented only — NOT in this plan's scope)

- **9.1 Per-era hardware table** (D-14.4 tail): CPU/GPU/FPGA/ASIC-era conversion of hashrate → participant power, replacing the era-agnostic normalization — pending proposal for after Basic Mode v0.1.
- **9.2 Pool history** (D-14.5 tail): competing pools following the historical rhythm on the fractal base; the casino pool and the future player-created pool stay history-exempt by decision.
- **9.3 Post-2025 series extension** (D-14.3 tail): after Basic Mode v0.1, same regeneration path as the price dataset (§1.3 of the step13 plan: re-run with a later end date).
- **9.4 Fee replay implementation**: making `NetworkFeePolicy` consume `fee_mean_btc`/`fee_median_btc` — its own step; direction locked here (D-14.6), data delivered by ND.0. **→ PROMOTED into this plan's scope as ND.7 (§10, spec locked 2026-07-12) — ✅ IMPLEMENTED 2026-07-13; combined auction+fees calibration playtest ✅ PASSED 2026-07-14/15 (§11). ND.7 is finished.**
- **9.5 Visible-cast churn/retirement** (D-14.12 tail — v1 ships spawn-only).
- **9.6 Pools & mining-power rebalancing on top of this step** (D-14.8 tail): the pool layer that resolves the late-game "pools become mandatory" pressure — pool adaptation, competition between pools, and any rebalancing of mining powers — is designed AFTER this scheduler exists, on top of it. Includes P5's era-gated hardware availability (the player-side enforcement of "not much more than baseline", §3.1's interplay note).
- **9.7 Decouple non-miner wallet identity from full `NodeAgent` broadcast registration** (§6.2 tail): the prerequisite for scaling the non-miner pool well past ~50 without the `O(registered nodes × chain length)` cost found during round 3 — read balance/UTXO state off the player's canonical chain (the `GetAddressBalanceDetails`/`CollectUsedAddressSet` pattern) instead of giving every non-miner its own broadcast-synced `.Blockchain` replica.
- **9.8 Bot SC→BTC replenishment (the auction system's long-range fuel)** (§8.2.5 tail): every settled auction drains `bot_1..4`'s BTC into the casino one-way (sweep in BTC, refund in SC), while their mining income shrinks along the decades curve — left alone, the late game re-derives "player always wins" economically. Proposed structural fix: let `bot_1..4` buy BTC at the swap desk with their settlement SC (`GetSwapFeePercentFor(clientId)` is already the per-client hook), closing the loop and giving the desk organic non-player volume. Deferred, but must be decided before the auction economy is declared long-range sustainable.

## 10. ND.7 — Historical Fee Replay: real daily network fees from Market Birth (✅ SPEC LOCKED 2026-07-12; ✅ BUILT 2026-07-13; ✅ combined auction+fees calibration playtest PASSED 2026-07-14/15 (§11) — **ND.7 is FINISHED**, developer commit is the remaining step; the ONE spec amendment made during the build is D-ND7.2's median source, §10.5 ND.7.0)

### 10.0 What this is, and what it supersedes

The last big flat-constant in the economy becomes historical data. Today every fee in the game descends from one scaffold: `NetworkFeePolicy.MinFee = DefaultFee = 0.1`, `MaxFee = 1.0`, active from 2009-04-26 (Step 10). D-14.6 locked **Option A — historical fee replay** (mirroring `BtcMarketDataService`'s price-history architecture) as the chosen mechanism and §9.4 deferred the implementation; the developer promoted it into scope on 2026-07-12 so the next playtest exercises the ND.6 auction AND realistic fees together.

**This RETIRES the Step-10 flat-fee era entirely** (developer decision, 2026-07-12): the 2009-04-26 activation and its 0.1 BTC band were built to create and test the fee plumbing; now that real data exists they go. **No fee exists anywhere on the network before Market Birth (2010-07-18); from Market Birth the daily historical band governs.** Option B (a reactive fee market from our own simulated mempool congestion) remains the future validation experiment recorded in `PRIVATE_ROADMAP.md` — untouched by this phase, and explicitly the future layer that will give participants reasons to pick fees ABOVE the daily base (e.g. queue-jumping when the 24-tx block cap saturates).

### 10.1 Data reality (measured against the shipped CSV, 2026-07-12 — read before re-litigating the band)

- `btc_network_daily_2009_2025.csv` carries **`fee_total_btc` only** (daily TOTAL; mean = total ÷ `tx_count`, the derivation the ND.1 comment anticipated). No fee cell is null; zero-fee days: **109 in 2009, 255 in 2010, zero from 2011 onward** (every 2011+ day has a positive mean).
- Real fees are TINY vs the 0.1 scaffold: mean ≈ **0.00318548** on 2010-07-18 itself; yearly average daily means run ~0.0001–0.002 BTC (2025: ~0.0000117); one day is even sub-satoshi (0.0000000002 on 2010-09-21). Fees stay **fractal-exempt** (D-14.6 — replayed at face value, never /100, like `price_usd`).
- Fee data actually begins **2009-02-03**, not at Market Birth — but 2009→mid-2010 is dominated by zero-fee days, so replaying from fee-activation would have made the network effectively free for a year anyway; gating the whole fee era at Market Birth (D-ND7.1) is both the developer's call and the honest reading of the data.
- **True daily min/max per-tx fees exist in NO confirmed source** (Coin Metrics community, blockchain.com — §2's vetted set). The real-world daily minimum was effectively ZERO for most of history (zero-fee txs were mined routinely into the mid-2010s), so a literal "min of the day" collapses to "free forever". Coin Metrics community DOES publish the daily **median** (`FeeMedNtv`) — the closest real "registered low fee actual users paid". *(Correction at ND.7.0, 2026-07-13: that claim was wrong — `FeeMedNtv` is paid-tier, as ND.0 had itself recorded; the median column ships from the Blockchair + BitInfoCharts hybrid instead, see §10.5.)* The developer accepted: **median = the day's min**, and a **derived max** (no real max metric exists anywhere; the multiplier is a documented approximation, not data).

### 10.2 Decision round (2026-07-12, developer): gate, band, and who pays what

Asked as three questions; answers, verbatim intent:

1. **Replay gate** — Market Birth 2010-07-18, AND retire the 2009-04-26 flat-0.1 era entirely ("lo usamos más para crear y testear el sistema de fees"). No fee before 2010-07-18, confirmed.
2. **Zero-fee days** — carry forward the last positive value (the price service's halt-day pattern).
3. **Who pays what** — only the **cast bots** pay the day's MEAN; every other participant (other bots, casino, and the player's default) uses the day's MIN as base; future mechanics (mempool congestion at the 24-tx cap) will create reasons to choose higher fees. Follow-up round: MIN = the day's real **median** (new dataset column), MAX = derived (mean × multiplier).

### 10.3 Locked specification (D-ND7.1…10 — canonical)

- **D-ND7.1 — the fee era begins at Market Birth, data-driven; Step 10's activation date is retired.** `NetworkFeePolicy`'s activation becomes the fee schedule's own first replayed day (= Market Birth, read from `BtcMarketDataService.FirstDataDateLocal` by the schedule builder — never a second hardcoded date). Before it: every tx on the network is fee-free and the fee row stays hidden in all four send panels (the player sees no fee UI for ~484 canonical days — consistent with the swap-desk teaser, and the desk unlocks the very day fees go live, so its flat network-fee component is never 0 on an open desk). `TimelineConfig.FeeActivationLocal` and D-13.9's alt-timeline special case are ABSORBED: the alt timeline lands on 2010-07-18, so "fees begin at Market Birth" satisfies it naturally, with zero special-casing left.
- **D-ND7.2 — the daily band, from real data + one documented approximation.** Per day: `FeeMedian(day)` = the new `fee_median_btc` column (real Coin Metrics `FeeMedNtv` — **AMENDED at ND.7.0, 2026-07-13**: `FeeMedNtv` is still paid-tier, so the column is built from Blockchair true medians + BitInfoCharts, developer-approved hybrid — see the ND.7.0 checklist entry in §10.5 for full provenance); `FeeMean(day)` = `fee_total_btc ÷ tx_count` (derived, as anticipated); `FeeMax(day)` = `max(median, mean) × MaxFeeMeanMultiplier` (**10**, a named tunable constant — explicitly an approximation, no real max metric exists). All `Money.Normalize`d.
- **D-ND7.3 — who pays what.** Cast miners' sell-flow (`TryCastSellFlow`) pays the day's **MEAN** (they ARE the network's average activity). Every other automated tx — non-miner↔non-miner exchanges, casino pool payouts (+ retry path), casino-bot auction bids, auction settlement sweeps, swap-desk on-chain legs — pays the day's **MEDIAN**. Player: default-fill = median, clamp `[median, FeeMax]`. The old randomized 0.1–1.0 bot band (`MinBotFeeBtc`/`MaxBotFeeBtc`) and `CasinoTxFee = 0.1` ("lowest available to the casino" — now literally the median) are all replaced; no participant chooses above base until the Option-B congestion layer exists (recorded as the phase's one open follow-up).
- **D-ND7.4 — gap policy: per-component carry-forward, seeded from the data's own start.** The effective series is built over the WHOLE dataset (first positive value 2009-02-03 onward), each component (median, mean) independently carrying forward its last positive value across zero/sub-satoshi days — so by Market Birth a positive carry value always exists regardless of what the birth day's own median turns out to be. Past the dataset end: freeze at the last value (mirrors D-13.5). Same shape as `GetEffectivePriceUsd`'s halt-day carry-forward.
- **D-ND7.5 — architecture: schedule pushed into a pure-static `NetworkFeePolicy`.** `BtcNetworkDataService.EnsureLoaded()` builds the effective day-indexed schedule and pushes it via a static `NetworkFeePolicy.SetFeeSchedule(firstReplayDayLocal, entries[])` (the `SetNonMinerIntroSchedule` precedent — works identically for the real autoload and EB.1's throwaway bootstrap instances, so entry-year fast-builds crossing 2010-07-18 pay replay fees during the bootstrap itself). `NetworkFeePolicy` stays a pure static class, O(1) by day, with BOTH lookup bases it already serves: `MedianFeeFor(DateTime gameLocal)` / `MeanFeeFor` / `MaxFeeFor` and timestamp twins (`…At(long blockTimestampMs)`, midnight-UTC convention as today); `IsActive`/`IsActiveByTimestamp` keep their signatures but read the schedule start. **No schedule pushed (CSV load failure) ⇒ fee-free fallback + one `GD.PushWarning`, never a crash and never the 0.1 scaffold back.**
- **D-ND7.6 — `WorldFormatVersion` 2 → 3.** Fee semantics are world-defining: an existing chain carries flat-0.1 fees from 2009-04-26 that the new policy could never produce (and the historical bootstrap regenerates differently). The established clean-reset mechanism (the account→UTXO precedent) handles migration; no compatibility shim.
- **D-ND7.7 — auction constants are NOT fees.** The 0.1 BTC opening bid floor, the `max(0.1, 10%×leading)` bot min-raise, and the player's 1-satoshi raise (D-ND4b.5…8, ND.4d) are bid amounts and stay exactly as they are. Only the network fee ATTACHED to bid txs switches to the daily median (it was `NetworkFeePolicy.MinFee`-pinned already — D-ND4b.5's rationale carries over unchanged).
- **D-ND7.8 — legacy constants deleted, not shadowed.** `NetworkFeePolicy.DefaultFee`/`MinFee`/`MaxFee` (the consts), `NetworkRoot.MinBotFeeBtc`/`MaxBotFeeBtc`/`CasinoTxFee` all go; `ClampOrDefault` becomes date-aware (`ClampOrDefaultFor(fee, gameLocal)` — outside `[median, FeeMax]` ⇒ median). Compile errors are the sweep's checklist: every remaining `MinFee` reference (the swap desk has ~10) must consciously pick median/mean/max at the right date.
- **D-ND7.9 — swap desk reads the CURRENT game date's median, live.** All `NetworkFeePolicy.MinFee` uses in `CasinoCoinSwapService`/`CasinoCoinSwaps` (additive `casinoFee = fee×(base+MinFee)`, `BaseFromNet`, `MinSwapGrossBtcFor`, Max-clamp, quote/execution legs, the UI's fee-breakdown lines) become the live median. The D-SW formulas are unchanged in shape; the min-swap size and panel thresholds were ALREADY fee-dependent live computations, they just get much smaller (≈0.00055 BTC at a 0.0002 median vs ≈0.275 today) — the D-SW.12 crossover and value floor scale with it by construction. Quotes recompute per keystroke and executions re-gate on fresh state, so a day boundary between quote and click is already handled.
- **D-ND7.10 — telemetry.** `network_population_trace.csv` gains two columns (`feeMedianBtc,feeMeanBtc` — the day's effective values at each block row); the load prints one summary line (first replay day, seed values, dataset-end values). `swap_desk_trace.csv` rows already carry their fee figures per swap; no change there.

### 10.4 Consumer sweep map (the ND.7b checklist — every current call site, found by grep 2026-07-12)

| Site | Today | Becomes |
|---|---|---|
| `NetworkRoot` pool payouts + retry (`CasinoTxFee`, 3 sites) | flat 0.1 gated on `IsActiveByTimestamp` | median-at-block-timestamp (0 pre-birth) |
| `NetworkRoot.TryCastSellFlow` | random 0.1–1.0 | **mean**-at-block-timestamp |
| `NetworkRoot.TryNonMinerExchanges` | random 0.1–1.0 | median-at-block-timestamp |
| `NetworkRoot` casino-bot bid slot (feeds `TryBuildCasinoBotBid`) | `MinFee` pinned | median-at-block-timestamp |
| `NetworkRoot.TrySettleResolvedAuctions` sweep | `MinFee` | median-at-block-timestamp |
| `CasinoCoinSwapService` (~10 `MinFee` refs) + `CasinoCoinSwaps` UI fee breakdown | flat 0.1 | live median for the current game date (D-ND7.9) |
| 4 send panels (`BTCWallet`, `FoundersWallets`, `CasinoFinances`, `BotsBtcWallets`): row visibility, default-fill, clamp | `IsActive` / `DefaultFee` / `ClampOrDefault` | `IsActive` (new gate date) / day median / `ClampOrDefaultFor` |
| `BTCWallet` auction "minimum to compete" hint | adds `MinFee` if it does | verify at implementation; any fee term → day median |
| `InjectHistoricalSignedTxStatic` (scripted founder events) | `fee = 0` default | unchanged — all pre-birth, naturally fee-free |

### 10.5 Implementation subphases (ND.7.0 + ND.7a…d)

- [x] **ND.7.0 — dataset extension (data task, ND.0's pipeline)** ✅ BUILT (2026-07-13) — developer verification + commit pending:
  - [x] **Source pivot (developer-approved 2026-07-13)**: Coin Metrics community `FeeMedNtv` is STILL paid-tier — exactly as ND.0 recorded on 2026-07-07; the spec's premise didn't survive re-verification (API 403; community catalog exposes only `FeeTotNtv`; the coinmetrics/data GitHub community CSV lacks it too; Messari's old community time-series endpoints are dead; blockchain.com/mempool.space/bitcoinvisuals have no daily median). Hybrid replacement, all legs empirically verified live: **(a) Blockchair daily transaction dumps** (gz.blockchair.com, free) → TRUE medians for **2010-07-18 → 2011-04-13** (270 days; median over each day's non-coinbase txs' satoshi `fee`, rows filtered by date prefix — dumps carry a few adjacent-day boundary rows, immaterial to a median; even-count median = mean of the two central values rounded away-from-zero to the satoshi); **(b) BitInfoCharts** daily "Median Transaction Fee" (**USD-denominated only**, real values begin exactly 2011-04-14) ÷ BitInfoCharts' own daily price series (internal consistency — recovers their underlying BTC median, ~4 significant digits) for **2011-04-14 → 2025-12-31**, rounded to 8 decimals. Scripts (out-of-repo per policy): `Get-BtcFeeMedianDaily.ps1` (cached/resumable polite crawl + continuity assertion), `Merge-BtcFeeMedianIntoNetworkDaily.ps1` (join assertions, refuses double-merge), `Compare-BtcFeeMedianSpot.ps1` (second-source true-median spot-check).
  - [x] Column merged into `btc_network_daily_2009_2025.csv` — header now exactly the planned `date,tx_count,hashrate,active_addresses,block_count,fee_total_btc,fee_median_btc,source`; 6,207 rows unchanged; blank median on the 561 pre-birth days (no data, never 0); `.csv.import` untouched (`importer="keep"`).
  - [x] **Recorded findings**: median at Market Birth (2010-07-18) = **0** (a REAL zero — most txs paid no fee); **first positive median 2011-04-14 = 0.01 BTC** (exactly the era's standard client default fee — strong credibility signal); zero-median days: **2010 = 167, 2011 = 103, none after** (i.e. the ENTIRE Blockchair gap window is zero-median); blank (no-source-data) days: 2011 = 257, 2012 = 116, none after (BitInfoCharts' early series is sparse; D-ND7.4 carry-forward holds 0.01 through that window — historically correct, 0.01 WAS the standard fee).

- **OQ-ND7.1 — fee choice pressure**: with everyone at base (median) and cast at mean, fees are a pure cost, never a choice. The Option-B congestion layer (mempool saturation at the 24-tx cap → pay above base to jump the queue) is where fee CHOICE enters; it must also revisit whether bot auction bids should outbid on fees during contested windows.
