#### 12.5.4 ND.8f — Bots as first-class casino clients (✅ BUILT 2026-07-19; resolves OQ-11.1)

Requested by the developer right after the ND.8c verification: the casino's books (`CasinoGamblingFinances` and its sub-scenes) accounted **only for the player** — bot bets never touched the casino's SC balance sheet (Step 11's deferred **OQ-11.1**), bots had no ledger entries, and the per-client scenes hardcoded a single client. `bot_1..4` ARE the casino's other four canonical clients (each holding an ND.8c 40,000 genesis grant), so the casino must do their accounting identically.

- [x] **ND.8f.1 — OQ-11.1 resolved: every settled bet routes to the casino.** `SimulationService.ExecuteBotBet` (bot autobet + manual bursts) now calls `CasinoScBalanceService.ApplyBetResult(-betEvent.CreditedProfit)`; `DiceGame.ExecuteBet`'s casino routing moved OUT of the player-only gate (a bot active in the node selector settles against the casino too); `ExecutePlayerBetOnce`'s already-unconditional call keeps its behavior with a corrected comment (a delegated autobet on a bot node is that bot's play, now correct semantics rather than an inconsistency). The casino Bankroll therefore fluctuates with ALL five clients' play — more loans/recharges is the intended, correct behavior.
- [x] **ND.8f.2 — per-client bet-stats book** (`CasinoClientLedgerService.ClientBetStats`: bets/wins/losses/wagered/net-profit per client, `RegisterSettledBet`): the casino-side stats source for the non-player clients (the player's row keeps reading `UserStatsService` — richer and already checkpoint-consistent). Lives in the ledger service (NOT on `NodeFinancialState` — `DiceGame.SaveActiveNodeFinancialState` rebuilds that DTO from the shared services, which would clobber any stats fields). Registered from `ExecuteBotBet`, from `ExecutePlayerBetOnce` when the delegated session runs a bot node, and from `DiceGame.ExecuteBet` with a bot active. **No per-bet disk I/O**: stats mutate in memory with a dirty-flag flush (1 s) — and per-bet `ApplyBetResult` saves got the same throttle (0.5 s flush; loans/transfers/setters still save immediately). Safe because a restart restores from the block checkpoint anyway (block = the only commit; the eager file is only a legacy fallback).
- [x] **ND.8f.3 — bot ledger entries**: `EnsureCanonicalInitialDeposits()` — all five canonical clients get their `"initial"` 40,000 entry (the ND.8c genesis-grant mirror), established in `_Ready`, re-established after a checkpoint restore (a legacy checkpoint's entry list would otherwise wipe the migration), and on every pre-genesis reset (which now clears + re-registers all five, at the player-start clock). `TryAutoRechargeBot` registers `"auto_recharge"` entries with wagered/profit snapshots from the bet-stats book (the bots' "P/L since recharge" baseline). Auction settlement's bot payouts register `"auction_payout"` (previously player-only at `NetworkRoot.SettleResolvedAuction`).
- [x] **ND.8f.4 — scenes**: `ClientsTransactions`' client selector lists all five clients; `ClientsBetsHistory` builds one row per client (player from `UserStatsService`, bots from the bet-stats book — same metrics: bets/won/lost/win-rate, lifetime + since-deposit wagered, casino P/L all-time / since-deposit / since-recharge) and "Total SC wagered (all clients)" now sums all five sources each refresh. **Live feed (developer-requested follow-up after the verification playtest, same day): shows ALL five clients' bets** — a new typed `SimulationService.ClientBetSettled` event (`nodeId, gameId, BetTransactionEvent`; fired from `ExecuteBotBet` and the delegated player autobet — manual DiceGame bets can only happen while DiceGame is the active scene, so the feed can't miss them live) replaces the player-only `BetSettled`-signal wiring; each row is prefixed with the client's display name, capped at the same 50 rows; a **per-client filter dropdown** ("All Clients" + the five canonical clients, mirroring the game filter — either filter change clears the feed) isolates one client's play from the fast all-clients stream.
- [x] **ND.8f.5 — checkpoint coverage**: the bet-stats book joins the ledger's checkpoint surface (`ClientBetStats` map captured at each block beside the entries list; restored verbatim; a legacy pre-ND.8f checkpoint's null map keeps the loaded book — no better source exists; pre-genesis reset zeroes it). No new `user://` file (the book persists inside `casino_client_ledger.json`), so no delete-list change; no `WorldFormatVersion` bump.
- [x] `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors.
- [x] **ND.8f docs pass** ✅ DONE (2026-07-19): CLAUDE.md (`CasinoScBalanceService` write-path row updated to every-client + throttle note; `CasinoClientLedgerService` section rewritten multi-client; new Implemented bullet), `Documentation/ProjectDesignManual.md` **§31.3** (the full ND.8f write-up, superseding Ch. 31's "per settled player bet" phrasing) — and the same audit backfilled **§25.9** for ND.8a's Martingale-parity fix, which had never been mirrored into the manual.

**Known limitation (OQ-ND8f.1, deliberate)**: with a bot active in DiceGame's node selector, a Bankroll auto/manual recharge still flows through `BankrollProgramService`, which registers the ledger entry as `"player"` (it has no active-node awareness) — a pre-existing misattribution on a DEV-only path, NOT introduced here. Fix belongs to a future selector-aware recharge pass; the bots' own background recharges (`TryAutoRechargeBot`) attribute correctly.

**Developer verification checklist (before commit):** open `ClientsBetsHistory` with bots running → four bot rows accrue bets/wagered live (2 s refresh) and the casino Bankroll visibly moves with bot results in `CasinoGamblingFinances`; `ClientsTransactions` → selector shows Player + Bot 1–4, each with an `[INITIAL DEPOSIT] 40,000` entry; force a bot recharge streak → `auto_recharge` entries under that bot (hidden from the tx list by design, visible in the "since recharge" metric); mine a block, restart → bot stats/entries revert to the block's snapshot; pre-genesis restart → exactly five clean `initial` entries and a zeroed stats book.

#### 12.5.5 ND.8d — bots-only bid-flow stagnation: the bid-count-aware shallow-tier ladder + last-bid preservation (✅ BUILT 2026-07-20 — `dotnet build` clean; developer in-game verification + commit pending)

**Diagnosis (trace-driven — `user://logs/casino_bot_bid_trace.csv`, the calibration run right after the ND.8f docs pass; blocks 825→883, from Market Birth 2010-07-18, 3 pools open).** The three companies then in auction — `non_miner_1` Bitcoin Market, `non_miner_2` Papa's Pizzeria, `non_miner_3` ArtForz Cluster — all converge to the SAME structural state: 3 distinct bots each holding a distinct top-3 tracked slot (all `satisfied`, D-ND6.7a) + exactly ONE lone challenger stuck rolling tier 4.

| Pool | tier 1 | tier 2 | tier 3 | lone challenger (tier 4) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `non_miner_1` Bitcoin Market | bot_1 | bot_4 | bot_2 | **bot_3** |
| `non_miner_2` Papa's Pizzeria | bot_3 | bot_2 | bot_1 | **bot_4** |
| `non_miner_3` ArtForz Cluster | bot_1 | bot_4 | bot_3 | **bot_2** |

Root cause: `SatisfiedTopTierCount` (3) sits only ONE below the bidder count (4). Once 3 distinct bots each hold a distinct top-3 slot — near-instant in the early rush — the pool has PERMANENTLY at most one eligible challenger, and each time it wins it enters the top-3 and pushes a previously-satisfied bot down to tier 4 as the new lone challenger (never more than one active bidder per pool). `bot_1` in the trace held a top-3 slot in ALL THREE pools ⇒ fully idle (`no-qualifying-target`). ArtForz only *felt* worse because it is the youngest (5 donations vs 7) and its lone challenger `bot_2` hit a run of tier-4 declines. **The bottleneck is the challenger COUNT, not the ladder probability** — which is why the ND.6d/6e probability bumps did not resolve it: they raise the odds for a single challenger that is still alone.

**Locked fix (developer walkthrough over the ArtForz trace, 2026-07-20).** Re-bid eligibility for the two runner-up tiers becomes **bid-count-aware** — keyed on the bot's own tracked-slot count in that pool (`ownTiers.Count`) — turning tiers 2 and 3 back into live challengers while the leader stays untouchable:

- **D-ND8d.1 — tier 1 stays ALWAYS satisfied.** The current leader never re-bids (no self-outbid, no window reset). NO `TryBuildCasinoBotBid` leader-skip is added: the existing tier-1 `satisfied` rule already IS the bots' "last-bid preservation," so bot code needs no change beyond the tier-2/3 relaxation. (Developer's chosen resolution of the Q1 fork — keep tier-1 satisfied rather than relax it and add a separate bot leader-guard.)
- **D-ND8d.2 — tier 2 is NEVER satisfied** (its entire purpose): it always rolls a re-bid probability, in every mode and at any bid-count.
- **D-ND8d.3 — tier 3 is satisfied ONLY at ≥2 own bids**; with exactly 1 own bid it rolls. (A tier-3 holder locks its NST slot and rests once it has bid twice; before that it keeps climbing — this is exactly `bot_3`/`bot_4` in ArtForz, the "why don't they place a second bid?" case.)
- **D-ND8d.4 — the bid-count-aware probability matrix** (tiers 2 & 3 ONLY; keyed by pool mode × the bot's own bid-count). `1 bid` = `ownTiers.Count == 1`; `≥2 bids` = `ownTiers.Count >= 2`:

  | mode | tier 2 · 1 bid | tier 2 · ≥2 bids | tier 3 · 1 bid | tier 3 · ≥2 bids |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | early-rush (< 7 occupied slots) | 21% | 21% | 13% | satisfied (0%) |
  | normal (≥ 7 occupied slots) | 5% | 3% | 2% | satisfied (0%) |
  | urgency (normal pool, final 7 in-game days) | 5% | 5% | 3% | satisfied (0%) |

  *(Retuned 2026-07-20 round 2 — see the post-playtest revision at the end of this subsection. Invariants: **tier 2 out-probabilities tier 3 at EQUAL bid-count in every mode** (early 21>13 · normal 5>2 · urgency 5>3), all values Fibonacci, and **urgency ≥ normal** at every cell.)*

  Direction by mode is intentional: **early-rush** = commitment ⇒ aggression (tier 2 fights hard at 21% regardless of stake count; tier 3 is hot at 13% on its first stake, then rests); **normal / urgency** = *fewer bids ⇒ hungrier ⇒ higher* (the runner-up sitting on a single stake pushes hardest to reclaim the lead). The early-rush 1-bid crossover — tier 2 `21%` > tier 3 `13%`, against the base ladder's "worse slot ⇒ more prob" — is deliberate: it preserves the last top-tier slot and rewards the tier-2 holder's stronger incentive to make the trade-off that secures two superior tiers.
- **D-ND8d.5 — tiers 4–9 unchanged** (the existing two-mode + urgency ladder, bid-count-INDEPENDENT: normal 5/8/13/21/34/55 · early-rush 34/55/89 · urgency 8/13/21/34/55/89); **tier 10 self-eviction guard unchanged** (unconditional — always effective even at 1 bid, `D-ND6.7b`).
- **D-ND8d.6 — player bid-safety warnings + LAST-BID PRESERVATION (BTC wallet, NON-BLOCKING — never blocks a transfer; round-2 correction 2026-07-20).** The wallet surfaces clear warnings but ALWAYS lets the send proceed. Two non-blocking warnings join the existing below-minimum one: (a) **already-leading (last-bid preservation)** — the player already holds the leading bid; a further send is **IGNORED by the auction**: it does NOT count as a donation, never becomes a new leading bid, never resets the 20-day window, and is excluded from the tracked pool — the player's current leading bid simply stands. (The transfer still leaves the wallet on-chain as a plain, non-participating transfer, so the warning strongly advises against it. This is the round-2 fix of the original wording, which wrongly said the re-bid "resets the window / delays your win" — that was the OLD unpreserved behavior.) (b) **closing-soon** — the auction closes within the final **2 in-game days** (`NetworkRoot.AuctionClosingSoonWarningDays = 2`); if no block is mined before it closes this bid may not be counted (see D-ND8d.7). All three can co-occur (multi-line label). **This DOES touch `ComputeAuctionLedger` (round-2):** the ascending ratchet now skips any bid whose donor equals the CURRENT leader (recorded and also dropped from the tracked pool) — universal, though in practice only the player triggers it (bot_1..4's tier-1 satisfied rule keeps them off their own leader pool, so bot code still needs no leader-guard). **Future debt (documented, NOT built): move the entire bid system into internal casino wallets that then broadcast to the blockchain, so the contest is manageable without waiting for the next mined block to read the standings** — the per-block-resolved warnings + the D-ND8d.7 cleanup are the interim stopgap.
- **D-ND8d.7 — stale-bid cancellation + cash-back (the closing-race safety net, developer proposal 2026-07-20).** A bid counts ONLY once MINED into a block with `timestamp ≤ WindowCloseUnixMs` (`ComputeAuctionLedger` walks CONFIRMED blocks; `D-ND4b.12` bars post-close bids) — a bid mined into a post-close block would spend BTC for nothing. Key model fact (CLAUDE.md Pattern 2): a MEMPOOL tx has NOT spent its UTXOs yet (the UTXO set is chain-derived), so a bid that never confirms costs its sender nothing. Mechanism — prevention-first, applied to ALL miners (player + `bot_1..4`) uniformly:
    1. **Assembly prune (primary).** When a block is assembled from the mempool, EXCLUDE any bid tx whose target company's auction is already resolved as of the block's prospective timestamp — the stale bid is never mined ⇒ its UTXOs stay unspent ⇒ the sender keeps the BTC automatically (this IS the "cash-back," achieved by not spending, no refund tx). Complemented by a mempool sweep in the `TrySettleResolvedAuctions` diff the instant an auction resolves, so a stale bid can't confirm on the next block either.
    2. **Explicit refund (edge-case net).** If a bid still confirms after close (mined into the very block that crossed the window), the founded company's treasury sends the BTC back to the sender — a real on-chain refund tx, network fee deducted (the ND.5-sweep precedent), memo-tagged so wallets render it "· AUCTION REFUND". With (1) this essentially never fires, but it guarantees no BTC is ever stranded at a company for a non-counting bid.
  A "bid tx" (for both) = a tx from a qualifying bidder (`BuildAuctionBidderIdentity`) to a company's address; staleness is judged against that company's `WindowCloseUnixMs`. Cancellations/refunds logged to the auction trace.

**New UI objective (added by the developer, 2026-07-20).** The live per-slot re-bid probability labels in `AuctioningCompanyDetails` must reflect the REAL current odds — i.e. update with **how many bids the bot occupying that tier holds** (a tier-3 slot flips from its `1 bid` % to `satisfied` the instant its owner reaches 2 own bids). And a slot **occupied by the player shows NO re-bid probability at all** (blank/"—" — the player bids manually and never rolls the ladder).

**Implementation checklist:**
- [x] **ND.8d.1 — bid-count-aware satisfied + ladder** (`NetworkRoot.TryBuildCasinoBotBid` + `ReBidProbabilityPercentFor`): replace the flat `ownTiers[0] <= SatisfiedTopTierCount` test with the D-ND8d.1/2/3 per-tier rules (tier 1 always skip; tier 2 never skip; tier 3 skip iff `ownTiers.Count >= 2`; tier 10 self-eviction skip unchanged). Thread the bot's own bid-count (`ownTiers.Count`) into `ReBidProbabilityPercentFor`, extended to select the new tier-2/tier-3 tables by `(mode, bidCount)` and defer to the existing tables for tiers 4–9. Add named constant tables for the tier-2/tier-3 values (literal per `D-ND6.4` — no formula-derivation). `SatisfiedTopTierCount` is retired/reduced accordingly.
- [x] **ND.8d.2 — live UI probability labels** (`ReBidProbabilityLabel` + `AuctioningCompanyDetails`, and any other caller): the label gains the occupying donor's bid-count so each tracked-pool slot shows the real current probability, recomputed live as counts change; a slot whose donor is the **player** renders no re-bid probability (blank/"—"). Share the same `(mode, bidCount, tier)` source as the roll (single source of truth, the ND.6d pattern).
- [x] **ND.8d.3 — player BTC-wallet warnings (NON-blocking, `D-ND8d.6`)**: extend `RefreshAuctionBidWarning` (a multi-line label now) with two new non-blocking lines — **already-leading** (new `NetworkRoot.IsPlayerLeadingCompanyBid(address)`, reusing `ComputeAuctionLedger` + `BuildAuctionBidderIdentity`) and **closing-soon** (new read of the target's `WindowCloseUnixMs` vs. current game time, `AuctionClosingSoonWarningMs = 2` in-game days). `OnSendConfirmed` NEVER blocks — the send always proceeds. NO `ComputeAuctionLedger` change.
- [x] **ND.8d.4 — stale-bid cancellation + cash-back (`D-ND8d.7`)**: at block assembly, prune mempool bid txs to companies whose auction has resolved as of the block's prospective timestamp (+ a mempool sweep in the `TrySettleResolvedAuctions` diff); the edge-case explicit refund from the founded company's treasury for any bid confirmed post-close (memo-tagged "· AUCTION REFUND", ND.5-sweep precedent), for player + bots alike; auction-trace telemetry for cancellations/refunds. Detect bid txs via the qualifying-bidder identity (`BuildAuctionBidderIdentity`); staleness judged against the target's `WindowCloseUnixMs`.
- [x] **ND.8d.5 — docs pass + build**: CLAUDE.md (the Referral-auction canonical row — the ladder is now bid-count-aware for tiers 2/3 + the player non-blocking bid-safety warnings + the stale-bid cancellation/cash-back), `Documentation/ProjectDesignManual.md` Ch. 22 §22.10 (the bid-count matrix, the tier-1-always-satisfied decision, the live-label rule, the non-blocking warnings, the D-ND8d.7 closing-race cancellation+refund, and the future internal-casino-wallet-bidding debt), this checklist ticked; `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors.

**✅ BUILT 2026-07-20 — `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors. Two spec-wording deviations, both deliberate:**
- **ND.8d.4 is done post-mine, not at pre-assembly.** The candidate block is cached and the block timestamp is only finalized at mine time, so a literal pre-assembly prune was fragile. Instead: (1) a **tracked-pool close filter in `ComputeAuctionLedger`** (`bids.Where(b => b.ts <= WindowCloseUnixMs)`) guarantees a post-close bid earns no stock / joins no donor set — the correctness backstop that makes the confirmed-stale case harmless; (2) **`NetworkRoot.CancelAndRefundStaleAuctionBids`** runs in `HandleMinedBlock` right after `TrySettleResolvedAuctions`: the mempool sweep (drop still-PENDING qualifying bids to any resolved company from every node's mempool — coins never spent) PLUS the confirmed-stale refund (`RefundStaleBid`, treasury → bidder, memo "· AUCTION REFUND", fee-deducted). Net effect matches the spec (pending bids cost nothing; the rare confirmed-stale is refunded).
- **Telemetry is console `[ND.8d]` `GD.Print`, not a CSV.** Cancellations/refunds are rare edge events; a dedicated trace file + world-reset delete-list entry was judged not worth the surface for v1 — the console line suffices for the playtest. (A CSV can be promoted later if calibration needs it.)
- Also of note: the closing-soon threshold const is `NetworkRoot.AuctionClosingSoonWarningDays = 2d` (days, not the ms name the checklist drafted); tier-2/3 probabilities live as named `TierN…Percent` consts + `IsBidderSatisfied`/`ShallowTierProbabilityPercent` helpers; `SatisfiedTopTierCount` is fully removed.

**Developer verification checklist (before commit — assistant never launches the game):** run a stretch where the BOTS (not the player) drive the auctions and watch `casino_bot_bid_trace.csv` — a pool with 3 top-3 holders now shows tier-2 AND tier-3 (1-bid) challengers rolling, not a single lone tier-4 bot, and no bot sits fully idle across all open pools; in `AuctioningCompanyDetails`, each tracked slot's re-bid % tracks its holder's bid-count (a tier-3 slot flips to "satisfied" once its owner has 2 own bids) and a player-held slot shows no %; as the player, send BTC to a company you already lead → a clear NON-blocking warning shows and the send still proceeds; open a bid on a company inside its final 2 in-game days → the closing-soon warning shows; let an auction close with a still-pending bid to it in the mempool → the pending bid is cancelled and the coins remain in the sender's wallet (no BTC moved); if a bid ever confirms post-close, a "· AUCTION REFUND" tx returns the BTC from the company.

##### ND.8d post-playtest revision (2026-07-20 round 2) — three developer adjustments after the first in-game test

**(1) Shallow-tier matrix retuned** (`Tier2*Percent` / `Tier3*Percent` consts + `ShallowTierProbabilityPercent`). New requirement: **tier 2 must out-probability tier 3 at EQUAL bid-count in every mode**, all Fibonacci, urgency ≥ normal. The matrix above is the locked result; the round-1 values (tier 3 normal/urgency `5%`, tier 2 ≥2-bids normal `2%` / urgency `3%`) are superseded. Concretely: tier 2 ≥2-bids normal `2→3` and urgency `3→5`; tier 3 1-bid split from a shared `3%` into normal `2%` / urgency `3%`. Every same-bid-count pair now reads tier 2 > tier 3 (early 21>13 · normal 5>2 · urgency 5>3).

**(2) Last-bid preservation is now REAL, not just a warning** (`ComputeAuctionLedger` ratchet + `BTCWallet` text). The round-1 build only warned; the leader's self-raise still counted (became a bigger leading bid, reset the window). The developer clarified the intended behavior: **a send from the current leader must NOT count as a bid at all.** Implemented by skipping any ratchet bid whose donor equals the current leader (recorded in `ignoredSelfRaiseSeqs`, also excluded from the tracked pool). The wallet's already-leading warning was corrected to state the true consequence ("won't count as a bid · won't participate · your leading bid is preserved") instead of the old "resets the window / delays your win." Non-blocking still — the on-chain transfer proceeds as a plain, non-participating send. Only the player can trigger this (bots' tier-1 satisfied rule already keeps them off their own leader pool). See D-ND8d.6 (updated).

**(3) Two-lowest-probability re-bid roll** (`SumTwoLowestReBidProbabilities`, replacing the single best-tier lookup in the roll). A participated pool's roll probability is now the **SUM of the bot's two LOWEST slot re-bid probabilities**, ranked by PROBABILITY (not tier — the tier2/tier3 inversion means the two lowest probabilities need not be the two best tiers). A satisfied slot contributes 0, so a bot that still holds a satisfied slot only ever sums `{0, its one active prob}` = that single prob — the boost therefore applies SOLELY to a bot with no satisfied slot (all-positive), exactly the developer's rule; a single-slot bot is unchanged. Clamped to 100 (early-rush sums can exceed it). Effect: a bot spread across several non-satisfied slots re-bids noticeably harder, adding challenger pressure without touching the per-tier table. The gate (best-tier `IsBidderSatisfied`) and the per-slot `AuctioningCompanyDetails` label are unchanged — the label still shows each slot's own %, while the actual roll now sums the two lowest (a deliberate display/mechanic split, noted for a future label polish if it confuses).

**(4) Dividend / vote audit** (developer report: "first quarter of Papa's Pizzeria passed, still can't claim dividends; PST company can't claim and its daily labels don't change; not sure votes apply"). Findings from `company_governance_trace.csv` (empirical, not a guess):
- **Votes ARE applying.** Founding-day votes fired and closed for all three founded companies — `awaiting_player` on Papa's & Bitcoin Market (the player holds NST there → the game paused and the player voted), `bots_only` on ArtForz; two `>30%-inflow special` votes also opened/closed and applied their reserve %.
- **BTC→SC conversions ARE running** (many `btc_to_sc` rows; each company's `ScReserve` is filling).
- **Dividends had NOT started for ANYONE — and this is the back-loaded DESIGN, not a code bug.** The dividend cycle (`QuarterCycleStartMs`, `QuarterDividend*`) is initialized ONLY when the first **quarterly** vote closes at `founding + 3 months`; the founding-day vote is reserve-only. Empirically Papa's was founded **2010-09-24** and the session had reached only **~2010-11-27** (~2 in-game months) — still inside Q1, before any quarterly. So `QuarterCycleStartMs = 0` everywhere ⇒ `AccrueDailyDrip`/`SettleDividendCycleAtQuarterEnd` early-return ⇒ zero claimable, daily labels stay 0. The player's "traversed the first quarter" was ~1 month short. First PST daily drip begins at `founding+3mo`; first NST lump lands at `founding+6mo` (end of the first full cycle, Q2).
- **Open UX concern (OQ-ND8d.1, not yet fixed):** a ~3-month first quarter with zero dividends and unchanging labels reads as broken. A future `CompanyDetails` polish should surface "dividends begin after the first quarterly vote on `<date>`" / a next-quarterly countdown so the empty ramp is legible. **No dividend-engine code change is warranted** — the engine is correct; the shortfall is purely legibility. (Whether to also pay a pro-rated Q1 dividend from founding is a separate design call, deferred.)

##### ND.8d round 3 (2026-07-21) — dividend re-audit + The Silk Market stagnation diagnosis + the stuck-bidder escalation rule (✅ BUILT 2026-07-21 — `dotnet build` clean; developer in-game verification + commit pending)

**Dividend re-audit, several in-game months later — CONFIRMS round 2's diagnosis, no engine bug, and pins down exactly what's missing.** Read live off `state.json`'s `CompanyGovernance`/`CompanyFoundings` (not a guess):
- **Papa's Pizzeria** — player holds NST (3729.34, the single LARGEST holding, tier 1). `QuarterCycleStartMs` is set (the first quarterly vote already closed, opening the Q1→Q2 accrual cycle) but `QuarterLumpCredited = false` and `NextQuarterlyDueMs` (Q2 close) was only **~8–9 in-game days** away as of this read. **Correctly nothing to claim yet** — the first NST lump lands at `founding+6mo`, not `founding+3mo` (round 2's finding holds).
- **ArtForz Cluster** — player holds PST (921.95). `ClaimableByHolder["player"]` shows a REAL, nonzero, unclaimed balance: **3.63683387 BTC + 0.89356015 SC**, accrued via the daily drip. The engine is crediting correctly; `CompanyDetails.cs`'s gating (`hasPst`, the "Daily Dividend" panel, the `Claimable now: …` label, 1 s refresh) reads this exact dictionary entry correctly. **The likely explanation is simply that this specific company's `CompanyDetails` screen hasn't been opened / the claim button hasn't been pressed since the drip started** — not a code defect.
- Player holds nothing in Slush Pool or Grass Hill Alpacas (bots-only foundings) — correctly no dividend expectation there.
- **Expanded OQ-ND8d.1 (still unfixed, now with two concrete asks):** (a) a next-dividend countdown/date on `CompanyDetails`, AND (b) some positive signal — on the Enroll Mode "Founded" row or elsewhere — that a founded company the player holds shares in has a **nonzero unclaimed balance sitting right now**, so a real accrued amount (like ArtForz's 3.6 BTC) doesn't go unnoticed indefinitely. Both remain UI-only asks; no engine change.

**The Silk Market (`non_miner_6`) stagnation diagnosis — trace-reconstructed, not a bug in the ND.8d round-2 mechanics.** Reconstructing the tracked pool from `casino_bot_bid_trace.csv` (7 donations, no evictions yet — pool below `MaxTrackedDonations`): tier 1 = `bot_2` (2.834 BTC), tier 2 = `bot_3` (2.161), tier 3 = `bot_2` (1.182, a repeat — bot_2 holds tiers {1,3,7}), tier 4 = `bot_4` (0.866, single bid), tier 5 = `bot_1` (0.342, single bid), tier 6 = `bot_3` (0.169, bot_3's second slot), tier 7 = `bot_2` (0.165). The pool has reached 7 occupied slots (NORMAL mode) and its rolling window is inside the final-7-day URGENCY band.
- `bot_2` — best tier 1 ⇒ `IsBidderSatisfied` ⇒ correctly silent (never re-bids on itself, matches the trace: no `bot_2` rows after the block where it took tier 1).
- `bot_3` — tiers {2,6}, best tier 2 (never satisfied). `SumTwoLowestReBidProbabilities({2,6}, …)` genuinely computes a decent probability (≈16% NORMAL, ≈26% URGENCY — confirmed against the trace's logged `rolledProbabilityPercent` values, which matched the formula exactly at every sampled row). **Bot_3 is NOT silent by rule — it is simply busy elsewhere**: the bot-centric preference order (D-ND6.6) sorts a bot's qualifying pools by ASCENDING own-slot-count, so bot_3 spends its once-per-block draw contesting pools where it holds ZERO slots before ever revisiting Silk Market, where it already holds two. This matches the developer's own read ("bot-3 tiene una buena probabilidad actualmente" but isn't shown bidding here).
- `bot_4` — tier 4 only, single bid, URGENCY mode ⇒ rolls a flat **8%** every time it's drawn (`UrgentReBidProbabilityPercentByTier[4] = 8`), repeatedly declining in the trace tail (blocks 1145/1148/1151/1158). **This is the real residual case**: a bot stuck alone at ONE low-probability non-top-3 slot, with no other bot actively re-bidding against it in THIS pool, can decline indefinitely at a flat low probability — the exact shape ND.8d round 1/2 did not target (those fixed the *challenger-count* problem; this is a *lone, low, unchanging probability* problem for a bot that has nowhere better to go).

**Fix (developer proposal, 2026-07-20/21 — table below, confirmed base/growth 2026-07-21, ✅ BUILT 2026-07-21):** an ESCALATING probability for a bot stuck at exactly ONE non-top-3 slot in a pool. Every block that passes without that bot placing another bid in THAT SAME pool, the slot's roll probability grows by the tier's BASE amount (linear, not the mode-adjusted early-rush/urgency value) — reset to the new slot's base the instant the bot's tier there changes (its own new bid, or ANYONE ELSE's bid re-ranking it).

- **Base value = the existing NORMAL-mode per-tier value** (no new dedicated constants) — `ReBidProbabilityPercentByTier`: tier4=5, tier5=8, tier6=13, tier7=21, tier8=34, tier9=55. The escalation itself grows off this plain NORMAL base regardless of the pool's actual current mode.
  - **⚠️ Non-regression floor (2026-07-21 audit fix — supersedes the original "ignore the mode entirely" wording).** The first cut REPLACED the mode rate with `NORMAL base × (blocksElapsed+1)`. That SUPPRESSED bidding in **EARLY-RUSH** pools: a single-slot below-top-3 bot there would otherwise roll the steep early-rush rate (tier 4/5/6 = 34/55/89%), but the raw escalation handed it the NORMAL base (5/8/13%) on block 1, only climbing back over ~7 blocks — and active-pool churn kept resetting it to base. This was **the DeepBit (non_miner_7) stagnation** the developer observed: reconstructed from `casino_bot_bid_trace.csv` block 1166, bot_4 alone at tier 5 in a 6-slot (early-rush) DeepBit rolled `8%` — the escalation base, where early-rush would have given `55%`. The fix composes the escalation as **`max(mode-appropriate rate, escalation)`** at the call site (`TryBuildCasinoBotBid`): the escalation is a FLOOR ON TOP of the mode rate, never a replacement, so early-rush's steep young-pool aggression is preserved AND a genuinely stuck bot still escalates toward 100% the longer it waits. (This also fixes the diagnosed URGENCY case, where the raw escalation's block-1 tier-4 `5%` was itself below the urgency `8%` it meant to cure.) `ComputeStuckEscalationProbabilityPercent` is unchanged (still returns the raw NORMAL-base escalation + owns the edge-triggered signature side effect); only the caller's composition changed from replace to `max`.
- **Growth = linear, +base per block stuck**, clamped at 100%. (Not geometric doubling — "doblando" in the original phrasing was corrected to linear during confirmation.)

  | Tier | Base (block 1) | Block 2 | Block 3 | Block 4 | Block 5 | Block 6 | … | Reaches 100% at block |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 4 | 5% | 10% | 15% | 20% | 25% | 30% | … | 20 |
  | 5 | 8% | 16% | 24% | 32% | 40% | 48% | … | 13 (last step 96→100 clamped) |
  | 6 | 13% | 26% | 39% | 52% | 65% | 78% | … | 8 (last step 91→100 clamped) |
  | 7 | 21% | 42% | 63% | 84% | 100%(clamped) | 100% | … | 5 |
  | 8 | 34% | 68% | 100%(clamped) | 100% | 100% | 100% | … | 3 |
  | 9 | 55% | 100%(clamped) | 100% | 100% | 100% | 100% | … | 2 |

  (Tier 10 is excluded — the self-eviction guard already blocks it unconditionally, regardless of probability, so the escalation never gets a chance to apply there.)

- **Scope / trigger:** any bot with EXACTLY ONE tracked donation in a pool (`ownTiers.Count == 1`), whose tier is below the top 3 (tier 4–9 — tiers 1–3 are the existing satisfied states and never reach this path). Bots with ≥2 slots keep the existing `SumTwoLowestReBidProbabilities` roll unchanged — this escalation is single-slot-only, exactly the `bot_4`/Silk-Market shape.
- **Reset conditions:** (a) the bot successfully places another bid in that pool (now ≥2 slots there, or possibly satisfied — falls out of this path entirely); (b) ANY other party's bid (another bot, or the player) changes the bot's tier — e.g. a new larger donation pushes it from tier 4 to tier 5 — the escalation restarts from the NEW tier's base, not carried over.
- **Implementation, first cut (✅ BUILT, then revised same day — see below):** a chain-derived-only version computed "since" purely from `TrackedDonations`' snapshot, watching only for a GREATER-valued donation arriving after the bot's own. **Gap found (developer question, 2026-07-21):** this missed the case where the bot's OWN OTHER slot gets evicted — e.g. a bot holding {tier 5, tier 9} loses its tier-9 slot to a third party's larger donation, dropping it to a single tier-5 slot WITHOUT any disturbance above tier 5 itself. The bot was governed by the round-2 multi-slot roll (`SumTwoLowestReBidProbabilities`) during the whole 2-bid period — never "stuck at 1 bid" — so the escalation must start FRESH the moment it actually becomes single-slot, not reach back to that surviving slot's own (possibly old) donation timestamp. The first cut got this wrong: it would have read the OLD donation's age and could jump the probability near 100% instantly instead of ramping from base.
- **Implementation, corrected (✅ BUILT 2026-07-21):** `NetworkRoot.ComputeStuckEscalationProbabilityPercent(target, botNodeId, ownSlotCount, bestTier, currentBlockIndex)`, backed by an in-memory-only signal `_stuckBidderSignatures: Dictionary<(nonMinerNodeId, botNodeId), (signature, sinceBlockIndex)>`. The signature is `"multi"` when `ownSlotCount ≥ 2`, else `"single:{tier}"` — folding BOTH triggers (a rank-push changing the tier, AND a multi→single transition from losing another slot) into ONE comparable value. Called on EVERY participated roll (both multi- and single-slot cases) so the multi-slot state is always kept fresh; whenever the recorded signature differs from the current one, `sinceBlockIndex` is stamped to the CURRENT block — an edge-triggered update. This is explicitly a developer-requested "avoid `_Process`/polling — use an internal signal, event-driven" design: no history replay, no new field to thread through the pipeline (the earlier `chain`/`FindBlockIndexAtOrBeforeTimestamp` plumbing was removed — no longer needed), just a boolean-shaped signal checked once per this bot's per-block evaluation, inside the SAME block-mined event that already drives the whole bidding cascade.
- **Deliberately NOT part of `BlockchainStateSnapshot`** (no checkpoint/pre-genesis-reset/delete-list work): like `_lastMinedByNodeId`/`_currentMinerStreak` elsewhere in `NetworkRoot`, this is pure bidding-behavior bookkeeping, not economically meaningful world state — an app restart just resets the dictionary, and the escalation harmlessly restarts from base, exactly how the existing streak counters already behave.
- **Accepted residual imprecision:** if a bot's tracked donation is evicted down to ZERO slots in a pool (fully gone, not just reduced from 2→1) and it LATER lands a brand-new donation at the EXACT SAME numeric tier by coincidence, the stale recorded signature would match and wrongly skip the reset. Extremely low-probability (requires full eviction to zero AND an exact tier-number coincidence on rejoining) — accepted rather than adding cleanup bookkeeping for it.
- **Label parity follow-up (✅ BUILT 2026-07-21, after the `max()` fix).** The developer, verifying the escalation from the `AuctioningCompanyDetails` per-slot "re-bid NN%" labels, reported "no change in the labels." Root cause: the escalation lived ONLY in the roll (`TryBuildCasinoBotBid`); the label helper (`ReBidProbabilityLabel` → `ReBidProbabilityPercentFor`) computed only the static mode-rate table and never the escalation, so a lone below-top-3 slot's label sat frozen (e.g. `bot_1`'s tier-5 BitInstant/`non_miner_8` slot displayed a fixed `8%` while the trace shows its actual roll climbed `8 → 16 → 24` across blocks 1206–1208 — the escalation WAS working, the label just didn't reflect it). Fix: a **side-effect-free peek** (`PeekStuckEscalationProbabilityPercent` — reads `_stuckBidderSignatures` without stamping it, so the 1 s UI refresh can never corrupt the roll's timing; both it and the compute path now share `EscalatedStuckPercent`), consumed by a new **instance** method `ReBidProbabilityLabelForSlot(summary, donorAddress, tier, occupied, urgent, ownBidCount)` that composes the SAME `max(mode rate, escalation)` the roll uses — resolving the occupant bot from the donor address (bots are single-address, OQ-8.2) and reading the current chain tip index. The static `ReBidProbabilityLabel` is retired (its one caller, `AuctioningCompanyDetails`, now calls the instance method). The label now visibly climbs in lockstep with the roll (and, in an early-rush pool, shows the `max()` floor). Note BitInstant is a 7-slot NORMAL pool, so `max()` there returns the same numbers "replace" did — the fix's early-rush floor is only observable in a `<7`-slot pool.

**Status: ✅ BUILT 2026-07-21 (corrected same day; + the `max()` non-regression floor audit fix + the label-parity follow-up, all same day, after the DeepBit early-rush stagnation and the frozen-label report were diagnosed from live trace/state) — `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors. Developer in-game verification + commit pending.**

#### 12.5.6 ND.8g — Player Dividend Claim History panel (✅ BUILT 2026-07-21 — `dotnet build` clean; developer in-game verification + commit pending)

Requested 2026-07-21, right after confirming ArtForz Cluster's PST claim genuinely works (§12.5.5 round 3's dividend re-audit). The developer wants a durable, per-company log of every dividend the **player** has actually claimed (not the bots' auto-claims, a separate code path), with the day's BTC/SC price captured at claim time, plus running lifetime totals.

**Scope (confirmed 2026-07-21):** this is the **PLAYER's own claim history for that one company** — every press of "Claim Dividends" in that company's `CompanyDetails`, not an all-holders/all-bots ledger. `TryClaimPlayerCompanyDividends` is the one call site where the PLAYER claims; bots go through `TryAutoClaimBotDividends`, which never touches this history.

**New data captured per claim (one record per successful `TryClaimPlayerCompanyDividends` call that paid something — a no-op "nothing to claim" attempt writes nothing):**

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Claimed at (game time) | `CalendarTimeService` game time, per the project's canonical "game time, never wall-clock" rule — not `DateTime.Now` |
| BTC paid this claim | The BTC leg actually broadcast this press (0 if the BTC leg didn't fire — e.g. below the fee floor, or the treasury was tied up) |
| SC paid this claim | The SC leg actually credited this press (0 if the SC reserve was empty) |
| BTC/USD price that day | `BtcMarketDataService.GetEffectivePriceUsd` at claim time (SC is USD-pegged 1:1, so this doubles as the BTC/SC price) — `null` only in the practically-unreachable case a founded company predates Market Birth (it can't; Market Birth 2010-07-18 is well before the earliest possible founding) |

**Illustrative mock-up (fabricated numbers, NOT real data — just to show the panel's shape):**

| Date | BTC paid | SC paid | BTC/SC price that day |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2011-02-14 | 0.42150000 | 0.10395000 | 0.247 |
| 2011-03-02 | 0.38700000 | 0.09250000 | 0.261 |
| 2011-03-19 | 0.51200000 | 0.00000000 | 0.298 |
| … | … | … | … |

**Lifetime totals shown above/below the log (all computed from the SAME record list, recomputed live each refresh — nothing frozen except each row's own historical price):**

1. **Total SC received (all-time)** = `Σ ScAmount` across every record.
2. **Total BTC received (all-time)** = `Σ BtcAmount` across every record. Two DERIVED valuations of that same BTC total, shown side by side:
   - **2a. Historical BTC/SC payment value** = `Σ (BtcAmount × BtcPriceUsdAtClaim)` — each payment valued at ITS OWN day's price (a true "what it was worth when you got it" figure; never recomputed).
   - **2b. Current BTC/SC payment value** = `TotalBtcReceived × GetEffectivePriceUsd(now)` — the SAME BTC total revalued at TODAY's live price (recomputed every scene refresh, the established "always live" convention already used for `TrackedDonation.CurrentValueSc` / the auction's `LeadingDonorScValue`). The gap between 2a and 2b is a direct, at-a-glance read of how much BTC appreciation/depreciation your dividends have seen since you were paid.

**Implementation (✅ BUILT):**
- New `CompanyDividendClaimRecord { ClaimedAtUnixMs, BtcAmount, ScAmount, BtcPriceUsdAtClaim (decimal?) }`.
- New `List<CompanyDividendClaimRecord> PlayerClaimHistory` field on `CompanyGovernanceState` (sits beside `ClaimableByHolder`), capped defensively at `MaxPlayerClaimHistoryPerCompany = 500` (the `PlayerBankAccountService.BankTransferRecord` precedent). **No new persisted file, no new checkpoint/pre-genesis/delete-list work** — `CompanyGovernanceState` already rides inside `NetworkRoot`'s own `BlockchainStateSnapshot`/`state.json` for free (the same inheritance `CompanyFounding`/`CompanyGovernanceState` themselves rely on).
- `TryClaimPlayerCompanyDividends` appends ONE record right after a successful claim (`paidBtc > 0m || paidSc > 0m`), using the same `tipMs` (the chain tip's block timestamp — game time) already used for the BTC leg's fee lookup, and `_marketData.GetEffectivePriceUsd` at that timestamp for the day's price.
- New UI section in `CompanyDetails.cs`'s `RebuildInfo` (placed after Vote History, in `_infoVBox` — the SAME scroll `_actionVBox`'s action panels already share, no new `ScrollContainer` needed, Ch. 29 compliant by construction): the three lifetime-total labels (`Total SC received`, `Total BTC received`, the 2a historical / 2b current SC-value pair) + the claim log, most-recent-first, capped to the 30 most recent rows shown (`"Most recent 30 of N claims"` when N > 30 — the full history up to 500 stays in state regardless of how many are displayed). Rebuilt every 1 s refresh (NOT signature-gated like the action panels) so a claim shows up immediately without needing a holding/vote-state change to trigger a rebuild. 2b's live price reuses the `_networkRoot.GetPlayerLatestBlock().Timestamp` pattern already established in the sibling `AuctioningCompanyDetails` scene, via a new `_btcMarketDataService` autoload reference (the `BTCWallet.cs` precedent for fetching `BtcMarketDataService` directly rather than through `NetworkRoot`).

**Status: ✅ BUILT, VERIFIED & COMMITTED 2026-07-22 — `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors; developer in-game verification PASSED; committed (latest commit on the branch).**

## 13. ND.9 — Company Data Presentation Polish (✅ BUILT 2026-07-22 — first batch ND.9a–h implemented, `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors; developer in-game verification + commit pending)

**Why this phase exists.** ND.8b–ND.8g landed the full company system — founding, stock distribution, dividends & votes, the `CompanyDetails` / `AuctioningCompanyDetails` scenes, the SC Monetary Ledger, bots-as-clients, and the player dividend-claim history — all now BUILT & VERIFIED. With the *mechanics* working and verified in-game, the developer, now playing as a real player, is finding rough edges in **how the company data is presented**: what's shown, where, how it's labelled, what's missing at a glance, and which numbers a player actually needs surfaced. This phase collects those presentation/UX refinements. It is deliberately **polish-only** — no new economic mechanics, no changes to the auction/dividend/vote *math* already locked in §12; only how existing data is displayed, organized, and navigated.

**Scope guardrails (what belongs here vs. not):**
- ✅ In scope: labels, panels, layout, ordering, groupings, missing-but-already-computed figures, navigation between company scenes, at-a-glance summaries, clarity of the `CompanyDetails` / `AuctioningCompanyDetails` / `CompanyDetails`-adjacent readouts, BlockExplorer Enroll-Mode company rows, `WorldEconomy` readouts.
- ❌ Out of scope (defer to a later phase / re-open §12 if truly needed): changing dividend rates, vote resolution math, auction ladder probabilities, stock distribution formulas, SC provisioning economics — any *behavior* change. Presentation of those values is in scope; the values themselves are not.
- **Ch. 29 (`Documentation/ProjectDesignManual.md`) is mandatory reading before touching ANY of these scenes' scroll/footer layout** — these are exactly the scrollable-panel scenes that have historically regressed when Ch. 29 wasn't consulted.

### 13.1 Subphase roster (ND.9a … ND.9h — first batch, described 2026-07-22)

*The developer described a first batch of 7 concrete presentation gaps (as a real player) in one pass. Each is written out as an `ND.9x` subphase in §13.2 below, in the ND.7/ND.8 style. **All §13.3 open decisions were RESOLVED 2026-07-22 (see §13.3) — the phase is ✅ READY FOR IMPLEMENTATION. No code written yet.** Roster is appended to as more needs surface.*

| Subphase | Title | Player item | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| ND.9a | BlockExplorer toggle rename → "Auction / Company Mode" + record the deferred Basic-Mode split | 1 | ✅ BUILT |
| ND.9b | `CompanyDetails` holding-keyed border colour (NST gold / PST silver / none black) | 2 | ✅ BUILT |
| ND.9c | Founding Snapshot — per-participant tier / participation% / bonus / NST-PST distribution at close | 3 | ✅ BUILT — Option B, no helper; legacy foundings show no snapshot |
| ND.9d | Reserves-at-a-glance — BTC & SC reserves side by side, original% vs current% | 4 | ✅ BUILT — in the grouped panel |
| ND.9e | Market (Light↔Dark) level — initial vs current, as a white→black gradient slider | 5 | ✅ BUILT — gradient slider |
| ND.9f | Reserves/Dividends split (payout rate) — initial default vs current voted | 6a | ✅ BUILT — in the grouped panel |
| ND.9g | Last-Vote Snapshot — every participant's ballot + weight + result + company before/after | 6b | ✅ BUILT — persisted on **every** `CompanyVoteRecord` |
| ND.9h | Per-participant dividend distribution on a quarterly snapshot (PST quarter-total → daily breakdown) | 7 | ✅ BUILT |

**Shared implementation notes (apply to every ND.9x that touches a scene):**
- **`Documentation/ProjectDesignManual.md` Ch. 29 is mandatory reading before editing `CompanyDetails.tscn` / `AuctioningCompanyDetails.tscn` scroll/footer layout.** Both scenes already follow the reference chain (`RootMargin → RootVBox(bounded) → ContentScroll(size_flags_vertical=3) → InfoVBox/ActionVBox`, then a fixed `BackBtn` footer OUTSIDE the scroll). New readouts go inside `_infoVBox` (rebuilt each 1 s refresh in `RebuildInfo`) — no new `ScrollContainer`, Ch. 29-compliant by construction (the ND.8g precedent, §12.5.6).
- **Presentation-only:** ND.9d/e/f surface values the engine already computes (`gov.ReserveScPercent`, `gov.MarketCategory`, `gov.QuarterPayoutRatePercent`, `gov.ScReserve`, `CompanyTreasuryBtc`). No dividend/vote/auction *math* changes here (that stays locked in §12). ND.9c and ND.9g are the only two that may need a **new persisted field** — both flagged as open decisions.
- **Number/locale discipline:** every compound string uses `string.Create(CultureInfo.InvariantCulture, $"…")` (already the file's convention); BTC 8-dp, SC via the existing `:N8`/`:F8` patterns in `CompanyDetails.RebuildInfo`.
- **Data map (verified 2026-07-22 against `NetworkRoot.cs`):** `CompanyFounding{ Holdings[] = CompanyShareHolding{ HolderId, Nst, Pst } }` stores only FINAL token counts — **not** tier/participation/bonus. `NonMinerDonationSummary.TrackedDonations` (the value-ranked top-10 pool) persists and is FROZEN after resolution — the founding math (`FoundCompany`, `NetworkRoot.cs:1339`) is deterministically reconstructible from it. `CompanyGovernanceState` holds `ReserveScPercent`, `ScReserve`, `DefaultMarketCategory`/`MarketCategory` (axis `["official","light_grey","dark_grey","black"]`), `QuarterPayoutRatePercent`, `QuarterDividendBtc/Sc`, `QuarterCycleStartMs/EndMs`, `VoteHistory[] = CompanyVoteRecord{ Kind, Opened/ClosedAtMs, ResultReserveScPercent, ResultMarketCategory, ResultPayoutRatePercent, FinalizedDividendBtc/Sc }`. **`CompanyVote.Ballots` (per-participant votes) is DISCARDED at `CloseCompanyVote` (`gov.OpenVote = null`, NetworkRoot.cs:1828)** — this is the gap ND.9g must close.

### 13.2 Subphase specifications (⏳ ALL SPEC DRAFT — awaiting developer confirmation)

#### ND.9a — Rename the BlockExplorer toggle + record the deferred Basic-Mode split

- **Player intent (item 1):** the "Enroll Mode" toggle now surfaces BOTH still-recruitable auctions AND founded companies, so its name is stale. Rename it to **"Auction / Company Mode"** — fine for DEV now. Separately, record the **non-priority Basic-Mode objective**: in Basic Mode the toggle reverts to **Auction Mode** only, and founded companies get their **own dedicated list in a separate scene** (not folded into the explorer's **Auction Mode** view).
- **Build checklist:**
  - `Screens/BlockExplorer/BlockExplorer.cs:96` — `new CheckBox { Text = "Enroll Mode" }` → `"Auction / Company Mode"`.
  - `BlockExplorer.cs:136` — the header string `"Enroll Mode — referral auction | …"` → `"Auction / Company Mode — referral auction & founded companies | …"` (keep the counts).
  - Doc-note the deferred objective (do these as part of ND.9a, not now): `Documentation/PRIVATE_ROADMAP.md` (add a Basic-Mode line: "companies get their own scene/list; the explorer toggle reverts to Auction Mode only — non-priority") and `Documentation/ProjectDesignManual.md` §22.3 ("Enroll Mode") — note the rename + the future split. Grep for user-facing "Enroll Mode" strings elsewhere before finishing (GLOSSARY/PLAYER_GUIDE reference it — decide per-file whether to rename or leave the historical text).
- **Open decision:** none. Low-risk, no data model. *(Note: internal identifiers `_enrollModeToggle` / `BuildEnrollModePanel` / `RefreshEnrollMode` / `SceneId` can stay as-is — code-facing names, not player-facing; renaming them is optional churn.)*

#### ND.9b — `CompanyDetails` border colour keyed on the player's holding

- **Player intent (item 2):** paint the `CompanyDetails` page border **gold** when the player holds **NST**, **silver** when they hold **PST**, **black** when they hold **neither**. An at-a-glance "what's my stake here" signal.
- **Data:** the holding class is already computed in `RebuildOrUpdateActions` (`hasNst` / `hasPst`, CompanyDetails.cs:266-267). Precedence: NST (gold) wins over PST (silver) if somehow both (can't happen per holder today, but define gold > silver > black anyway).
- **Build checklist (Ch. 29-safe — a border overlay, not a layout change):**
  - Add a `PanelContainer` (or a `Panel` with a `StyleBoxFlat`: transparent `bg_color`, `border_width_* = 4`, `corner_radius` optional) wrapping `RootVBox` inside `RootMargin`, OR override `_Draw()` on the root `Control` to stroke a rectangle just inside `RootMargin`. **Recommend the StyleBoxFlat overlay** (declarative, no per-frame `_Draw`).
  - In `RefreshAll` (after holding is known), set `border_color` = gold `Color(0.85,0.65,0.13)` / silver `Color(0.75,0.75,0.78)` / black `Color(0.05,0.05,0.05)`. Colours final only after developer eyeballs them in-game.
  - Optional: a tiny caption under the identity label ("You hold: NST (voting) / PST / none") so the colour is legible for colour-blind readers.
- **✅ DECISION LOCKED (2026-07-22):** `CompanyDetails` **only** (not `AuctioningCompanyDetails` — pre-founding the player holds no stock). Colours per the recommendation (gold/silver/black), final only after the developer eyeballs them in-game.

#### ND.9c — Founding Snapshot: how the company was born, participant by participant

- **Player intent (item 3):** "understand clearly how the company was founded from the beginning" — a snapshot of the **NST/PST distribution per the tier table at auction close**, with each participant's **participation %, slot-bonus %, and resulting tokens/class** spelled out. The current "Stock Distribution (founding mint)" section (CompanyDetails.cs:117-138) only shows final tokens + profit share + vote %; it hides tier occupancy, participation %, base-vs-bonus split.
- **The founding math to surface (from `FoundCompany`, NetworkRoot.cs:1339-1412):** per unique donor — the tier(s) they occupy in the value-ranked pool (`tiers`, 1 = largest bid), `participationShare = liveBtc / poolTotal`, `baseTokens = participationShare × 10,000`, `bonusFraction = Σ SlotBonusPercent(tier) / 100` (ladder `5.2 / 2.6 / 1.3 / 0.65 / … %` halving to tier 10, NetworkRoot.cs:1416-1419), `finalTokens = baseTokens × (1 + bonusFraction)`, class = **NST if any tier ≤ 3 else PST**.
- **✅ DECISION LOCKED (2026-07-22): Option B, NO shared helper. Legacy already-founded companies simply show no founding snapshot.**
  - Persist the founding breakdown at `FoundCompany` time. Concretely: add a parallel `List<CompanyFoundingBreakdown>` to `CompanyFounding` (keeps `CompanyShareHolding` — the live token-count record — untouched), one entry per unique donor: `{ HolderId, Tiers (List<int>), AmountBtcAtClose, ParticipationShare, BaseTokens, BonusFraction, FinalTokens, IsNst }`. Populate it INSIDE the existing per-donor loop in `FoundCompany` (NetworkRoot.cs:1371-1395) — every value is already computed there, so this is pure "also store what you just computed", no second copy of the ranking/participation math and no separate helper (the developer explicitly rejected the shared-helper path — the display just reads the frozen list).
  - Rides the existing `BlockchainStateSnapshot`/`state.json` for free (the ND.8g inheritance argument — no new file/checkpoint/delete-list work).
  - **Legacy fallback (explicitly accepted):** companies founded before ND.9c ships have an empty/absent breakdown list ⇒ the founding-snapshot section renders a one-line `"Founding breakdown unavailable (company founded before this feature)."` and falls back to today's plain token list. NO reconstruction-at-display path is built.
- **Build checklist:** enrich the "Stock Distribution (founding mint)" section into a per-participant table read from `founding.FoundingBreakdown` (when present): `holder — tier(s) [e.g. #1,#4] — bid X.XXXXXXXX BTC — participation NN.NN% — base T tokens ×(1+bonus B.BB%) = final tokens — CLASS (NST votes / PST)`, plus the pool total BTC and total minted NST/PST (already shown). Order descending by final tokens (as today). When the list is empty (legacy), show the fallback line + the existing plain list.

#### ND.9d — Reserves-at-a-glance: BTC & SC side by side, original vs current %

- **Player intent (item 4):** understand the **current BTC/SC reserve proportion**, both **original% and current%**, with **both reserves shown side by side in ONE panel** (today they're split across two lines — "Reserve mix target: X% SC / Y% BTC" and "SC reserve: N SC" — CompanyDetails.cs:150-159).
- **Data (all already persisted):** current mix = `gov.ReserveScPercent` (SC%) / `100 − gov.ReserveScPercent` (BTC%). Actual holdings = `gov.ScReserve` (SC) and `CompanyTreasuryBtc(nodeId)` / `GetNodeSpendableBalance` (BTC). **Original% = the founding-day vote's result** = `gov.VoteHistory.FirstOrDefault(v => v.Kind == "founding")?.ResultReserveScPercent` (falls back to the first VoteHistory entry). Optionally show the BTC reserve's live SC-equivalent via `BtcMarketDataService` (the ND.8g "current value" precedent) so both sides are comparable in SC.
- **Build checklist:** replace the two scattered lines with one "Reserves" sub-section: a two-column-feel readout —
  - `BTC reserve: {treasuryBtc:F8} BTC ({100−current:F0}% target; ~{treasuryBtc×price:F2} SC)` and `SC reserve: {gov.ScReserve:N8} SC ({current:F0}% target)` on adjacent lines (or an `HBoxContainer` with two labelled columns).
  - A "mix" line: `Reserve mix — original {orig:F0}% SC / {100−orig:F0}% BTC → current {current:F0}% SC / {100−current:F0}% BTC ({gov.CurrencyBand}, band {FormatBounds})`.
- **✅ DECISION LOCKED (2026-07-22):** show the BTC side's live SC-equivalent (yes — makes the proportion legible). This subphase is one of the three grouped rows in the combined "Company Policy (initial → current)" panel (see the layout note below).

#### ND.9e — Market (Light↔Dark) level: a white→black gradient slider

- **Player intent (item 5):** understand the company's **initial Light/Dark level** and the **current** one as votes move it — **rendered as a visual slider/gradient**, not just text.
- **✅ DECISION LOCKED (2026-07-22):**
  - **Labels confirmed:** the four `MarketCategoryOrder = ["official","light_grey","dark_grey","black"]` ids (light→dark, NetworkRoot.cs:1528) map to player-facing **"Official / Light-grey / Dark-grey / Black market"**.
  - **A gradient slider showing the WHOLE scale at once:** a horizontal bar with a **white → grey → black gradient**, the four categories placed as labelled ticks at their correct positions along it, each tick showing its name **and** its light/dark percentage. **Only the CURRENT configuration is marked on the gradient** (a caret/marker at the current category's position). **The DEFAULT (initial) configuration is shown in numbers only** (a plain text line beside/under the slider — no second marker on the bar).
  - **Light/Dark % mapping** (4 categories on axis indices 0–3): **darkness% = index / 3 × 100** ⇒ Official `0% dark` (fully light/legal), Light-grey `33% dark`, Dark-grey `67% dark`, Black market `100% dark`. Each tick label reads e.g. `Dark-grey — 67% dark`. (Confirm the % direction/rounding when it's on screen; "% dark" chosen since higher = riskier/darker.)
- **Build checklist (Ch. 29-safe — a control inside `_infoVBox`):**
  - A small custom control for the gradient bar: either a `TextureRect` with a `GradientTexture1D` (stops white → mid-grey → black) at a fixed `custom_minimum_size` (e.g. `~360×24`), or a lightweight `Control` with `_Draw` painting the gradient rectangle. Overlay the four tick labels (name + % dark) at x-positions `index/3 × width`, and a current-position marker (a small triangle/caret or a bright outline box) at the CURRENT category's x.
  - A text line under it: `Current: Dark-grey (67% dark)   |   Default: Official (0% dark)` — the default in numbers only, per the decision.
  - Map ids→labels+% once in a **shared static helper** on `CompanyDetails` (or a tiny `MarketLevel` helper), **reused by ND.9g's snapshot** for consistent naming (this is the one place a shared helper IS wanted — it's display-label mapping, not the founding math).
  - Note the reachable range (default ±1, D-ND8.19b) as a subtle caption if it reads cleanly; optional.

#### ND.9f — Reserves/Dividends split (payout rate): initial default vs current

- **Player intent (item 6a):** understand the **Reserves/Dividends proportion** — what share of the company's value is paid out as dividends vs. retained as reserve — again **original/initial vs current**.
- **Data:** the quarterly **payout rate** IS this dial: `gov.QuarterPayoutRatePercent` = % of each reserve side paid out per quarter (dividends); the complement is retained. **Initial/default = `NetworkRoot.DefaultQuarterlyPayoutRatePercent(gov.DefaultMarketCategory)`** (category defaults 5/8/13/21%/quarter per CLAUDE.md; the ballot clamps to `[0, 2×default]`). **Current = `gov.QuarterPayoutRatePercent`** (0 until the first quarterly vote finalizes — show "not yet set (first quarter pending)" in that case).
- **Build checklist:** a "Dividend policy" line: `Payout rate: current {current:F1}%/quarter → dividends; {100−current... }` — careful: the retained-vs-paid framing is per-quarter of the reserve, not a 0–100 split of the whole; present it as `Dividends: {current:F1}% of reserves per quarter (default {default:F0}%, max {2×default:F0}%); the rest stays in reserve`. Also surface the last finalized quarter amounts (`gov.QuarterDividendBtc/Sc`, already shown at CompanyDetails.cs:180-188) beside it.
- **✅ DECISION LOCKED (2026-07-22):** use the **rate framing** above ("% of reserves paid out per quarter as dividends; the rest stays in reserve"), initial default → current voted. One of the three grouped rows in the "Company Policy" panel.

> **✅ LAYOUT LOCKED (2026-07-22) for ND.9d + ND.9e + ND.9f:** these three render as a **single "Company Policy (initial → current)" sub-section** in `_infoVBox` — three grouped rows: **Reserves** (ND.9d, BTC & SC side by side + original→current mix), **Market level** (ND.9e, the white→black gradient slider), **Dividend rate** (ND.9f, initial default → current). Replaces today's scattered governance lines (CompanyDetails.cs:150-188).

#### ND.9g — Last-Vote Snapshot: every participant's ballot + the before/after

- **Player intent (item 6b):** a snapshot of **only the last vote**, showing **every participant's cast ballot** and **the result**, with the company's changed values **before and after** the vote made explicit (light/dark level, SC-reserve %, dividend %, …).
- **✅ DECISION LOCKED (2026-07-22): persist the detail on EVERY `CompanyVoteRecord`** (not a single `LastVoteDetail`) — the "last-vote snapshot" is just `VoteHistory.Last()`, and every historical row can expand to the same detail later. The per-participant ballots (`CompanyVote.Ballots`) and pre-vote dials are **discarded** at close today (`CloseCompanyVote` sets `gov.OpenVote = null` after reading them, NetworkRoot.cs:1825-1928); we capture them at close time. New persisted shape (rides `BlockchainStateSnapshot`, ND.8g precedent — no new file/checkpoint/delete-list work):
  - Extend `CompanyVoteRecord` with: `BeforeReserveScPercent`, `BeforeMarketCategory`, `BeforePayoutRatePercent` (snapshot the three dials right before the result is applied), and `List<VoteBallotRecord>{ HolderId, Nst, WeightPercent, ReserveScPercentTarget, MarketShift, PayoutRatePercent }` (one row per cast ballot; weight = `nst/totalNst`, already computed in `CloseCompanyVote`).
  - Cap: the ballot list is ≤ the number of NST holders (tiny). No extra cap needed beyond the existing `MaxVoteHistoryPerCompany`. Legacy records (closed before ND.9g) have empty before-values/ballots ⇒ the snapshot section shows only what it has (the existing result fields) — same graceful-degradation stance as ND.9c.
- **Build checklist (once shape confirmed):**
  - `CloseCompanyVote` — before `gov.ReserveScPercent = reserveResult` etc., snapshot the three "before" values; inside the ballot loop, record each `(holderId, nst, weight, ballot.*)`; attach both to the new `CompanyVoteRecord` fields.
  - New "Last Vote Snapshot" sub-section in `_infoVBox` (above or replacing the terse Vote History list): `{Kind} vote closed {date}` → a before→after block for each changed dial (reserve% , market level via ND.9e's shared label, payout%), then a per-participant ballot table `holder — weight NN% — voted: reserve X%, market ±/hold, payout Y%`. Mark ballots that matched vs. diverged from the result if useful.
- **Bots' ballots** are cast at vote open (`GhostVoteFor`-style, NetworkRoot.cs:~1815) — they WILL appear in `vote.Ballots`, so "every participant" is genuinely all NST holders that voted, bots included.

#### ND.9h — Per-participant dividend distribution on a quarterly snapshot (PST daily breakdown)

- **Player intent (item 7):** when the last-vote snapshot (ND.9g) is a **quarterly (vote/pay) one**, publish **how many dividends each participant receives** this quarter; for **PST members**, show the **quarter total → daily-amount** division so it's clear *why* they receive a given amount per day.
- **Data (all persisted — this is mostly a display computation):** finalized quarter dividend `gov.QuarterDividendBtc` / `gov.QuarterDividendSc`; per holder `share = (h.Nst + h.Pst) / totalTokens` (the profit-participation share, NetworkRoot.cs:1962/1998); `daysInQuarter = max(1, (QuarterCycleEndMs − QuarterCycleStartMs) / GameDayMs)` (NetworkRoot.cs:1941). Per holder: quarter total = `share × dividend`; **NST** = paid as a lump at quarter end; **PST** = `quarterTotal / daysInQuarter` per in-game day (the drip, NetworkRoot.cs:1964).
- **Build checklist:** extend ND.9g's snapshot (only when `Kind == "quarterly"`) with a "This quarter's dividends" table: `holder — CLASS — profit share NN.NN% — quarter total {btc:F8} BTC + {sc:F8} SC` and, for PST rows, an extra `→ daily {btc/days:F8} BTC + {sc/days:F8} SC × {daysInQuarter} days`. Include a header line: `Quarter total pool: {QuarterDividendBtc:F8} BTC + {QuarterDividendSc:F8} SC over {daysInQuarter} days`.
- **✅ DECISION LOCKED (2026-07-22):** show it for **ALL participants** (the "why does each get what" ask), rendered **inside the ND.9g snapshot section** when that snapshot's `Kind == "quarterly"` (it's "publish on that snapshot"), not a separate section.

### 13.3 Cross-cutting decisions — ✅ ALL RESOLVED 2026-07-22

1. **ND.9c founding snapshot:** ✅ **Option B, NO shared helper.** Persist a `List<CompanyFoundingBreakdown>` on `CompanyFounding`, populated inside `FoundCompany`'s existing per-donor loop. **Legacy already-founded companies show no founding snapshot** (a fallback line, no reconstruction path).
2. **ND.9g last-vote detail:** ✅ **Approved — store on EVERY `CompanyVoteRecord`** (before-values + per-ballot `List<VoteBallotRecord>`). Legacy records degrade gracefully (result fields only).
3. **ND.9d/e/f grouping:** ✅ **One combined "Company Policy (initial → current)" panel** (three grouped rows: Reserves / Market level / Dividend rate).
4. **ND.9b border colours:** ✅ Gold (NST) / silver (PST) / black (none), **`CompanyDetails` only.** Exact colours finalized on-screen.
5. **ND.9e:** ✅ Labels **"Official / Light-grey / Dark-grey / Black market"** confirmed; rendered as a **white→black gradient slider** — the whole scale with labelled ticks + % dark, a marker for the CURRENT config only, the default config shown in numbers only. Darkness% = index/3 (0 / 33 / 67 / 100).
6. **Build order:** ✅ Confirmed — **ND.9a → ND.9b → ND.9d/e/f (grouped) → ND.9c → ND.9g → ND.9h.** ND.9c/g touch engine-side capture (persist new founding-breakdown / vote-detail); ND.9a/b/d/e/f/h are display-only.

*Status: entire §13 is ✅ **BUILT**, 2026-07-22 — implemented in the §13.3(6) build order (ND.9a → b → d/e/f → c → g → h), `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors. Developer in-game verification + commit pending.*

### 13.4 Implementation notes (as built, 2026-07-22)

- **ND.9a** — `BlockExplorer.cs`: toggle text + header string → "Auction / Company Mode"; deferred Basic-Mode split recorded in `ProjectDesignManual.md` §22.3 and `PRIVATE_ROADMAP.md`. Internal `enrollMode` ids kept (code-facing).
- **ND.9b** — `CompanyDetails.cs`: a mouse-transparent `Panel` + `StyleBoxFlat` border overlay (index 0, inset 8 px, Ch. 29-safe), colour set per refresh from the player's holding (gold `#D9A621` NST / silver `#BFBFC7` PST / black `#0D0D0D` none); a "You hold: …" caption appended to the status line.
- **ND.9d/e/f** — `CompanyDetails.BuildCompanyPolicySection`: one "Company Policy (initial → current)" section replacing the old scattered governance lines. Reserves (BTC+SC side by side, live SC-equiv of BTC, initial→current mix — initial read from the founding vote's `ResultReserveScPercent`), Market level (`BuildMarketGradientBar`: fixed-width 420 px white→grey→black `GradientTexture1D`, 4 labelled ticks with % dark, a "▲ current" caret; default in numbers only), Dividend rate (initial default via `DefaultQuarterlyPayoutRatePercent(DefaultMarketCategory)` → current).
- **ND.9c** — `CompanyFounding.FoundingBreakdown` (`List<CompanyFoundingBreakdown>`), populated in `FoundCompany`'s existing per-donor loop (no helper); `CompanyDetails` "Founding Snapshot — Stock Distribution" table reads it (tier(s), bid BTC, participation%, base×(1+bonus)=final, class), legacy fallback line when empty.
- **ND.9g** — `CompanyVoteRecord` gained `BeforeReserveScPercent/BeforeMarketCategory/BeforePayoutRatePercent`, `QuarterDaysInCycle`, `List<VoteBallotRecord> Ballots`; captured in `CloseCompanyVote` (before-values before mutation, ballots + weight inside the tally loop). `CompanyDetails.BuildLastVoteSnapshot` renders before→after dials + every ballot; legacy records degrade to result-only.
- **ND.9h** — inside `BuildLastVoteSnapshot`, on a quarterly record with a finalized dividend: per-holder quarter total (`share × finalized`) for all participants, PST rows split to a daily amount using `QuarterDaysInCycle`.
- **No new persisted files / checkpoint / delete-list / `WorldFormatVersion` work** — `FoundingBreakdown` and the new `CompanyVoteRecord` fields ride `NetworkRoot`'s existing `BlockchainStateSnapshot` (the ND.8g inheritance). Presentation-only otherwise; no auction/dividend/vote *math* changed.
- **Post-verify tweaks (2026-07-22, developer feedback round 1):**
  - **ND.9e slider layout fix** — the tick labels (name + % dark) overlapped the gradient. Rewrote `BuildMarketGradientBar` into three non-overlapping bands: tick labels ABOVE (y 0–32), gradient in the middle (y 34–50), the "▲ current" caret below (y 52). Widened to 440 px, tick width 96 px. The current caret was already fine (kept).
  - **ND.9b extended to BlockExplorer** — the holding-keyed border (gold NST / silver PST / black none) now also frames each **founded company's title** in Auction / Company Mode: the title `Label` is wrapped in a bordered `PanelContainer` (`StyleBoxFlat`, 2 px border + content margins), colour from the player's holding via `GetCompanyFounding(...).Holdings["player"]`. Coexists with the existing vote-pending red text (border = your stake, red = your ballot blocks play).
- **Verify in-game:** open a founded company's `CompanyDetails` (BlockExplorer → Auction / Company Mode → Founded row → Details →) and check: border colour matches holding; Founding Snapshot table (a company founded pre-ND.9c shows the legacy notice); the gradient slider marks the current market level; a company that has closed at least one quarterly vote shows the Last Vote Snapshot with ballots + the per-participant/daily dividend split.

## 14. ND.10 — Post-ND.9 UI & auction-behavior fixes (⏳ OPEN, 2026-07-22 — objectives specified incrementally; first subphase is a technical auction-behavior fix diagnosed during ND.9 playtesting)

**Why this phase exists.** ND.9 shipped and was committed successfully. Continuing to play, the developer surfaces further needs — a mix of **UI-detail refinements** (like ND.9) AND **technical/behavioral fixes** where the underlying simulation is wrong, not just its presentation. ND.10 collects both. Unlike ND.9 (presentation-only), ND.10 explicitly ALLOWS engine/behavior changes when the diagnosis calls for one. The first such fix — the stuck-bidder escalation not engaging — was diagnosed from live trace during ND.9 playtesting (§13's BitInstant audit), so it opens the phase.

### 14.1 Subphase roster (appended incrementally)

| Subphase | Title | Kind | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| ND.10a | Stuck-bidder escalation actually engages — Fix A (escalation-boosted re-selection) + Fix B (per-block signature sweep) | technical / auction behavior | ✅ BUILT & VERIFIED 2026-07-22 |

### 14.2 ND.10a — the stuck-bidder escalation must actually engage (Fix A + Fix B)

**The bug (audited from live trace, 2026-07-22 — the BitInstant / bot_4 case, §13 audit).** BitInstant (`non_miner_8`) received, in order, bids that ranked bot_4's single 3.717 BTC donation (blk 1201) at **tier 2** (top-3) through blk 1224. Then bot_3 (8.62, blk 1225), bot_2 (18.47, blk 1230) and bot_1 (22.10, blk 1231) out-bid it, pushing bot_4's slot to **tier ~5 (below top-3)** — exactly the stuck-single-bidder escalation's target case. **But the escalation never engaged**, and its `AuctioningCompanyDetails` label sat frozen at the flat base (8%).

**Root cause.** The stuck-escalation is only ever updated/consulted when the bot's bid pipeline (`TryBuildCasinoBotBid`) SELECTS that pool — and selection orders by ascending `ownSlotCount` (**spread-wide-first**), returning after the first affordable target. bot_4 always had fresh 0-slot pools to seed (`non_miner_7/9/10`…), so it **never re-selected BitInstant** after dropping below top-3. Consequently: (1) `ComputeStuckEscalationProbabilityPercent(non_miner_8, bot_4)` was never re-called → `_stuckBidderSignatures` stayed at the stale `single:2` (from the tier-2 era) → the roll never escalated; (2) the label's `PeekStuckEscalationProbabilityPercent` saw the `single:2` signature ≠ `single:5` → returned base → frozen. The round-3 fix corrected the escalation MATH but not that the escalation depends on the pool being re-selected — and selection deprioritizes already-held single-slot pools. This is the same structural pattern as the original DeepBit stagnation, one layer deeper.

**The fix (developer-approved 2026-07-22: A + B).**

- **Fix B — a per-block signature sweep (`SweepStuckBidderSignatures`).** Once per block, for EVERY (recruitable pool × casino bot) pair, recompute the bot's slot count/best tier in that pool and update `_stuckBidderSignatures` edge-triggered — **independent of whether the bot is selected to bid**. This keeps the signature accurate as OTHER bots' bids re-rank a pool (the tier 2→5 transition is stamped `single:5` at the block it happens, so `blocksElapsed` then grows correctly), so both the roll and the label reflect the true stuck duration. Also REMOVES the signature when a bot is fully evicted from a pool (a future re-entry starts fresh — closes the round-3 "accepted residual imprecision"). Runs in `TryCasinoBotDonation` right after `recruitable` is computed, BEFORE the `count == 0` early-return (so it advances every block, even donation-less ones). The roll path's own `ComputeStuckEscalationProbabilityPercent` stays (harmless no-op once the sweep has stamped this block; still returns the value for the `max(mode, escalation)` roll).
- **Fix A — escalation-boosted re-selection.** In `TryBuildCasinoBotBid`, before the affordability walk, roll each qualifying single-slot-below-top-3 pool's current escalation (`PeekStuckEscalationProbabilityPercent`, kept live by Fix B); the FIRST pool that HITS its roll is moved to the front of the walk and, if affordable, contested outright this slot — **skipping the ladder re-roll** (the escalation roll already decided to bid). If none hits, the normal spread-wide order is unchanged. This turns the escalation from a roll-only modifier into an escalating CHANCE to actually re-select and re-contest the stuck pool, growing each block until the bot re-bids (becoming multi-slot → signature resets → escalation clears) or the auction closes. An unaffordable escalation pick simply falls through to the normal walk.

**Why A+B together (not either alone):** B alone makes the label climb toward 100% but bot_4 still never re-bids (selection still deprioritizes it) → a ~100% label that does nothing (misleading). A alone can't roll a correct escalation because the signature is stale/never-updated for a non-selected pool. Together: B keeps the escalation accurate for every pool; A lets that accurate escalation pull the bot back to re-contest.

**Files:** `NetworkRoot.cs` only — `SweepStuckBidderSignatures` (new), `TryCasinoBotDonation` (call the sweep), `TryBuildCasinoBotBid` (escalation pick + reorder + skip-roll-on-commit). No new persisted state (`_stuckBidderSignatures` stays in-memory, D-ND8d.round3). Trace: the escalation-committed bid records its hit % in `casino_bot_bid_trace.csv`'s `rolledProbabilityPercent`.

**Verify in-game:** open BitInstant (or any pool where a bot holds a lone below-top-3 slot) in `AuctioningCompanyDetails` — the bot's per-slot "re-bid NN%" now climbs block by block; and within a few blocks the stuck bot actually re-bids (its slot value jumps, it becomes multi-slot, the label resets). Trace: `rolledProbabilityPercent` on that bot's `non_miner_8` rows climbs, and a `donated` row appears where before there were only `roll-declined`s.

**Status: ✅ BUILT & VERIFIED 2026-07-22 — `dotnet build` 0 warnings / 0 errors; developer in-game verification PASSED (stuck bots now re-bid, labels climb).** (`NetworkRoot.SweepStuckBidderSignatures` new + called in `TryCasinoBotDonation` before the count==0 return; `TryBuildCasinoBotBid` gains the escalation-pick reorder + skip-roll-on-commit. The roll-path `ComputeStuckEscalationProbabilityPercent` is kept — redundant with the sweep but still the `max()` source.)

### 14.3 ND.10b — Per-bot "real leading-bid roll %" panel (⏳ SPEC DRAFT — awaiting Q&A)

**Player intent (2026-07-22).** In each in-auction pool (`AuctioningCompanyDetails`), add a panel **to the RIGHT of the bids list** showing the **4 casino bots** (`bot_1..4`), each with a label = **the real probability that this bot places THE leading bid in THIS pool** (per pipeline run). The number must account for the fact that a bot's single per-slot action is **distributed across ALL in-auction pools by priority order** — "dividing the bot's total first-bid/re-bid probabilities across the in-auction pools per the priority order, shown as faithfully as possible." If only ONE pool is in auction, it degenerates to that pool's own in-pool rule (sum-of-two-lowest / escalation / deterministic first-bid).

**The probability model (derived from the real pipeline, `TryCasinoBotDonation` → `TryBuildCasinoBotBid`).** A bot's single pipeline run picks AT MOST ONE pool to bid, so its per-pool bid probabilities sum to ≤ 1 — the panel shows how that total is split. For bot X, conditional on X running its pipeline this block (i.e. NOT multiplied by the count draw / fair-selection layer — this is what makes the single-pool case reduce to the raw in-pool rule):

1. **Qualifying set** = every in-auction pool EXCEPT where X is satisfied (tier 1, or tier 3 at ≥2 bids), the current leader, or self-eviction-guarded; further filtered by the **half-spendable affordability cap** (`required + fee ≤ spendable × 0.5`).
2. **Escalation pre-roll** (Fix A) over the qualifying single-slot-below-top-3 pools **in priorityTargets order** (soonest-to-expire, then awaiting-first-bid): `E1..Em` with escalations `e1..em`. First hit commits.
3. **Ladder fallback** (no escalation commit): the spread-wide first-affordable pool `P*` (ascending own-slot count, ties priorityTargets order) rolls its ladder `r*` (`SumTwoLowestReBidProbabilities`, or `max(mode, escalation)` if single-slot-below-top-3, or **deterministic 1.0** if unparticipated).
4. **Marginal P(X bids pool P)** = `[if P = Ek: (∏_{j<k}(1−ej)) · ek]` + `[(∏_all(1−ej)) · 1{P*=P} · r*]`. Satisfied/leader/guarded pools → 0.

This reduces correctly for one pool: multi-slot → `sum-of-two-lowest`; unparticipated → `100%`; single-slot-below-top-3 → `e + (1−e)·max(mode,e)` (see Q2 — Fix A gives it TWO chances, so the true value slightly exceeds the raw escalation).
